# New pages 2026-09 — Planning out, keyboard shortcuts out, Cameras and Teleprompter in

Status: **in progress — the workstation catch-up** (2026-09-28, on the studio workstation, which carries the rest of the program since the operator's handoff of 2026-09-27, Part C's second **Rescope:**; the order of what is left and Slice 9's first steps answered by the operator on 2026-09-28, "Go with your recommendations on all of it"; Slice 8 verified, landed by #229 as `4c2d4bb`, built in the cloud and merged from the workstation; Slice 6b landed by #228 as `d86de87`, operator verification pending (the catch-up); Slice 6a landed by #227 as `d2cb91f`, operator verification pending (the catch-up); Slice 6 split into 6a and 6b at its first steps, answered by the operator the same day; the next session's entry point is [`new-pages-2026-09-next-session.md`](./new-pages-2026-09-next-session.md); Slice 5a landed by #226 as `5261565`, the first cloud slice, operator verification pending (the catch-up); Part C re-cut for the cloud and S5a's first steps answered by the operator on 2026-09-27, the re-cut recorded as Part C's `**Rescope:**`; Slice 4 verified, landed by #225 as `e6244e6`; on 2026-09-27 the operator re-cut Part C for the cloud, amending D21 and D23; C0 verified, landed by #224 as `9af8028`; Slice SW landed by #221 as `f28909e`; Slice 1 verified, landed by #211 as `e8d43c5`; Slice 2 landed by #213 as `23a2f9a`, operator verification pending; Slice SF verified, landed by #214 as `7fb6f4d`; Slice 2b landed by #215 as `f3b588c`, operator verification pending; Slice 3 landed by #216 as `da18125`, operator verification pending (Appendix B item 2); C0's decisions taken and Part C's slices approved on 2026-09-26). The operator approved this plan on 2026-09-24 ("start S1"), took decisions D1–D8 the same day ("Go with your recommendation on all eight") and approved the interview's outcomes, D9–D16; after the research of C0 the operator decided D17 (NDI from vMix, vMix only) and amended D10, D11 and D14 (the BGH1s do not record; `REC` always means the main camera); on 2026-09-26, in a cloud session, the operator decided D18–D21 with the ten decisions the boards raised, taking every recommendation, and amended D11 (Update the prompter is armed) and D12 (the page's own copy of the glass). Base: `main` at `4c2d4bb` (the program started from `359ee4a`). The live app is `main`'s build of `98bbb06` (since 2026-09-23 14:15) on the real data at schema 7, and it stays so until the close-out; the studio walk happens on the build this program ends with.

Tracking: one pull request per slice; its branch is pushed only after the previous slice merged, rebased onto `main` when it was started earlier (below); ten required checks, "up to date" on, squash merges. Each slice lands as one commit `Pages S<N>: <what landed>` with a What / Why / Tests / Verified body, its refreshed captures as `Pages S<N> (baselines): …` when boards move, and its CI record in the next slice's commits (a run cannot be recorded by the commit that triggers it; since 2026-09-25 no commit is pushed only to record runs). The per-slice `Status:` lines below are the execution record. Status vocabulary: `planned` → `in progress (next: <step>)` → `landed (operator verification pending)` (merged, every lane green, hardware not yet walked) → `verified (<date>, Appendix B item N)`; a slice with no hardware exposure goes straight to `verified` once its lanes are green and its pull request has merged. Every slice records, before it closes: what the gates asserted before the change, the tests it added that fail without it, and every test that had to change (old assertion → new assertion → reason). Every slice gets an adversarial review before its pull request merges. The operator's rulings of 2026-09-25, after the waiting was measured (Slice SF): CI runs once per commit, on the push; the pull request is opened when the slice is pushed, and the operator's go-ahead is asked then, to merge (squash, `--match-head-commit`) once all ten checks are green — never auto-merge unless the operator says so; the rule "push only after the previous CI run has finished" is dropped (a new push cancels the run before it); the next slice may be started locally while CI and the operator's answer are pending, and is pushed after the previous slice has landed (`git rebase --onto origin/main <archive tag> <branch>`); the review starts right after the push.

Goal: the operator's request of 2026-09-24 — (A) remove the Planning workspace completely; (B) remove every keyboard shortcut; (C) add two workspaces on the footing of Lighting and Audio, with no keyboard shortcuts: **Cameras** (see what the three studio cameras see, big, sharp and quick, and set them) and **Teleprompter** (Studio Control draws the main camera's script on the Elgato Prompter XL). All of it before the studio walk, which then happens on this program's last build, with a rewritten checklist.

Sources: the session prompt (`~/.claude/plans/new-pages-session-prompt-2026-09-24.md`, on the workstation, not in the repository); the interview of 2026-09-24, recorded as D9–D16; `docs/redesign/system-a-2026-09.md` (Concept A — §10 is measured on every board); `docs/plans/visual-overhaul-a-2026-09.md` and `docs/plans/production-readiness-2026-09.md`, whose conventions this ledger copies.

Session protocol: start with `git status && git log --oneline -10`; read `AGENTS.md`, this file's `Status:` and `Tracking:` paragraphs and the first slice that is not `verified`; check whether the live app is running (processes, port 38201) and that TotalMix FX and Companion run (never end them). Agents: as many as the work needs and as appropriate (the operator lifted the one-agent-at-a-time rule on 2026-09-25); agents that run Playwright or build `dist` never overlap, and none runs a qualification lane.

**Rescope:** three so far. 2026-09-27, evening: the rest of the program back on the studio workstation — Slices 9 and 7+12, which the re-cut had given to the cloud, included, their boards captured in-slice again (Part C's second **Rescope:**, D23 amended). 2026-09-25: Slice 2 split in two — S2 takes Planning out of the hardware link, and the legacy db.json import it was to retire leaves in a slice of its own, S2b, because seven lanes and scripts seed a test engine through that import (S2's **Rescope:** has the list). 2026-09-27: Part C re-cut for the cloud sessions of D23 — Slice 5 split into 5a (the glass and the Prompter XL's state, in the cloud) and 5b (the Prompter XL's window, on the workstation), and Slices 7 and 12 built as one slice after Slice 9 (Part C's **Rescope:** has the whole). A slice whose premise changes on inspection gets its own `**Rescope:**` paragraph under its heading — old scope → new scope → why — and a title that matches what landed.

## Hardware safety (binding for every slice and lane)

Unchanged from the programs before this one: tests never write to TotalMix ports 7001–7010 or to the real sACN bridge (10.1.0.1); a scratch engine runs with a scratch `SSE_APP_DATA_DIR`, `SSE_AUDIO_SIMULATED_INPUT_MODE=1`, `SSE_CAMERAS_SIMULATED=1` (since Slice 8), `SSE_SAFE_START=1` and a bridge port other than 38201; nothing starts an engine against `%APPDATA%\ExEd Studio Control Native`; the live database is read only from a copy with its `-wal` and `-shm`; TotalMix FX and Companion stay running and untouched; the live app is closed only with the operator's go-ahead, by ending its shell, and reopened from its folder with no `SSE_*` variable, fullscreen on display 3; `scripts/native-package.mjs` runs only with `release\native\windows` moved aside (keep-and-restore); never `--include-release`; never `npm audit fix`; Playwright and a qualification lane never run together; the qualification lanes run in CI only — Slice SF found them unsafe on the workstation even with the live app closed (the Setup/Support lane probes off-host addresses, this PC has a second default route through 10.1.0.1, and a lane step arms the light outputs), so they stay off it until the operator decides otherwise with a safety design of their own.

The six rules of D15, agreed 2026-09-24, in force before the first line of camera or prompter code:

1. **The BGH1s (network).** A camera address exists only once the operator types it into Setup; new saved data holds none, so a scratch or test run contacts nothing. Test builds refuse to connect to any camera address that is not on this PC, the way they drop every TotalMix datagram. Tests talk to a fake BGH1 on 127.0.0.1, on a port chosen at random.
2. **The Pocket 6K Pro (Bluetooth).** Tests, lanes and scratch runs never use Bluetooth; they run the simulated camera. Only the live app uses Bluetooth. Pairing happens once, in Setup, with the operator present (the camera shows the PIN).
3. **Pictures.** Tests never open the DeckLink, the Cam Link or any camera stream; they get a still test picture, and the screenshots mask the live picture as they mask the Console's meters.
4. **The prompter.** Only the live app draws on the Prompter XL. Tests and lanes draw the prompter into an ordinary window, never onto a real screen.
5. **Checks against the real cameras** happen only with the operator present and no camera recording, and put back every setting they touched.
6. **Learning the BGH1's protocol.** If Panasonic has not published it, watch LUMIX Tether talk to one BGH1 while the operator uses it — listen only, never send — with the operator's go-ahead at the time.

The workstation facts these rules rest on (read without changing anything, 2026-09-24): the main camera is a Blackmagic Pocket Cinema Camera 6K Pro (HDMI → DeckLink 8K Pro; set on the body or from the iPad app Bluetooth+ over Bluetooth; about 3 m from the PC, whose Intel radio does Bluetooth); two Panasonic BGH1 on PoE on the office network at 172.16.16.85 and 172.16.16.30, set from LUMIX Tether over the network — one HDMI → SDI → DeckLink, one HDMI → Cam Link 4K; vMix normally, OBS while the vMix licence is expired; the ATEM is not used; Companion 5.0.6 holds only the Stream Deck, X-keys and Studio Control connections; the Prompter XL is a USB-C screen Windows has seen (`Prompter XL`, not connected on 2026-09-24), run today by a home-built program.

## Mechanical drift guards

The existing ones (`scripts/check-slice-rescope.mjs`, `npm run protocol:check`, `tests/contract.rs`, `EVENT_DOMAIN_REFRESH satisfies Record<EventName, …>`, `scripts/file-health.mjs`, the ui-contract ratchets, `scripts/check-operator-copy.mjs`, the coverage floors, `every_contract_method_is_classified`, the Playwright quarantine check), and from this program:

- S2b: `setupRequests.test.ts` reads the restore summary and the old-export refusal out of `support.rs`, so the fixture double cannot drift from the hardware link's words; `scripts/native-lanes.test.mjs` holds every lane to `laneProcessEnv` (scratch app data, a bridge port of its own, the safe start, the simulated console) and every script that does release work to a main-module check.
- S1–S2: `tests/contract.rs` refuses a `planning.` method or event; a migration test proves schema 8 drops the four tables and the settings and turns a saved Planning page into the Console.
- S3: `scripts/check-no-shortcuts.mjs`, in `scripts:test`, fails on a key listener or key handler outside the places it lists with their reasons (the dialogs, the drawer, the popups, the two arms' Esc, focused sliders, lists and fields), on any modifier-key read but a dialog's Shift+Tab trap, on a `<kbd>`, `aria-keyshortcuts`, `accessKey` or a name about shortcuts, and on a key hint in operator copy; it also holds the native shell's WebView2 setting and its one `#[allow(unsafe_code)]`. `frontend/app/tests/no-shortcuts.spec.ts` presses every key the program bound and expects the screen not to move.
- SW: Playwright compares captures on Windows only (`ignoreSnapshots` off Windows, so no capture can be refreshed from another system); the UI contract samples contrast on Windows only (`SAMPLES_CONTRAST`) and skips that measure by name elsewhere; off Windows the Storybook spec checks only that each story loads and paints. The 2560 cases that took over from the size-only ones hold the Console, Lighting, Setup and the recovery screens to no scroll and to their boxes at 2560×1440, and a strip key's tooltip to its strip. The shell's `fullscreen_display` tests hold the window's one rule (the saved display, else the 2560×1440 display, else the one the window is on) and an older build's window file.
- S4: `setupRequests.test.ts` reads `WORKSPACES` and the page refusal out of `shell_settings.rs`, so the fixture double cannot accept a page the hardware link refuses; `prompter/clock/motion-cases.json` holds the hardware link's clock and the front end's `motion.ts` to the same cases; `storage/tests_schema_9.rs` takes the live data's schema 7 to 9 in one start; `action_log`'s classification test wants a class and, when recorded, an example for every `prompter.*` method.
- S5a: `prompterScreen.test.ts` reads `screen.rs` up to its tests and holds the fixture double's state words, sentences and refusals to it word for word; the shell's `page_may_send` test holds `prompter.screen.report` to the shell alone (the page's request path refuses it).
- Part C: the camera and prompter rules as tests — the prompter window opens only on a screen named `Prompter XL` (S5b); a test build refuses a camera address that is not on this PC (S8); no Bluetooth call is reachable from a test, and nothing writes the Pocket 6K Pro's status characteristic (S11).

## Slice 1 — Planning leaves the screen

Status: verified 2026-09-25 (no hardware exposure; landed by #211, squash `e8d43c5`, on the operator's go-ahead "Yes, open and merge it, then start S2"; the six commits under the tag `archive/new-pages-s1-2026-09`, whose tree `git diff` finds identical to `e8d43c5`). The runs: push 36025593650 on `0100fc3` and the pull request's own 36113956361, both all ten jobs green on the first attempt; `main`'s run on `e8d43c5` is recorded with S2. Pushed 2026-09-24; the linux captures followed as `a873e53` `Pages S1 (baselines, linux): …`, and the review's corrections as `Pages S1 (review): …` (below). Committed on 2026-09-24 as four commits — `19cb1fa` `Pages S1: Planning leaves the screen` (the app and the lanes), `cf2a898` `Pages S1 (store and fixtures): …`, `Pages S1 (docs): …` (this record) and `Pages S1 (baselines): …` — because the pre-commit hook's prettier call overflowed the Windows command line with all 61 paths at once (lint-staged chunks at 8,191 characters on win32, which leaves no room for the `prettier.cmd` wrapper); the pull request squashes them into one. Worked on 2026-09-24 on branch `new-pages/s1-planning-leaves-the-screen`, cut from `main` at `359ee4a`; the edits ran as one workflow, one agent at a time (store, screens, fixtures, tests and lanes, consistency), then were checked and finished by hand.

**Rescope:** four differences from the scope below, the title unchanged because what landed is what it says. (1) D1 on screen: the scope said "a saved or unknown page reads as the Console"; what landed reads a saved `planning` as the Console and any other value this front end does not know as Setup, as before — D1 names Planning, and Setup is the safe landing for a value a build cannot place (a page saved by a newer build, say). (2) The Stream Deck's PROJECTS and TASKS pages stay in the fixture double (`transports/fixture/state.ts`), in Setup's echo matcher (`setup/setupControlEcho.ts`) and in `shell.spec.ts`: the hardware link exports them until S2 (D5). (3) Ctrl+4 is no longer handled (it only opened Planning), and the shortcut guide's workspace row reads "1–3"; every other key waits for S3. (4) The setup-support qualification's Planning steps, listed under S2, went in S1: the test bridge lost `seedPlanningDemo` and `createPlanningProject` with the store's Planning actions, so the lane had to follow other saved data in this slice (a snapshot slot of the Console, below); S2's lanes bullet no longer lists it.

What landed:

- **Screens.** `frontend/app/src/app/planning/` (23 files) deleted; `workspaceChunks.ts` cuts three chunks; `OperatorShell.tsx` loses the tab, its palette entry, the Shift+B / Shift+T handling, Planning in the locked-tab list, and the surface chain's Planning branch (its last branch is the Console). Setup's `CONSOLE` key, the recovery surface's "Back to Console" and the key after Publish — "Open the Console", action `open-console` — set `audio`. Load sample planning, its dialog and the Support plate's Sample data section are gone. The test bridge loses its seven Planning commands and takes `setWorkspace` for setup, lighting and audio only.
- **Store and fixture double.** `WorkspaceId` is setup | lighting | audio; `deriveWorkspace` reads a saved `planning` as `audio`. The planning domain leaves the refresh maps (`ALL_DOMAINS` 9); `planning.changed` refreshes the commissioning snapshot only — the hardware link still raises it from the deck's project and task keys until S2, and Setup's summary line reads the counts it carries. Twelve store actions and five request types are gone. The double no longer holds Planning data, answers `planning.snapshot` or the seed, or counts projects and tasks in its commissioning snapshot, its backup replies or its Verify sentence.
- **Fixtures and the design system.** The five Planning fixtures are deleted (22 remain, `check-fixtures.mjs` requires four); two sentences of `setup-degraded` that named the Planning seed are reworded; MetricCard's three Planning-only theming hooks, the css-literals allowlist entry, the ShellPrimitives test and stories' Planning tab and rows, and three comments.
- **Tests and lanes.** `planning.spec.ts` (19 cases), the 15 Planning ratchet entries and the 33 Planning captures (30 visual-review, 3 Storybook; 11 per platform) are deleted. The workspaces lane ends on Lighting and checks that Lighting comes back after the restart (not the Console: until S2 the hardware link's default page is `planning`, which reads as the Console, so a check for the Console would pass with nothing saved). The setup-support lane follows a snapshot slot of the Console, found by name, through the archive restore, the restart, the database restore and the recovery restore, in place of the Planning projects: read from the Rust source, a slot is stored under `app.audio.snapshots_state`, both kinds of backup carry it, and creating or deleting one is never held back by the audio probe (only a recall is). Each changed check says so in its evidence (`markerChanged`, `scopeChanged`).
- **Docs.** This ledger; the production readiness ledger's record of #207–#210 and its Appendix A row F11; DEVELOPMENT (the front-end map, both lanes, the refresh note, 66 boards); AGENTS and the system doc §10 (66 boards); CHANGELOG (Removed); the `dev-checks.yml` comment; the `font.size.name` token's description.

Gates before: `planning.spec.ts` (19 cases); the Planning boards in `ui-contract.spec.ts` (15 ratchet cases and 4 at-rest cases); 10 Planning cases in `visual-review.spec.ts` and the Planning story in `storybook.spec.ts`; the Planning checks of both lanes; the store tests that asserted the planning domain.

Tests added that fail without the change: `createShellStore.test.ts` "reads a saved Planning page as the Console" (at `359ee4a` `deriveWorkspace` returns `planning`); in `domainRefresh.test.ts`, a workspace switch to `planning` fetches the app snapshot only; `startup.spec.ts` counts three tab buttons (the header had four); and the reduced-motion case's probe (below), which fails when the setting does not apply (proved with a copy that left motion allowed: "Expected: 0, Received: 1"). Also added, a regression guard that passes at `359ee4a` as well: `createShellStore.test.ts` "keeps the pages it has, reads anything else as Setup, and holds an uncommissioned desk on Setup" — it pins the rules that did not change (a saved Lighting, Console or Setup page stays; an unknown value lands on Setup; commissioning outranks a saved page, a Planning one included).

Tests changed (old → new → reason):

- `createShellStore.test.ts`: the typed-snapshot set and the expected batches lose `planning.snapshot` (event refresh, unknown event 8 → 7, catalog-once `refresh()` 10 → 9, events raised together 8 → 7, a restart's abandoned batch) — the planning domain is gone; "a request refreshes only what its method can change" loses its three Planning requests, and its archive restore's batch goes 8 → 7 (the changeable domains lose planning); "a request that fails does not cost the others their answers" answers `lighting` instead of `planning`; the two guard cases use the audio snapshot's `channels` instead of Planning's `tasks` / `projects`.
- `domainRefresh.test.ts`: workspaces opened lose `planning`; `ALL_DOMAINS` 10 → 9; `planning.task.create` falls to the changeable domains and the seed to commissioning; `planning.changed` → `["commissioning"]`.
- `snapshotGuards.test.ts`: the Planning cases move to lighting's `groups` or go (the optional `activityLog`).
- `ShellErrorBoundary.test.tsx`: only the stub's `planning.snapshot` answer goes.
- `ShellPrimitives.test.tsx`: the locked tab is Lighting; three tabs instead of four; the locked-label loop without Planning.
- `css-literals.test.ts`: unchanged; its allowlist loses the deleted stylesheet.
- `check-fixtures.mjs`: the required ids lose the two Planning fixtures, and the `planningSnapshot` checks go.
- `setup.spec.ts`: after Publish (and after "Publish anyway") the Console mounts instead of Planning; after Cancel the Console never appears; "loading sample planning asks for confirmation first" deleted.
- `lighting.spec.ts`: the bookmark case leaves with Ctrl+3 to the Console instead of Ctrl+4 to Planning.
- `startup.spec.ts`: three locked tab labels and a count of three; the Planning surface check goes.
- `audio.spec.ts`: the no-refresh list loses `planning.snapshot`, which could never be asked for any more.
- `shell.spec.ts` "lazy workspace loads": three chunks.
- `ui-contract.spec.ts`: "prefers-reduced-motion stops everything, the loading skeleton included" opens `audio-loading` instead of `planning-loading-board`; Planning's loading sweep was the only animation running at rest on any board (8 in each theme at `359ee4a`), so the case now adds a probe that would run forever — it must run with motion allowed (the control) and stop with reduced motion, which only the app-wide rule in `global.css` can do (the adversarial review found the first version of this change could no longer fail); the x-band case drops `planning-populated`.
- `visual-review.spec.ts`, `storybook.spec.ts`, `helpers/openFixture.ts`: the Planning fixtures, the story, the clock freeze, the crash target and the workspace mark go.
- The lanes: `planning-live-mutations-round-trip` deleted; `restart-preserves-migrated-workspace-state` on Lighting; `backup-restore-round-trips-native-support-backup`, `persisted-restart-restores-dashboard-state`, `database-backup-verifies-and-restores-after-restart` and `corrupt-db-restores-from-database-backup` follow the snapshot slot. `database-backup-verifies-and-restores-after-restart` read the Planning counts on the commissioning snapshot, so it also passed through the `planning.changed` → commissioning refresh; since this slice `domainRefresh.test.ts` alone holds that coupling, until S2 removes the event (the production readiness ledger's Appendix A row F11 says so). The workspaces lane checks no Planning data after its restart any more; it checks the Lighting fixture and the recalled scene, and the Console's state only where the audio probe runs.

Validation (workstation, 2026-09-24): `npm run frontend:typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run frontend:test` (app 104, design system 175, engine client 113, tokens 15) and `frontend:test:coverage` (every floor met; app 11.51 / 11.23 / 10.29 / 11.79 %), `npm run format:check`, the copy gate 0, `npm run file:health`, `npm run scripts:test` (150: 146 passed, 4 skipped). The full Playwright lane: 391 cases (441 before, 50 fewer), 362 passed, 29 failed — captures only. 16 of them moved at 2560×1440 or in Storybook and were refreshed after their diffs were inspected (the header without the Planning tab; the Publish key; the Support plate without Sample data; the three design-system stories): `setup-ready`, `protocol-mismatch` and `setup-degraded` at 2560×1440, `setup-ready` and `protocol-mismatch` in Graphite and Bone, six OperatorShell stories and three design-system stories. The update run rewrote one more, `lighting-populated` in Graphite at 2560×1440: its change is the Planning tab's place in the header (x 640–896), inside the tolerance on the first run and over it on the second, so it was kept. 13 win32 captures at other sizes keep their old baselines by the operator's rule of 2026-09-22 and fail the local lane by design: `lighting-populated` at 1280, 1440, 1600, 1728 and 1920 and in the 1512×982 studio preview (stale since 2026-09-22), and now `setup-ready` at the same five sizes and in the studio preview, and `protocol-mismatch` at 1920 (the 12 before this slice included the six Planning ones, deleted with their board). The census (`node scripts/ui-census.mjs --write-ratchets`): 66 boards, no measure rose and none fell, the 15 Planning entries gone. `npm run dev:check` green end to end (engine 417 passed / 1 ignored, shell 20, contract 4, end-to-end 5). No hardware-link change, so no `native:acceptance`. Neither lane can run here while the live app holds port 38201; CI's `qualification` job runs both.

CI (result, 2026-09-24): push run 36017658749 on `b94f4a2` — nine jobs green; `frontend-e2e` failed on exactly the 24 linux captures the slice moved (367 passed), as expected; `qualification` green on both lanes with the new saved data (setup-support 17 checks in 378.5 s, the four changed checks carrying `markerChanged` / `scopeChanged` in their evidence; workspaces 5 checks — 6 before, less the Planning round-trip — in 89.6 s). The 24 captures were taken from that run's `playwright-test-results` artifact, paired from the job log's Expected / Received lines, compared with their win32 siblings after an 8× reduction (mean 0.00–0.97 of 255 against the win32 captures refreshed in this slice, 1.00–1.64 against the stale win32 ones at other sizes, and 9.11 for `setup-ready` at 1280×800, compared side by side with its stale win32 baseline — the same board without the Planning tab; `a873e53`'s message gives 0.00–1.64 for the first two groups together) and looked at on a contact sheet. Run 36019338566 on `a873e53`: all ten jobs green on the first attempt, `frontend-e2e` 391 passed.

Review (2026-09-24; a workflow, one agent at a time: four reviewers — the product's behaviour, the lanes, the tests' strength, the records — and a skeptic for every finding, asked to refute it): 15 findings, 12 upheld as real (none high; one medium), 3 refuted. The upheld ones, eight distinct: (1) the reduced-motion case could no longer fail — it now carries a probe that must run with motion allowed and stop with it reduced, proved by a copy that left motion allowed ("Expected: 0, Received: 1"); (2) the medium one: the production readiness ledger's Appendix A row F11 still named the database-restore lane check as a guard of the `planning.changed` → commissioning refresh, which it no longer passes through — corrected there and above; (3) "Tests added" counted a regression guard as failing without the change — it passes at `359ee4a` too, and is now listed as such; (4) "Tests changed" left out the archive restore's batch going 8 → 7; (5) the Rescope paragraph missed that the setup-support lane's Planning steps, listed under S2, went in S1 — now item (4), and S2's lanes bullet no longer lists it; (6) DEVELOPMENT and CHANGELOG said both lanes follow a snapshot slot, while only the setup-support lane does (so does the message of `19cb1fa`; the pull request's squash message says it right); (7) "Found, not changed" named one board where six carry a copy-gate hit; (8) the readiness ledger dated #207–#210's landing 2026-09-24 in its heading — they merged on 2026-09-23. Refuted: the first form of (1), because the loss had been recorded; that a page saved while Planning was open is never saved again as the Console (it is not meant to be: the store reads it as the Console on every start, and S2's schema 8 rewrites it); and a wording point in the baselines commit.

Found, not changed: six boards carry one copy-gate hit each, all ratcheted at 1 before this slice too — `audio-offline` in the three themes ("Console did not answer OSC ping.", the fixture's sentence) and `protocol-mismatch` in the three themes ("Shell expected protocol 1 but engine reported 2.", the fixture's recovery message).

Scope: the Planning tab, workspace and chunk (`frontend/app/src/app/planning/`, 23 files; `workspaceChunks.ts`); `OperatorShell.tsx` (the tab, its palette entry, the Shift-key handling while Planning is active, the locked-tab list, and the surface chain whose final `else` renders Planning); D1 on screen — Setup's `CONSOLE` key (`SetupSupportPilot.tsx`), the recovery surface's key (`SetupRecoverySurface.tsx`) and the key after Publish (`useSetupPilotActions.ts`, "Open planning") go to the Console, and a saved or unknown page reads as the Console; "Load sample planning" (`commissioning.seedPlanningDemo`) leaves Setup / Support; the store's Planning domain, requests and refresh mapping (`createShellStore.ts`, `domainRefresh.ts`, `snapshotGuards.ts`, `types.ts`, `index.ts`); the fixture double's Planning (`transports/fixture/planning.ts`, `planningRequests.ts`, and the Planning parts of `state.ts` and `setupRequests.ts`); the five Planning fixtures and the Planning parts of the others (`fixtures.json`, `check-fixtures.mjs`); the Storybook stories; `planning.spec.ts` and the Planning parts of the other specs; the 15 Planning boards and their ratchets; the 33 Planning captures (11 each for win32, linux and darwin); the workspaces qualification lane's Planning steps. The hardware link still answers `planning.*` after this slice — S2 removes it — and nothing on screen asks.

Also in this pull request: this ledger, and the production readiness ledger's record of #207–#210 (After the program) — their runs, by that ledger's convention.

Validation: `npm run dev:check`; the full Playwright lane — the header moves on every board, so the win32 captures at 2560×1440 are refreshed and inspected, and the linux ones are refreshed from the branch push run's artifact before merge; the census re-seeded with nothing rising; CI's ten checks (the qualification lanes run there, and since Slice SF only there).

## Slice 2 — Planning leaves the hardware link

Status: landed (operator verification pending — Appendix B items 1 and 3, at the close-out's build). Landed by #213, squash `23a2f9a` at 14:46 UTC on 2026-09-25, on the operator's go-ahead ("Both, flash fix first"); the five commits under the tag `archive/new-pages-s2-2026-09` (`943fd95`), whose tree `git diff` finds identical to `23a2f9a`. The runs after the review's: push 36146691591 on `943fd95` (`main` merged in, and `Pages S2 (ledger): …`) and the pull request's own 36148208506, both all ten jobs green on the first attempt; `main`'s run on `23a2f9a`, 36149738781, all ten green, and on #212's `1ed8d86`, 36146509724, all ten green. Committed on 2026-09-25 as `39336a0` `Pages S2: Planning leaves the hardware link` and `6cd80bb` `Pages S2 (docs): …` (two commits for the pre-commit hook's command-line limit, as in S1; the pull request squashes them); their push run 36131534655 was all ten jobs green on the first attempt. The adversarial review followed (below), and its corrections are `42e1104` `Pages S2 (review): …`, whose push run 36140370259 was all ten jobs green. The operator's go-ahead for the pull request came on 2026-09-25 ("Both, flash fix first"); after #212 landed, `main` was merged in (`0c503ff`). Branch `new-pages/s2-planning-leaves-the-hardware-link`, cut from `main` at `e8d43c5` on 2026-09-25; the edits ran as one workflow, one agent at a time (contract and core, storage, backups, the Stream Deck, the lanes, the documents, consistency), then were checked and finished by hand. `main`'s run on `e8d43c5` (S1's merge) was 36115092961.

**Rescope:** the legacy db.json import leaves in a slice of its own, S2b, after this one. The survey before S2 found that seven lanes and scripts put a test engine into a set-up state through it — `native:acceptance` (CI's `rust` job), `native:bridge:win:verify`, the packaging smoke's `dashboard` scenario, the packaged, installer and delivery acceptance scripts and the old Tauri candidate packager — and so do the development parity fixtures. Retiring it means seeding each of them through the app's own requests, which is a slice's work of its own. In S2 the import stays, reduced to what is not Planning (the setup flag and the page to open); S2b retires it (D3) together with `storage.importLegacyDb`, the auto-import and `SSE_LEGACY_DB_PATH`. The operator was told when the split was made.

**Rescope:** five more differences from the scope below, the title unchanged. (1) The protocol version moves to 2: `native/protocol/v1.md`'s own rule says removing fields or methods needs a bump, and a window and a hardware link of different versions now stop at the start with `PROTOCOL_MISMATCH` instead of failing in odd ways (the contract and the document keep their `v1` file names; `scripts/native-runtime-harness.mjs` and `scripts/tauri-smoke.mjs` read the version from the contract). (2) The deck mode is gone rather than moved: nothing read it once Planning left (page-follow reads `shell.workspace`), so `switchToDeckMode` is refused (501) on both routes and the page keys post nothing; `/api/deck/action` is no longer a bridge route (400). (3) The screen shows the restore's new `detail` sentence ("Planning data in this backup was not restored; …") after its own, in Setup / Support and on the recovery surface (`withRestoreDetail`, `shellData.ts`) — the first front-end change of this slice beyond the regenerated types. (4) The development parity fixtures lose their Planning parts (and the two Planning fixtures go), guarded by `the_bundled_fixtures_carry_no_planning`. (5) A new guard, `scripts/native-lanes.test.mjs` (in `scripts:test`), reads the lanes and fails on a request the contract does not have, a bridge route or LCD key the bridge does not serve, and a db.json fixture holding more than the import reads.

Scope: the contract's 18 `planning.*` methods and `planning.changed` (`native/protocol/v1.contract.json`, `v1.md`, `npm run protocol:generate`, `tests/contract.rs`, the generated TypeScript, the action log's two lists and `every_contract_method_is_classified`); `native/rust-engine/src/planning/` (13 files) and `planning_settings.rs`; the Planning parts of `app.rs`, `app_state.rs`, `commissioning.rs` (the counts, `seedPlanningDemo`), `control_surface.rs` (the project and task actions, the deck mode), `engine_events.rs`, `parity_fixtures.rs` and `storage.rs`.

- **Schema 8** (D2): drop the four tables and the `planning.*` settings, and turn a saved `shell.workspace = planning` into `audio`, behind the verified pre-migration backup; the step is keyed to its own literal (`if schema_version < 8`). `DEFAULT_WORKSPACE` becomes `audio` (D1).
- **Backups** (D3): the archive stops carrying Planning (format 5); formats up to 4 are accepted with their Planning part skipped, and the sentence says so; Verify's sentence loses "<n> projects, <n> tasks".
- **The legacy db.json import is retired** (D3) — moved to S2b; in S2 it is reduced to the setup flag and the page (see the Rescope above): `legacy_import.rs`, the staged auto-import, `SSE_LEGACY_DB_PATH`, a `db.json` among the restore sources (refused by name from now on), its fixtures under `native/rust-engine/fixtures/`, the end-to-end tests, and AGENTS.md's "Retained legacy surface".
- **The deck** (D5): PROJECTS and TASKS leave `exports.rs`; the page keys and the page-follow triggers chain LIGHTS · AUDIO until Part C adds CAMERAS and PROMPTER.
- **The lanes**: `native:acceptance` (`scripts/native-acceptance.mjs`, `native-parity-acceptance.mjs`); `native:bridge:win:verify` (`native-control-surface-qualification.mjs`); the packaged, installer and delivery acceptance scripts.
- **Docs**: `v1.md`, OPERATIONS ("Planning data looks wrong or missing"), ARCHITECTURE, README, HANDOFF, AGENTS, CHANGELOG, OPERATOR_WORKSTATION_ROLLOUT, the issue and pull-request templates.

Hardware exposure: the saved data's upgrade and the deck profile, which reach the studio only with the close-out's live build (Appendix B items 1 and 3).

Validation: `npm run dev:check`; `npm run native:engine:build && npm run native:acceptance`; `npm run native:test:dev-fixtures`; the migration tested on a synthetic schema-7 database, never the real one; CI's ten checks.

What landed:

- **Contract and core.** The 18 `planning.*` methods, `commissioning.seedPlanningDemo`, `planning.changed`, the two Planning parity fixture ids and every Planning field of the app and commissioning snapshots leave the contract (`protocol:generate`; 7 generated Planning bindings gone); protocol 2. `native/rust-engine/src/planning/` (13 files) and `planning_settings.rs` are deleted; `app.rs`, `app_state.rs`, `commissioning.rs` (the counts, the seed, `sampleSeedAvailable`; its sentences now match the fixture double's), `engine_events.rs`, `action_log.rs`, both protocol crates and `parity_fixtures.rs` follow. D1: `DEFAULT_WORKSPACE` is `audio`, and `planning` is no longer a page `settings.update` accepts.
- **Storage.** Schema 8 (`if schema_version < 8`): drops `task_checklist_items`, `tasks`, `projects` and `activity_log`, deletes every `planning.*` setting and the counts an earlier db.json import stored, turns a saved `shell.workspace = planning` into `audio`. A new database runs the old steps and ends at 8 without the Planning tables; the `planning.*` defaults are no longer seeded. The existing check writes the verified pre-migration copy before 7 → 8 (tested). A schema-9 database is refused by name ("supports up to 8") before any step or copy. The interim import reads only `schemaVersion`, `settings.dashboardView` and `settings.hasCompletedSetup` and writes four settings (the three commissioning keys and the page — Lighting stays Lighting, anything else opens the Console); its gate is now "a completed setup or an earlier import".
- **Backups.** Archive format 5 carries no Planning; an archive of format 4 or older restores everything but its Planning part (a saved `planning` page is restored as `audio`) and the reply's new optional `detail` says what was left out. Verify: "Backup archive, format N, exported T." / "Database backup, schema N, integrity ok: K settings.", with the Planning note when a backup has some. Database backups of schema 7 and 8 verify and restore (a schema-7 one is upgraded at the next start); schema 9 is refused. A db.json in the backups folder restores its four settings.
- **The Stream Deck** (D5). Two pages, LIGHTS (1) and AUDIO (2), from one `DECK_PAGES` table in `exports.rs` (Part C adds rows); PROJECTS, TASKS, the `<< PROJ` key, the Planning follow trigger, five Planning LCD keys and their custom variables (34 → 29) are gone. Two fixes the new LCD guard found: LIGHTS' `AUDIO >>` asked for four LCD keys that no longer existed, and arriving on LIGHTS now refreshes its four LCDs (only the PROJECTS page's `LIGHTS >>` had refreshed `scene_nav`). The deck's worst instant grows from 42 to 46 requests (the lighting follow trigger's four), inside the pool's 68. The fixture double mirrors the hardware link: two pages, 43 controls ("2 pages · 43 controls" on Setup instead of "4 pages · 48 controls").
- **The lanes** keep their intent through data that still exists: the import is seen by the page it saves (`commissioning-sample-db.json` opens on Lighting); restart persistence and the rollback follow the saved page; the archive is checked for format 5 with no Planning; the installer and delivery lanes' continuity sentinel is a lighting group (`native:acceptance` makes the same one on every CI run); the bridge lane checks the two-page profile (new step `profile-two-pages`) and presses the LIGHTS next-light key instead of the Planning filter. The two db.json fixtures keep only what the interim import reads.
- **Docs.** `v1.md` (the removals, the bridge, backups, the import, protocol 2), OPERATIONS ("Saved data looks wrong or missing", the deck's two pages and the re-import, format 5, schema 8), ARCHITECTURE, README, `native/README.md`, HANDOFF, AGENTS, DEVELOPMENT, OPERATOR_WORKSTATION_ROLLOUT, RELEASE, the pull-request and issue templates, CHANGELOG (Removed).

Gates before: the engine's Planning tests (14 in `planning/tests.rs` and the seed and count cases in `commissioning.rs`); the deck's PROJECTS / TASKS tests; the lanes' Planning workflow (`assertPlanningWorkflowParity`, `planning.report.time`, the imported 2 projects / 3 tasks, the Planning continuity sentinels, the bridge's `/api/deck/action` round trip); `tests/contract.rs` held the contract and the engine together but not "no Planning".

Tests added — 40, each proved against `e8d43c5`'s code or a sabotaged copy restored byte for byte (`cmp`), unless marked a regression guard: `tests/contract.rs` `the_contract_has_no_planning_method_event_or_fixture` (22 names at `e8d43c5`) and its self-test; `shell_settings` `new_saved_data_and_a_saved_planning_page_open_the_console`; `commissioning_snapshot_says_nothing_of_planning`; `planning_requests_are_unknown_methods`; `planning_fixtures_are_refused`; `planning_lcd_keys_and_context_are_gone`; `storage/tests_schema_8.rs` (the v7 → v8 migration after its copy; every other saved page kept — its page check a regression guard; a schema-9 database refused; the import gate); `legacy_import` `a_planning_board_opens_the_console` and `the_planning_part_of_a_db_json_is_ignored`; `bootstrap` `the_start_up_import_never_replaces_saved_data`; `support/tests.rs` (a new archive is format 5 without Planning; a format-4 archive restores all but its Planning part; Verify says the part is skipped; database backups of schema 7 and 8 restore, 9 refused); `exports.rs` (no Planning in the profile and the page model; every LCD shown is shipped, refreshed and answered; the page keys and follow triggers chain LIGHTS and AUDIO); `the_deck_mode_key_is_refused_and_stores_nothing`; `the_planning_keys_route_is_not_a_bridge_endpoint`; `parity_fixtures` `the_bundled_fixtures_carry_no_planning`; the fixture double's `controlSurface.test.ts` (2) and `setupRequests.test.ts` (3; two are regression guards); `shellData.test.ts` (2; one a regression guard) and `SetupSupportPilot.test.tsx` (the restore prints the hardware link's `detail`); `scripts/native-lanes.test.mjs` (4; one a self-check of its scanners); and in the lanes, the saved-page, archive and sentinel checks.

Tests changed (old → new → reason), 59, in the order the work touched them. In short — deleted with Planning: `planning/tests.rs` (14), `commissioning_snapshot_reflects_seeded_planning_counts` (its non-Planning checks moved into the new commissioning test), `sample_seed_preserves_commissioning_stage_and_workspace`, `normalize_settings_derives_project_from_selected_task`, `assertPlanningWorkflowParity` and the `planning.report.time` checks. Moved off Planning data, same assertion otherwise: the parity-fixture tests (`planning-empty` → `setup-ready`, 3 events → 2), `load_defaults_to_merge`, `cycle_value_wraps_*` (a local four-value list), `workspace_lcd_key_reads_shell_workspace` (stores `lighting`, since `audio` is now the default), the bridge tests (`/api/deck/action` → `/api/deck/light-action`; `projectCount` → `strips`; `project_nav` → `light_nav`), the end-to-end auto-import tests (the setup flag instead of the project count), the backup tests (counts → `formatVersion`, `detail`, settings), the migration tests (every road now ends at schema 8; the v2 → v3 test can no longer read the dropped `tasks` columns), the store and refresh tests (`planning.changed` → `support.changed` / `settings.changed`, 5 events → 4), `setupControlEcho.test.ts` and `shell.spec.ts` (the deck's pages), the export and page-model tests (4 pages → 2, 34 variables → 29, AUDIO page 4 → 2, 4 triggers → 3), and the lanes' import, restart, rollback, sentinel and bridge steps (above).

- `native/rust-engine/src/planning/tests.rs` — 14 Planning tests: read_planning_snapshot_returns_imported_planning_state, read_planning_context_returns_selected_projec: DELETED with the module. _Why:_ They exist only for Planning; the handlers they test are gone.
- `native/rust-engine/src/commissioning.rs` — commissioning_snapshot_reflects_seeded_planning_counts: DELETED (seed, then counts 2/3, summary contains '2 projects', import step current, config summary 'Lighting bridge', readiness 'Startup still routes') → replaced by the new commissioning_snapshot_says_nothing_of_planning, which keeps the non-Planning checks. _Why:_ It exists for the Planning seed and counts, which are gone.
- `native/rust-engine/src/commissioning.rs` — sample_seed_preserves_commissioning_stage_and_workspace: DELETED. _Why:_ It tests only the seed (commissioning.seedPlanningDemo), which is gone.
- `native/rust-engine/src/commissioning.rs` — control_surface_probe_records_passed_status: Seeded sample Planning data first, then asserted status passed → no seed; still asserts passed, plus: the message starts 'Control surface bridge exposes ' and does not contain 'planning'. _Why:_ The seed is gone, and the probe no longer describes a Planning selection.
- `native/rust-engine/src/app/tests.rs` — parity_fixture_unavailable_without_feature: fixtureId planning-empty, no parity-fixture-planning-empty.json written → fixtureId setup-ready, no parity-fixture-setup-ready.json written (the other assertions are unchanged). _Why:_ The Planning fixture ids left the contract.

- `scripts/native-acceptance.mjs` — Step 2 restart persistence: restarted projectCount 2 / taskCount 3 → restarted shell.workspace === 'lighting' and the sentinel lighting group present (dashboard and stage-ready checks kept). _Why:_ The same persistence proof, through data that still exists.
- `scripts/native-acceptance.mjs` — Mutations before the restore, and rollback after it: Planning workflow; mutated projectCount === 4 with temporary projects present; after the restore projectCount === 2 and temporary projects/tasks gone → settings.update to 'audio' (reply shows 'audio'); after the restore shell.workspace === 'lighting', the sen. _Why:_ Planning data left the saved data and the archive; the saved page is the marker the restore must roll back.
- `scripts/native-packaged-acceptance.mjs` — Import, restart and rollback checks (packaged engine): The same Planning counts, workflow and rollback as native-acceptance → saved page 'lighting' after the import, the restart and the restore; page moved to 'audio' before the restore; archive format 5 without Planning; restore detail undefined. _Why:_ Same as native-acceptance.mjs; the lane shares the helpers so the two runtime forms cannot drift.
- `scripts/native-installer-acceptance.mjs` — Import check and continuity sentinel: projectCount 2 / taskCount 3 after the import; planning.project.create 'Installer Continuity Sentinel' with projectCount 3; after the reinstall projectCount 3 and the project title present → shell.workspace 'lighting' after the import and after the reinstall;. _Why:_ Planning projects no longer exist; a lighting group is saved data the lane can create.
- `scripts/native-delivery-acceptance.mjs` — Import check and continuity sentinel (install, update, reinstall): projectCount 2/3 after the import; the 'Delivery Continuity Sentinel' project and projectCount 3 after the update and the reinstall → shell.workspace 'lighting' at each step; lighting group 'Delivery Continuity Sentinel' present after creation, the update and. _Why:_ As for the installer lane.
- `scripts/native-control-surface-qualification.mjs` — bridge-snapshot-contract: 4 pages containing PROJECTS, TASKS, LIGHTS and AUDIO → page labels exactly ['LIGHTS','AUDIO'] in order. _Why:_ D5.
- `scripts/native-control-surface-qualification.mjs` — bridge-http-routes: context has projectCount/viewFilter/sortBy and a workspace string; project_nav LCD includes 'PROJECT'; workspace LCD non-empty; setFilter via /api/deck/action round-trips to context and planning.snapshot; switchToDeckMode 'light' persists into planning.snapsho. _Why:_ The Planning route, LCD, context fields and deck mode are gone; the LIGHTS key is the deck-to-snapshot round trip that remains.
- `scripts/native-control-surface-qualification.mjs` — bridge-request-refusals: no-token and wrong-token POSTs of deleteProject to /api/deck/action; oversized (setFilter body), absurd-length and stalled POSTs to /api/deck/action; projectCount unchanged → the same requests (401/401/413/413/408, origin 403, host 400, key decoding unchanged. _Why:_ /api/deck/action is no longer a route; nothing getting through is now proved by state that exists.
- `native/rust-engine/src/action_log/tests.rs` — deck_keys_leave_rows_for_discrete_changes_only: The no-row list included ('/api/deck/light-action', 'switchToDeckMode', {deckMode: 'light'}) and expected no row. That entry is removed; every other entry and assertion is unchanged.. _Why:_ The deck mode was a Planning setting and left with Planning in S2. switchToDeckMode is now refused with 501 on both routes, and deck_actions is onl

Validation (workstation, 2026-09-25): the protocol bump and the checks after it by hand — `npm run protocol:generate` / `protocol:check`; `npm run native:test` (engine 420 passed / 1 ignored, shell 20, contract 6, end-to-end 5, after the two end-to-end protocol assertions moved to the crate's constant); `npm run native:engine:build && npm run native:acceptance` passed; `npm run frontend:test` (app 107, design system 175, engine client 118, tokens 15), `scripts:test` (154: 150 passed, 4 skipped), `lint`, `format:check`, `file:health`, `rust:clippy`, `rust:fmt:check`, the copy gate 0; the workflow's consistency pass also ran `native:test:dev-fixtures` (engine 424 / 1 ignored) and the coverage floors (met). The full Playwright lane: 391 cases, 377 passed; 13 failures are the known stale win32 captures at other sizes, and none at 2560×1440 moved beyond its tolerance (the Setup boards' deck line and the Support plate's "protocol 2" are small text changes); one behaviour failure, `lighting-rig-actions.spec.ts` "The page shows each flash start and end by itself", is a flake older than this program — run alone it failed 3 of 5 at `359ee4a` and 4 of 5 at `e8d43c5` (before this slice), and it has passed on every CI run so far; it is flagged as a task of its own. The census: 66 boards, no measure rose or fell. The packaged, installer, delivery and bridge lanes were read and `node --check`ed only (they need the live app closed or rebuild `release\native\windows`); they run at the close-out's build.

Review (2026-09-25; a workflow, one agent at a time: four reviewers — saved data and backups, the Stream Deck and its bridge, the lanes and tests, the records — and a skeptic for every finding, asked to refute it; the data-safety skeptic read the operator's newest backup archive, from 2026-09-24 11:44, read-only): 13 findings, 9 upheld (three medium), 4 refuted. Upheld and corrected in `Pages S2 (review): …`: (1) medium — schema 8 kept the Control Surface Probe's saved line, which still talked about Planning ("Planning context is reachable. …"), and Setup would show it; the operator's archive carries one. Now `retire_planning_probe_message` (`commissioning.rs`) turns any line that begins so into "Checked before this version of Studio Control. Run the probe again to refresh this line.", schema 8 does the same with one statement, and an archive's restore writes each probe's line through it; the probe's status stays, so publishing is not affected. Proved by `a_probe_line_about_planning_reads_as_checked_before_this_version` and by new checks in `migrate_v7_to_v8_drops_planning_after_snapshot` (the pre-migration copy keeps the old line) and `a_format_4_archive_restores_everything_but_its_planning_part`; all three fail with the helper made a no-op and the statement disabled. (2) medium — Setup's Map / Verify step drew the LIGHTS page one place early: its keys start at place 2 (place 1 held `<< PROJ`), and the matrix laid them one after another, so `AUDIO >>` showed at row 2, column 3 instead of 4. Now `deckKeySlots` (`setupPilotModel.ts`) places each key by its `position` in whole rows of four, with a dashed blank where the page has no key; `SetupMapVerifyStep.test.tsx` (4 cases) fails on the old drawing. It is a second front-end change of this slice beyond the regenerated types, and the Setup map boards move. (3) the comments in `exports_audio.rs` and `exports.rs` said the LIGHTS LCDs are refreshed by the keys that change them; only the Light dial's press refreshes three of them, and the dial turns and scene keys refresh none — reworded to match the code (the behaviour is unchanged; adding refreshes is left for a later slice). (4) `database_restore_stages_pending_file_and_keeps_rollback` no longer checked what is inside the pre-restore copy — it does again (the Tests changed list above). (5)–(9) this ledger: the top Status still said Slice 1 on base `359ee4a` (medium); the program-level Rescope line said "none yet"; the Program shape said "db.json retired" under S2; S2's Status named the commits as the next step though they were pushed and green; and Tests changed pointed at a report outside the repository instead of listing old → new → reason — all corrected above. Refuted: that OPERATIONS is wrong because the rollback build (`98bbb06`) calls a schema-8 database backup "not a Studio Control database" (true of that build, whose Verify counts Planning rows before it compares versions, but OPERATIONS documents this build, which refuses a newer backup by its schema number; the way back is the pre-migration copy, as D2 and OPERATIONS' "Upgrades are one-way" say); that Slice 1 cannot be `verified` while Appendix B item 1 is unsigned (S1 has no hardware exposure; item 1 is S2's gate); that Rescope item (3) hides the slice's other front-end changes (S1's Rescope (2) had named them as S2's work); and that CHANGELOG's entry must mention protocol 2 (`v1.md`'s Versioning section and this ledger record it, and the entry says nothing wrong). The corrections' validation (workstation, 2026-09-25): `npm run dev:check` (app 111, design system 175, engine client 118, tokens 15; engine 421 passed / 1 ignored, shell 20, contract 6, end-to-end 5; lint, format, file health, clippy, fmt); `npm run native:engine:build && npm run native:acceptance` passed; Playwright's `setup.spec.ts` and `shell.spec.ts`, 31 of 31 (both walk through the map); the map at 2560×1440 in the fixture preview, place 1 blank and LIGHTS' last key at row 2, column 4. No fixture opens on the map step, so no board or capture moves.

Found, not changed: the acceptance engine (`native-parity-acceptance.mjs`) sets neither `SSE_CONTROL_SURFACE_PORT` nor `SSE_SAFE_START`, so on the workstation its bridge tries the live app's port 38201 and fails harmlessly (os error 10048) — older than this program, for S2b, which rewrites that script's seeding anyway; `docs/release-assets/screenshot-*.png` (2026-05) still show the Planning tab (close-out); the fixture double's `exports.companion.export` reply lacks `triggerCount` and `deckSurfaceId`.

## Slice SF — Faster checks

Status: verified 2026-09-25 (no hardware exposure; landed by #214, squash `7fb6f4d` at 19:48 UTC, on the operator's go-ahead at the push, "Yes, merge when green"; the two commits under the tag `archive/new-pages-sf-2026-09` (`f8e736b`), whose tree `git diff` finds identical to `7fb6f4d`). The corrections' run 36180822808 on `f8e736b` was all ten jobs green on the first attempt, and its Rust cache listed only 1.95.0 (the image's stable uninstalled by `.github/actions/setup-rust`); `main`'s run on `7fb6f4d`, 36181929901, all ten green in 10.2 minutes with cold caches, saved the caches the branches now read, `qualification`'s first. Pushed as `eeb0017` (`Pages SF: faster checks …`) on 2026-09-25: run 36176953914, the first push-only run, all ten jobs green on the first attempt in 10.4 minutes with every Rust cache cold; pull request #214, opened at the push, read that run's checks and started no run of its own. The operator's go-ahead came at the push ("Yes, merge when green": when the review's fixes and the eight-worker commit are in and all ten checks are green on the final commit). Branch `new-pages/sf-faster-checks`, cut from `main` at `23a2f9a`; the operator's word for the slice: "Yes, before S2b (Recommended)", with every process change proposed and the agent limit lifted.

Why: on 2026-09-25 the operator asked where the waiting goes and how to use the studio workstation (an i9-13900KS, 24 cores and 32 threads, 32 GB) fully. A read-only workflow measured it — a CI analyst over the last 30 runs, a local analyst on this machine, a process analyst over #204 and #206–#213, then an adversarial verifier over all 31 proposals (6 sound, 21 sound with corrections, 3 unverifiable, 1 unsafe). What it found: a `dev-checks` run takes 13.2 minutes (median; 12.2 to 14.4), and `qualification` finished last in all 30 — it waited 2.4 minutes for `tauri-foundation` to share a cache that never holds what it builds, so its pre-build compiled about 300 crates on every run (96.5 s), and its two lanes waited out a five-second deadline on each of ten shell closes, because a process killed by a signal keeps `exitCode` `null`. A third of the pull-request branch runs tested a commit a push run had already tested (the concurrency group keys on the ref, which differs between the two events), and with the pull request opened after the push run, each slice waited two runs in a row. Locally, `dev:check` ran its twelve steps one after another, twice ran the same Vitest suites, kept about 4 of the 32 threads busy and took 56 s (76 s when its output was piped, as the assistant's runs are: a shell test helper left `ping` running with the output handles for 20 s); Playwright ran 3 workers, 188 s.

Scope (the verified, rule-free fixes; the process rulings are in `Tracking:` above):

- **CI once per commit.** `dev-checks.yml` runs on push only, every branch, Dependabot's included; a pull request reads the checks on its head commit. A pull request from a fork gets no run; to merge one, its head commit is pushed to a branch of this repository.
- **Qualification starts at once.** No `needs: tauri-foundation`, and a Rust cache of its own (`shared-key: qualification`): the dependencies of the debug `test-bridge` shell and of the engine (rust-cache keeps dependencies; the workspace's own crates still compile, and the first `tauri dev` launch still rebuilds the shell crate for a few seconds because the Tauri CLI's environment re-runs its build script). The pre-build passes the flags `tauri dev` runs cargo with (`--no-default-features --features test-bridge`), which with `default = []` changes nothing today.
- **Caches saved from `main` only** (`save-if` on all four Rust caches: the store sat at 9.93 of its 10 GB, about a third of it copies saved from pull-request refs that only those refs could read), and **the project's Rust alone**: `.github/actions/setup-rust` installs the toolchain `native/rust-toolchain.toml` names and removes every other one the runner image carries, because rust-cache hashes every installed toolchain into its key — with the image's stable left in place the key would change whenever the image moved to a new Rust, and every branch would build cold until `main` saved again. No third-party action (`dtolnay/rust-toolchain` is gone). `native:check` leaves the `rust` job (`rust:clippy --workspace --all-targets` checks more). `scripts/ci-workflow.test.mjs` holds all of this.
- **The shell closes end when the shell has gone** — `scripts/tauri-shell-running.mjs`: on Linux a close waits until no process of the shell's group is left, and a close starts unless the group has gone (an npm leader that exited can leave vite or the shell behind); the SIGKILL of the group at the deadline stays, and so does Windows' `taskkill /T /F`.
- **The frontend tests run once**, with coverage: tokens and shared graphics get a `test:coverage` script (no floor yet), `frontend-test` and `dev:check` run `frontend:test:coverage` only, and `scripts/frontend-test-gate.test.mjs` fails a workspace that has `test` but no `test:coverage`.
- **`dev:check` runs its steps side by side** (`scripts/dev-check.mjs`, started by `scripts/dev-check-cli.mjs`): below-normal priority, inherited by every step (`DEV_CHECK_PRIORITY=normal` keeps the normal one); a step starts once the steps it names have finished, passed or failed, so every step runs and one run shows every failure; the cargo steps in one chain (`protocol:check` → `rust:clippy` → `native:test`), prettier and the typecheck after `protocol:check` (it writes a staging folder they would read); local prettier and ESLint caches in places of their own, keyed on the lockfile and the tools' configuration; a log per step, and a failed step's log printed whole when short, else its failure lines and its end. `npm run dev:check:serial` runs the same steps one after another at the normal priority — a timing failure is re-run there before it is believed.
- **The shell test helper runs `ping` itself**, not `cmd /C ping`, so killing it leaves nothing holding the test run's output.
- **Local Playwright at eight workers** (`workers: process.env.CI ? 3 : 8`), below-normal priority from `scripts/frontend/run-playwright.mjs`. Two things stood in the way, found by an agent that ran the lane at eight workers nine times: (1) the metering case "keeps the audio workspace stable during meter-only ticks" read the canvas a third time after its poll had seen it change, and a live meter can stand on its first pixels again for a frame (A → B → A in 3 of 80 probed loads) — the poll's own change is now the proof, and the render-count checks are unchanged; (2) fourteen win32 captures at 2560×1440 still showed Planning's header tab, left within tolerance by Slice 1 (634–700 of the 800 pixels allowed), so the stage grid's render noise (about 240 px) now and then carried a lighting board over — they are refreshed: eleven visual-review boards (lighting populated, empty and DMX unreachable; audio populated, state assumed, not verified, offline and action failed; lighting and audio populated in bone; audio populated in graphite) and three Storybook shell stories (lighting populated, audio populated, audio state assumed); each new capture differs from its old one only in the header's tab strip, plus the stage grid's anti-aliasing on the three lighting ones, all inspected. Their linux counterparts carry the same tab within tolerance and have not failed on CI; they are refreshed with the next slice that moves boards (S3; S2b, named here first, moves none — its **Rescope:** says why), from a run whose comparison is made to fail.

Not in this slice: the ~32 s each `tauri dev` launch costs in the lanes (ten a run, eleven shells with the second copy launched directly; the cause is unproven — a CI-only experiment first); `--no-install-recommends` for the Tauri packages, splitting `qualification` and sharding Playwright (worth it only once `qualification` is shorter than `frontend-e2e`); a docs-only fast path (rare once records ride in the next slice); debuginfo off in CI builds (unmeasured); a Dev Drive or Defender exclusions (the verifier could not confirm the gain; a security change on the production machine, the operator's decision, not proposed). Rejected by the verifier or unavailable: running the qualification lanes on this workstation (unsafe — see Hardware safety above), a CI runner on this PC (the repository is public: pull requests from forks would run code on the studio machine), merge queue and larger runners (not offered for a repository a person owns).

Gates before: `dev:check` = twelve steps one after another; CI's `frontend-test` ran `frontend:test` then `frontend:test:coverage`; `rust` ran `native:check`; `qualification` needed `tauri-foundation`; `dev-checks` ran on `pull_request` and on pushes to every branch but Dependabot's; the Rust jobs installed the newest stable beside the project's 1.95.0; local Playwright ran 3 workers.

Tests added: `scripts/tauri-shell-running.test.mjs` (4: the Windows rule, the group rule with an injected signal, and two with real process groups that run on Linux — one killed by SIGTERM reads as gone although its leader's `exitCode` stays `null`, which the old check could never see; one whose member outlives the leader reads as running until the group is killed; both passed on CI's Linux in run 36176953914); `scripts/dev-check.test.mjs` (8: side by side and in order; a failure stops nothing; an unknown prerequisite is refused; the steps are the npm scripts `dev:check:serial` runs in an order that honours every `after`; the cargo chain and the staging readers; the cache places and their key; the failure report; the command line runs — through a directory junction too, where the first version's "am I the main module?" check was false and the gate passed without running anything); `scripts/frontend-test-gate.test.mjs` (2); `scripts/ci-workflow.test.mjs` (5: push only; the ten required checks are the jobs; the project's Rust alone and caches saved from `main`; qualification on its own cache without `needs`; the tests run once and `native:check` is left to clippy — four of the five fail against `main`'s workflow at `23a2f9a`, the fifth is a guard). The shell's `stop_with_grace` test keeps its assertions with the new helper; a piped `cargo test -p sse-exed-tauri-shell` took 29 s with the old helper and 3 s with the new one, with no `ping` left behind.

Tests changed: `frontend/app/tests/audio-metering.spec.ts` "keeps the audio workspace stable during meter-only ticks": a poll that the canvas checksum changes, then a separate read that it differs → the poll alone → a live meter can repaint back to the first read's pixels (the trace of the failure shows the poll passing and the read after it matching). Fourteen captures refreshed (above).

Review (2026-09-25, pushed at `eeb0017`, the review started with the CI run: five reviewers in parallel — the CI's GitHub semantics, the lanes' close, the parallel `dev:check`, the records, safety — and a skeptic for every finding): 25 findings, 23 upheld (one medium), 2 refuted. The medium one: pinning 1.95.0 did not keep the cache key stable, because rust-cache hashes every installed toolchain and the image's stable stayed — now the setup action removes it. Among the low ones: the "am I the main module?" check (above); a failed `protocol:check` skipped four steps for no reason; a start error was thrown away; the prettier cache was deleted by every prettier run without `--cache`, and the ESLint cache did not notice a plugin upgraded within its range; a failed coverage step's tail could hide an app failure; below-normal priority can starve a timing assertion when another build runs at the normal one (now said, with `dev:check:serial` as the re-run); the lanes still returned early on the npm leader's exit code; the ledger said twelve launches (ten), called the Dev Drive rejected (it was unverified), still allowed the qualification lanes on the workstation, still had `Tracking:` cut every branch after the previous merge, and SF's `Status:` was stale when pushed; stale text in DEVELOPER_QUICKSTART, README, HANDOFF and the pull-request template (the old triggers, `native:check`); the qualification cache described as holding the shell; a fork's pull request cannot merge without its head pushed here. Refuted: that the recorded "about 20 s" is unsupported (19.9 s, measured); that the rulings add a rule the operator did not choose.

Validation (workstation, 2026-09-25, after the review's corrections): `npm run dev:check` passed in 16.2 s and 16.5 s on an idle machine (56 s before, 76 s piped); the full Playwright lane at eight workers three times in a row, 95–96 s each, 378 passed and exactly the 13 known failures each time — the win32 captures at other sizes the ruling leaves stale (setup-ready and lighting-populated at 1280, 1440, 1600, 1728 and 1920, protocol-mismatch at 1920, and the studio preview's two at 1512×982); the agent's runs: the changed metering case 20 of 20 at eight workers, its old form 100 of 100 alone (the race shows under full-suite load), the Storybook lighting case 40 of 40; `scripts/ci-workflow.test.mjs` fails 4 of 5 against `main`'s workflow; `node scripts/dev-check-cli.mjs --list` through a directory junction lists all ten steps; `cargo test -p sse-exed-tauri-shell` 20 of 20. The engine is unchanged, so no `native:acceptance` run locally (CI's `rust` job runs it). CI: run 36176953914 on `eeb0017` (above); the corrections' run is recorded with the landing.

## Slice 2b — The db.json import retired

Status: landed (operator verification pending — the packaged, installer, delivery and bridge lanes it rewrote run at the close-out's build). Landed by #215, squash `f3b588c` at 01:47 UTC on 2026-09-26, on the operator's go-ahead given at the push ("Yes, merge when green"); the five commits under the tag `archive/new-pages-s2b-2026-09` (`aca5765`), whose tree `git diff` finds identical to `f3b588c`. The runs: `e80b5c7` 36187829117, `c549e9e` 36208347360 (8.2 minutes — `qualification` on the cache `main` saved after Slice SF) and `aca5765` 36209017984, each all ten jobs green on the first attempt; the merge was first held by CodeQL's review conversations (the Review paragraph below) until `aca5765` fixed the alerts and the conversations resolved themselves. `main`'s run on `f3b588c`, 36209592363, all ten green in 8.6 minutes. Committed as `bd41eb4`, rebased onto `main` (`7fb6f4d`) as `e80b5c7` with an identical tree, pushed on 2026-09-25 with pull request #215 opened at the push; its run 36187829117 was all ten jobs green on the first attempt — the qualification lanes' hardened launches, the step-8 waiver included, proved on CI. The Playwright lane showed no board moving, so no capture is refreshed here. Split from S2 on 2026-09-25 (S2's **Rescope:**). Worked on 2026-09-25 on branch `new-pages/s2b-db-json-import-retired`, cut from `new-pages/sf-faster-checks` at `f8e736b` while SF's pull request was open (Tracking: the next slice may be started locally). SF landed by #214 as `7fb6f4d`, whose tree `git diff` finds identical to `f8e736b`, so the branch is rebased with `git rebase --onto origin/main archive/new-pages-sf-2026-09 new-pages/s2b-db-json-import-retired` before its push. A read-only survey listed every site first (file and line, an order, the operator's questions); the edits then ran as one workflow of agents, each in files of its own — the lanes, the development parity fixtures, the fixture double, the hardware link with the contract — and a last one for the docs and this record.

**Rescope:** six differences from the scope below, the title unchanged because what landed is what it says. (1) The seeding: the scope said a fresh app-data, then `commissioning.update` to the stage each lane needs; what landed saves the page the lanes follow with `settings.update` (`seedSavedWorkspace`) and publishes only where a lane needs the dashboard, with `commissioning.update { stage: "ready", overrideProbes: true }` (`publishWithOverride`) — the saved page is what the lanes follow through their restarts and restores since S2, and no lane host can pass the probes, so a publish needs the override. (2) Every lane is hardened, not only the seven that used the import: the two Tauri qualification lanes, both packaging smokes and the installer's first-launch check too, and the engine's end-to-end tests — the operator's decision ("Yes, harden them (Recommended)"). (3) The parity fixtures write their settings directly, as planned, and also get a merge gate of their own (a completed setup only; the import's "earlier import" rule is gone), and the lighting payload stays, as `parity-lighting-populated.json` read by `lighting/parity_lighting.rs`: the scope deleted every `*-db.json` fixture, but that one also carried the rig `lighting-populated` loads. (4) Restore sources: the scope refused a `db.json` by name; what landed also recognises one by its content, and refuses any other JSON that is not a backup archive, because the survey found that a stray `{}` restored through the import reset the setup (Found, fixed). (5) The front end: the fixture double refuses a `db.json` in the same words, and the fixtures' `restoreSummary` sentence is fixed (the operator: "In S2b (Recommended)"); the scope named no front-end change. No board moves: the sentence shows only on Setup / Support's Support screen, which no fixture opens, so Slice SF's note that S2b moves the Support boards (and carries the linux capture refresh) was wrong — corrected there. (6) A left-over `db.json` at a start gets one warning line (the operator: "Ignore it, log one line (Recommended)"); the scope did not say.

Scope (D3): `native/rust-engine/src/legacy_import.rs`, the staged auto-import in `bootstrap.rs` and `SSE_LEGACY_DB_PATH`, the contract's `storage.importLegacyDb`, a `db.json` among the restore sources (refused by name from then on), the fixtures `native/rust-engine/fixtures/*-db.json` that only fed it, the end-to-end tests of the auto-import, and AGENTS.md's "Retained legacy surface". The seven users are seeded through the app's own requests instead (a fresh app-data, then `commissioning.update` to the stage each lane needs, the way the qualification lanes publish): `scripts/native-acceptance.mjs`, `native-control-surface-qualification.mjs`, `native-package.mjs` (the `dashboard` smoke), `native-packaged-acceptance.mjs`, `native-installer-acceptance.mjs`, `native-delivery-acceptance.mjs` and `scripts/legacy/tauri-package-candidate.mjs`; the development parity fixtures (`parity_fixtures.rs`) write their settings directly. Validation: as S2; the packaging, installer and delivery lanes cannot run on the workstation while the live app runs from `release\native\windows`, so they are checked by reading and run at the close-out's build with the operator's go-ahead.

Decisions (2026-09-25, asked while SF's CI and review ran). The operator's: the removal of `storage.importLegacyDb` goes into protocol 2, which has not shipped — no bump to 3 ("Into protocol 2 (Recommended)"); after this slice a left-over staged `import/db.json` or a set `SSE_LEGACY_DB_PATH` is ignored, with one warning line in the log and nothing imported ("Ignore it, log one line (Recommended)"); every lane is hardened — a reserved scratch bridge port (never 38201), `SSE_SAFE_START=1` and `SSE_AUDIO_SIMULATED_INPUT_MODE=1`, the workstation-only live console lane (`SSE_NATIVE_ACCEPTANCE_LIVE_CONSOLE=1`) keeping its opt-in as the only exception for the console ("Yes, harden them (Recommended)"); the fixtures' `restoreSummary` sentence ("…or a legacy db.json export.") is fixed in this slice ("In S2b (Recommended)"). The assistant's, the operator told: the three `legacy_import.*` keys an earlier import left in the operator's saved data stay, unread (no schema step — a schema 9 to remove three inert rows is not worth an upgrade); the wording below; the old candidate packager's `dashboard` scenario is re-seeded, not dropped; the installer and delivery lanes' publish without `overrideProbes` is a found bug, fixed here. The wording, the same in the hardware link and the fixture double: Verify and Restore of a `db.json` — "<file name> is an export from the old Studio Control (db.json); this version no longer restores those. Restore a backup archive or a database backup instead." (Verify answers `ok: false` with it; Restore refuses with `INVALID_PARAMS` before writing anything, a rollback archive included); the start-up line — "A db.json at <path> was left alone: Studio Control no longer imports db.json files."

What landed:

- **The hardware link.** `legacy_import.rs` (264 lines) and its `mod` line are deleted. `storage.rs` loses `import_legacy_db`, its gate (`legacy_import_finds_saved_data`, `legacy_import_would_replace`), `write_imported_settings` and `LEGACY_IMPORT_IMPORTED_AT_KEY`; `app.rs` loses the method's arm, so `storage.importLegacyDb` answers `UNKNOWN_METHOD`; `action_log.rs` drops it from `NOT_AN_ACTION_UI_METHODS` (the count in `every_contract_method_is_classified` is worked out from the lists). `bootstrap.rs`: the start-up import and `SSE_DISABLE_AUTO_IMPORT` go; each start asks `left_over_db_json_from` — the file `SSE_LEGACY_DB_PATH` names and `<app-data>/import/db.json`, each only when a file is there, the same file once, never the working directory — and `warn_about_a_left_over_db_json` writes the one `WARN` line and reads nothing; the "No legacy db.json source discovered" `INFO` line a start on saved data without a completed setup wrote is gone. `support.rs`: one `parse_support_archive` for Verify and Restore — a support archive as before; an export from the old Studio Control (a name ending in `db.json`, or a `projects` list beside a numeric `schemaVersion`) refused with the shared sentence, Verify with no format or schema number; any other JSON with Verify's existing "<path> is not a Studio Control backup archive."; a file that is not JSON with "<path> is not a JSON backup: <error>" — every refusal before the rollback archive is written. No schema step: the saved data stays at 8.
- **The contract.** `storage.importLegacyDb` leaves `v1.contract.json` (`protocol:generate`: one line each in the schema and `protocol.ts`), inside protocol 2; `tests/contract.rs` refuses any `storage.import*` method. `v1.md`: the method list and a paragraph on its removal; Verify's `db.json` and non-archive sentences; Restore's sources, and `sourceFormat` without `legacy-db-json`; "A left-over `db.json`" in place of the import and "Startup import behavior"; the three import error codes marked retired; `dev.parityFixture.load`'s gate and `sourcePath`; the Versioning note (protocol 2 also removes the method).
- **The fixtures.** `commissioning-sample-db.json`, `dashboard-ready-db.json` and the four `parity-*-db.json` are deleted; `native/rust-engine/fixtures/parity-lighting-populated.json` is new and holds only the rig (lights, groups, scenes, lighting settings — 8 lights, 4 groups, 3 scenes, bridge `2.0.0.1`, light output disabled, as before).
- **The development parity fixtures** (`parity_fixtures.rs`). A load writes, in one `set_settings_owned` transaction (three before), the setup flag as the three commissioning keys, the page, the fixture's own settings and, for `lighting-populated`, its rig — the values the import wrote, read from the old files: `lighting-populated` completed on Lighting; `audio-populated` and `setup-ready` completed on the Console; `setup-required` not completed, runner at `import`, on the Console. `targetSurface` follows the setup flag. The merge gate is new (F04): without `replaceExistingData` a load over a completed setup answers `INVALID_PARAMS`, "The saved data already holds a completed setup. Load the fixture over it with replaceExistingData: true.", before anything is written, the record file included; an earlier load no longer counts by itself.
