The reply's `sourcePath` names `<app-data>/parity-fixture-<id>.json`, a record `{ fixtureId, settings }` of every setting the load wrote (it named the copied db.json). Loads no longer write the `legacy_import.*` keys. `lighting/legacy_import.rs` is now `lighting/parity_lighting.rs`: `parity_lighting_settings(json)` returns the settings instead of writing them, with the conversion unchanged, and every wire struct refuses a field it does not read (`deny_unknown_fields`).
- **The fixture double and the fixtures.** `setupRequests.ts` refuses a `db.json` with the shared sentence (`oldStudioControlExportRefusal`) — recognised by its name, since the double cannot read the file — at Verify (`ok: false`) and at Restore, which throws before any state change, event, action-log row or rollback entry; the Tauri transport also passes on only the message, so the window gets the sentence the hardware link's `INVALID_PARAMS` carries. The legacy branch (`settingsRestored` 4, `legacy-db-json`) is gone. `state.ts` `findFixtureBackup` no longer lets an unlisted `*db.json` through: like the hardware link it answers "Backup file was not found: <path>". `fixtures.json`: `restoreSummary` in `setup-required`, `setup-ready` and `setup-degraded` is the hardware link's own sentence (`support.rs`), "Restore a backup archive or a database backup from the backups folder. A rollback backup is written first; a database backup takes effect once Studio Control has restarted its hardware link.", which the double already used as its default. It shows only in Support mode (`SetupSupportScreen`), which opens only when `shell.setup.activeSection` is `support`; no fixture sets it, so every board opens Setup on the runner, and the recovery fixtures carry no support data.
- **The lanes.** `native-parity-acceptance.mjs` gains `seedSavedWorkspace` (fresh saved data opens on the Console, then `settings.update` saves Lighting) and `publishWithOverride` (the publish, the dashboard asserted); `IMPORTED_WORKSPACE` becomes `SEEDED_WORKSPACE` (`lighting`) beside a new `NEW_DATA_WORKSPACE` (`audio`). `native:acceptance`, the bridge lane and the packaged, installer and delivery lanes start on fresh saved data (the last three's first shell smoke still expects commissioning) and seed through the helpers; the packaging smoke's and the old candidate's `dashboard` scenario publish through an `EngineHarness` on the packaged engine and the smoke's app data before the shell smoke runs. The hardening lives in `native-runtime-harness.mjs`: `reserveLocalPort` (moved from the bridge lane; never 38201), `hardenedLaneEnv`, `laneEnvRefusal` (read as the hardware link reads each variable — a port it cannot parse falls back to 38201, so it is refused), `assertHardenedLaneEnv` and `laneProcessEnv`, through which every engine (`EngineHarness.start()`, before it looks for the executable or makes a folder) and every shell a lane starts gets its environment; `acceptanceEngineEnv` is hardened too, and the live console lane keeps the real console with the reserved port and the safe start. The one waiver is step 8 of the Setup/Support Tauri lane, whose second launch passes `SSE_SAFE_START=0` (`envCheck: { safeStart: false }`) to prove that a hold outlives the launch that made it; its runtime has no lighting bridge address, so the arming sends nothing. The installer lane's install and reinstall run QtIFW's first-launch check with `installerRunEnv` (Found, fixed). The engine's end-to-end tests start their engines with `SSE_CONTROL_SURFACE_PORT=0` (the system picks), `SSE_SAFE_START=1` and the simulated console. The Tauri lanes' comments about a live TotalMix now say the simulated console.
- **Docs.** `v1.md` (above); AGENTS (the Retained legacy surface section goes, and the lanes' hardening is named under `native:acceptance`), README, SECURITY (the importer's line in the scope), `.env.example`, ARCHITECTURE, OPERATIONS ("Saved data looks wrong or missing", the restore path, the start-up line under Logs), DEVELOPMENT (the lanes' seeding and hardening, the parity fixtures), HANDOFF, `native/README.md`, `scripts/README.md` (the lanes' guards), CHANGELOG (Removed); this ledger, and SF's note on the linux captures (a cross-reference to this slice).

- **The Tauri smoke** (`scripts/tauri-smoke.mjs`, `tauri:foundation`'s last step, CI's `tauri-foundation` job): its engines go through `laneProcessEnv` like every other lane's, and the guard lists it ("every app a lane starts gets its scratch folders and the hardening check in one place" fails on the old script: `…eBinaryPath, { env: { ...process.env, SSE_APP_DATA_DIR: appDataDir, …`). Found by the lanes agent, done by the assistant.

Gates before: the import's own tests — `legacy_import.rs` (3), `storage/tests.rs` (2), `storage/tests_schema_8.rs` (1), `bootstrap.rs` (4: the source order, the decoy, the gate, `SSE_DISABLE_AUTO_IMPORT`), `support/tests.rs`'s `db.json` restore and the `db.json` part of its Verify case, and the end-to-end `auto_import_ignores_cwd` and `auto_import_reads_the_staged_app_data_file`; `parity_fixtures`' `the_bundled_fixtures_carry_no_planning`; `native-lanes.test.mjs`'s `db.json` fixture case; the lanes' saved-page checks after the import. `tests/contract.rs` held the contract and the engine together but not "no import"; nothing tested a restore of a JSON file that is neither an archive nor a `db.json`, and the fixture double's `db.json` restore had no test. The lanes' safety was each lane's own: only the bridge lane chose its bridge port, only the acceptance engines (`native:acceptance` and the packaged lane) ran the simulated console, only step 8 of the Setup/Support lane set `SSE_SAFE_START`, `EngineHarness.start()` checked nothing, and the end-to-end engines set only `SSE_DISABLE_AUTO_IMPORT`.

Tests added — 18, each proved against the code before this slice (the branch's base, `f8e736b`) or a sabotaged copy restored byte for byte (`cmp` or sha256), unless marked a regression guard:

- `app/tests.rs` `the_db_json_import_is_gone_from_the_requests`: `storage.importLegacyDb` answers `UNKNOWN_METHOD` with and without `force` and raises no event; a restore of `backups/db.json` answers `INVALID_PARAMS` with the shared sentence, raises no event, leaves the settings and writes no rollback archive. At `f8e736b` (its `native/` exported with `git archive`, the new tests copied in, built in a target of its own) it fails at `!reply.response.ok` — the old hardware link imported the file; with a copy of `support.rs` that writes the rollback archive before reading the file it fails at the backups listing.
- `support/tests.rs` `an_old_studio_control_export_is_refused_by_name_and_writes_nothing`: five files — `legacy-db.json`, `db.json`, an old export renamed (recognised by its content), `stray-db.json` holding `{}` (by its name), `broken-db.json` that is not JSON — each gets Verify `ok: false` with no format or schema number and the sentence naming the file, and Restore `InvalidParams` with the same sentence; the settings rows with their `updated_at`, the backups folder and the file are unchanged, and no rollback archive is written. At `f8e736b` Verify answered `ok: true` ("Legacy db.json export (schema 9); …"); the early-rollback copy fails at the backups listing.
- `support/tests.rs` `a_restore_of_json_that_is_not_a_backup_archive_changes_nothing`: `{}`, `[]`, a settings-only object and an object with a format version but no `archiveType` — Restore `InvalidParams` "<path> is not a Studio Control backup archive.", Verify the same sentence; no setting or file changes and the setup stays complete. At `f8e736b` `stray.json` was restored as `legacy-db-json` with a rollback archive and 4 settings (Found, fixed); the early-rollback copy fails too.
- `bootstrap.rs` `a_left_over_db_json_is_named_once_and_never_read`: a `data/db.json` decoy is no source; the staged file is found even with `SSE_DISABLE_AUTO_IMPORT=1`; a variable naming no file names nothing; with both files there the one line names both (the review's case); exactly one `WARN` line, ending in the agreed words; the settings unchanged and the file byte-identical. Its two functions are new, so it cannot compile at `f8e736b`; it fails on copies of `bootstrap.rs` with the warning a no-op (no line) and with the source obeying `SSE_DISABLE_AUTO_IMPORT` again.
- `tests/end_to_end.rs` `a_left_over_db_json_is_named_in_the_log_and_never_imported`: a real engine with a staged `import/db.json`, `SSE_LEGACY_DB_PATH` naming a second and a third at `<working dir>/data/db.json` — the setup not complete, the page `audio`, all three files unchanged, and exactly one log line naming a `db.json`, a `WARN` naming both the variable's file and the staged one. At `f8e736b` it fails at "no db.json may be imported" (the old engine imported the file into its scratch folder); with the warning a no-op it fails at the line count.
- `tests/contract.rs` `the_contract_has_no_db_json_import`: fails on `f8e736b`'s contract (`["storage.importLegacyDb"]`); a planted contract shows the guard finds the method.
- `parity_fixtures.rs` `a_load_onto_a_completed_setup_needs_replace_existing_data`: two `setup-required` loads in a row go through; over a completed setup a `lighting-populated` load without the flag is refused with the new sentence, and every setting and every file of the folder is unchanged; with `replaceExistingData: true` it loads (Lighting, the record written). On `f8e736b`'s load (through a small adapter, with the old fixtures and importer swapped in) it fails at the second `setup-required` load — the old gate counted an earlier import — and, with one earlier load, at "a refused load writes no file" (the old code copied the `db.json` in before refusing). It fails on three sabotaged copies: the gate disabled, the record written before the gate, the gate counting an existing record.
- `lighting/parity_lighting.rs` `a_field_the_payload_reader_does_not_read_is_refused`: one plain light loads; eight payloads with a field some wire struct does not read (`schemaVersion`, `settings`, a light's `red`, a group's `colorIndex`, a scene's `createdAt`, a scene light's `gmTint`, an effect's `depth`, `lightingSettings.sacnPriority`, and since the review `cameraMarker.z` and `subjectMarker.label`) are refused, ten in all, and a payload with both markers writes exactly their `x`, `y` and `rotation`. It fails with all the `deny_unknown_fields` removed (the old reader had none) and with each removed alone, at that struct's own case.
- `setupRequests.test.ts` "the fixture double and an export from the old Studio Control (db.json)" (5 since the review, which added "refuses Verify and Restore in the hardware link's own words, with the file's own name" and made every case read the two sentences from `support.rs` instead of copies — a one-character change on either side fails it): "lists it, and Verify says it is not restored any more" — before, Verify answered `ok: true` ("Backup archive, format 4, …"); "refuses to restore it and writes nothing, a rollback backup included" — before, the restore resolved as `legacy-db-json`, and a copy that throws only after its writes and events fails the before/after comparison; "answers a db.json that is not in the backups folder as any file not found" — before, an unlisted `<backupDir>/db.json` verified `ok: true`; "gives every scenario the hardware link's restore sentence, which names no db.json" — all 34 fixture ids (the 22 scenarios and the 12 derived from them); it fails on the old `fixtures.json` at `setup-required`.
- `scripts/native-lanes.test.mjs` "every publish a lane sends carries the probe override a fresh hardware link needs": fails on the old lane scripts (the installer's and the delivery lane's `{ stage: "ready" }`) and on a copy of the new tree whose `publishWithOverride` drops the override; a self-check covers its scanner (a `}` inside a string, params it cannot read).
- `scripts/native-lanes.test.mjs` "no lane names the retired db.json import, its variables or its fixtures": fails on the old scripts with 29 hits (`SSE_LEGACY_DB_PATH`, `SSE_DISABLE_AUTO_IMPORT`, the two fixtures, the fixtures path) in nine scripts.
- `scripts/native-lanes.test.mjs` "the lanes' hardening: a bridge port of their own, the light outputs held, the simulated console": with the old harness the file does not load (the helpers are new); it fails on copies whose `hardenedLaneEnv` drops `SSE_SAFE_START` and whose `laneEnvRefusal` accepts 38201 or the real console.
- `scripts/native-lanes.test.mjs` "the harness starts no engine without the lanes' hardening, and checks before it looks or makes anything": fails on a copy whose `EngineHarness.start()` skips the check, as the old one did (it went on to "executable not found" and made the folders); it starts no process.
- `scripts/native-lanes.test.mjs` "every app a lane starts gets its scratch folders and the hardening check in one place": fails on the old scripts (eight app-data hand-overs outside `laneProcessEnv`: the packaging smoke, the packaged, installer and delivery lanes, the old candidate, the Setup/Support lane twice, the workspace lane), on a copy with a second `safeStart: false`, and on a copy whose delivery smoke builds its environment the old way.
- `scripts/native-lanes.test.mjs` "the page a lane seeds can be seen: new saved data opens on another, and the restore's marker differs from it": a regression guard — it carries the deleted case's `IMPORTED_WORKSPACE !== DEFAULT_WORKSPACE` forward (it held before) and ties `NEW_DATA_WORKSPACE` to `shell_settings.rs`'s `DEFAULT_WORKSPACE`; it fails on a copy with `SEEDED_WORKSPACE = "audio"`.

Tests changed (old → new → reason), 32 entries; no front-end test changed.

- `legacy_import.rs` `parse_import_request_requires_path`: DELETED with the module — `parse_import_request({})` refused with "path is required and must be a non-empty string" → none. _Why:_ `storage.importLegacyDb`, whose request it parsed, is gone (D3).
- `legacy_import.rs` `a_planning_board_opens_the_console`: DELETED — a `dashboardView` of `kanban`, `planning`, empty or `audio` opened the Console and `lighting` (trimmed) Lighting, with runner `import`, stage `setup-required` and not completed, and `hasCompletedSetup: true` gave completed, `publish`, `ready` → none. _Why:_ nothing maps a `db.json`'s settings any more; the parity fixtures write their page and setup flag themselves (`each_fixture_writes_its_setup_and_page`).
- `legacy_import.rs` `the_planning_part_of_a_db_json_is_ignored`: DELETED — a `db.json` with a malformed Planning part loaded (schema 8, Lighting, completed, `ready`) → none. _Why:_ nothing reads a `db.json`.
- `storage/tests.rs` `import_legacy_db_writes_only_the_setup_flag_and_the_page`: DELETED — the import wrote exactly four settings (Lighting, completed, `ready`, `publish`), its reply had four keys and nothing else changed → none. The module doc no longer lists the import, and `WORKSPACE_KEY` and `SystemTime` / `UNIX_EPOCH`, which came in through `storage.rs`'s imports, are imported directly. _Why:_ `import_legacy_db` is removed.
- `storage/tests.rs` `import_legacy_db_requires_force_when_data_already_exists`: DELETED — a second import without `force` gave `ExistingDataRequiresForce` → none. _Why:_ the import and its gate are removed.
- `storage/tests_schema_8.rs` `import_legacy_db_counts_a_completed_setup_or_an_earlier_import_as_saved_data`: DELETED — `legacy_import_finds_saved_data` false on fresh data and true after an import or on a set-up desk, a refused import wrote nothing, `force` replaced → none; the module doc follows, an unused `json` import goes and `WORKSPACE_KEY` is imported directly. _Why:_ the gate and the import are removed; the schema-8 step and its test that keeps the three `legacy_import.*` keys are unchanged.
- `bootstrap.rs` `legacy_import_source_prefers_the_explicit_path`: DELETED — with a staged file and `SSE_LEGACY_DB_PATH` set, the source was the variable's → `a_left_over_db_json_is_named_once_and_never_read` keeps the order, and the file is only named. _Why:_ the start-up import is retired; a left-over gets one line and nothing is imported (the operator's decision).
- `bootstrap.rs` `legacy_import_source_reads_only_the_staged_app_data_file`: DELETED — a `data/db.json` decoy was no source, and a staged `import/db.json` was → the new test keeps the decoy rule. _Why:_ as above.
- `bootstrap.rs` `the_start_up_import_never_replaces_saved_data`: DELETED — new saved data took the staged import once (Lighting, completed), a second start with a changed file changed nothing, and a set-up desk did not look for a source → the new test: no start reads the file; the settings are unchanged and the file byte-identical. _Why:_ as above.
- `bootstrap.rs` `legacy_import_source_is_disabled_by_env`: DELETED — `SSE_DISABLE_AUTO_IMPORT=1` gave no source even with `SSE_LEGACY_DB_PATH` set → the new test: `SSE_DISABLE_AUTO_IMPORT=1` no longer hides the staged file, which is named. _Why:_ the variable is no longer read.
- `support/tests.rs` `restore_support_backup_accepts_legacy_json`: DELETED — the restore of `backups/legacy-db.json` succeeded with `sourceFormat` `legacy-db-json`, `settingsRestored` 4, `detail` `PLANNING_WAS_NOT_RESTORED` and the setup complete → `an_old_studio_control_export_is_refused_by_name_and_writes_nothing`. _Why:_ a `db.json` is no longer a restore source (D3).
- `support/tests.rs` `verify_says_a_format_4_archive_s_planning_part_is_skipped` (its `db.json` part): `checked.ok`, `schema_version == Some(9)`, detail "Legacy db.json export (schema 9); only whether setup is complete and the page to open are restored from it. {PLANNING_WILL_NOT_BE_RESTORED}" → `!checked.ok`, `schema_version == None`, detail "legacy-db.json is an export from the old Studio Control (db.json); this version no longer restores those. Restore a backup archive or a database backup instead."; the operator-words check is kept and the comment above the test reworded. _Why:_ Verify refuses a `db.json` by name.
- `support/tests.rs` `export_support_backup_writes_archive_and_lists_it`: seeded by importing `seed_legacy_payload`'s `db.json` with `force` → by `seed_completed_setup`, which writes the four settings that import wrote (completed, `publish`, `ready`, the Console); no assertion changed. _Why:_ the import is gone; the seeded data lacks only the three `legacy_import.*` keys, which the test does not read.
- `support/tests.rs` `restore_support_backup_round_trips_native_archive`: the same seed change; no assertion changed. _Why:_ as above.
- `support/tests.rs` `export_support_backup_records_storage_format_version`: the same seed change; no assertion changed. _Why:_ as above.
- `support/tests.rs` `restore_support_backup_accepts_format_v2_archive_without_storage_version`: the same seed change; no assertion changed. _Why:_ as above.
- `support/tests.rs` the `seeded_runtime` helper, which seeds 13 tests (`a_format_4_archive_restores_everything_but_its_planning_part`, `a_new_archive_is_format_5_and_carries_no_planning`, `backup_times_are_milliseconds_since_the_epoch`, `database_backups_of_schema_7_and_8_restore_and_a_newer_one_is_refused`, `database_restore_stages_pending_file_and_keeps_rollback`, `list_includes_sqlite3`, `pre_restore_archives_are_capped_at_five`, `restore_never_changes_whether_the_light_outputs_are_armed`, `restore_refuses_newer_format`, `restore_rejects_path_outside_backups`, `restore_round_trips_all_prefixes`, `verify_flags_junk_file`, `verify_says_a_format_4_archive_s_planning_part_is_skipped`): the same seed change; no assertion changed. `seed_legacy_payload` is renamed `write_old_studio_control_export`, same content, and now feeds only the refusal tests; two helpers are added, `backup_file_names` and `settings_rows`. _Why:_ as above.
- `tests/end_to_end.rs` `auto_import_ignores_cwd`: DELETED — a `<working dir>/data/db.json` was not imported (the setup not complete) → `a_left_over_db_json_is_named_in_the_log_and_never_imported`, whose third file sits there and stays untouched. _Why:_ the auto-import is retired.
- `tests/end_to_end.rs` `auto_import_reads_the_staged_app_data_file` and its `legacy_fixture_path` helper: DELETED — a staged `<app-data>/import/db.json` (`dashboard-ready-db.json`) was imported (the setup complete) → the new test: the staged file is left alone and the setup stays not complete. _Why:_ the auto-import is retired, and the fixture it read is deleted.
- `tests/end_to_end.rs` `EngineProcess::spawn` / `spawn_with`, which start every end-to-end engine (4 tests): set `SSE_DISABLE_AUTO_IMPORT=1` and no other variable of this kind → no `SSE_DISABLE_AUTO_IMPORT`; `SSE_CONTROL_SURFACE_PORT=0`, `SSE_SAFE_START=1`, `SSE_AUDIO_SIMULATED_INPUT_MODE=1`; no assertion changed, the module doc says "seed" for "import". _Why:_ the variable is gone; the hardening follows the operator's decision — never the live app's 38201, never the default console setup (sending to 127.0.0.1:7001, listening on 9001).
- `tests/end_to_end.rs` `second_engine_on_the_same_app_data_dir_is_refused`: its second and third engines set `SSE_DISABLE_AUTO_IMPORT=1` → not set; no assertion changed. _Why:_ the variable is gone.
- `parity_fixtures.rs` `the_bundled_fixtures_carry_no_planning` → renamed `each_fixture_writes_its_setup_and_page`: parsed the four bundled `db.json` payloads — no `projects`, `tasks` or `activityLog`, settings keys exactly `[dashboardView, hasCompletedSetup]`, `dashboardView` `lighting` for `lighting-populated` and `audio` otherwise → loads each fixture onto a fresh database of its own and checks, from a table, the three commissioning keys and the page, the reply's `fixtureId` and `targetSurface` (`dashboard`, or `commissioning` for `setup-required`), the `sourcePath` record (its `fixtureId`, page and setup flag), `lighting-populated`'s rig (8 fixtures, 4 groups, 3 scenes, `app.lighting.enabled` `false`), and that the other three leave every `app.lighting.*` setting as it was. _Why:_ three payloads are deleted and the setup flag and page are written directly; the one payload left cannot carry an unread part (`deny_unknown_fields` on eight wire structs, the markers' since the review). It fails with `setup_settings` empty (`lighting-populated`: "false" for "true"); on `f8e736b`'s code it fails only at the record checks and passes without them — the direct write matches what the import wrote.
- `scripts/native-lanes.test.mjs` "the db.json fixtures hold only what the interim import reads, and the sample's import can be seen": DELETED — `legacy_import.rs` renamed `schemaVersion`, `dashboardView` and `hasCompletedSetup`; both fixtures held exactly `[schemaVersion, settings]` and `[dashboardView, hasCompletedSetup]`, `hasCompletedSetup` false and true, `dashboardView` Lighting or the Console; the sample's page was `IMPORTED_WORKSPACE`, and `IMPORTED_WORKSPACE !== DEFAULT_WORKSPACE` → "no lane names the retired db.json import…" and "the page a lane seeds can be seen…" (`SEEDED_WORKSPACE !== NEW_DATA_WORKSPACE`, which is `DEFAULT_WORKSPACE`; `MOVED_WORKSPACE !== SEEDED_WORKSPACE`). _Why:_ the import, its reader and its fixtures are gone, and the page the lanes follow is saved with `settings.update`.
- `scripts/native-acceptance.mjs` steps 1 and 2 (and `native-parity-acceptance.mjs`'s constants and `acceptanceEngineEnv`): `SSE_LEGACY_DB_PATH=commissioning-sample-db.json`, then the page `lighting` asserted "after the import", an inline override publish asserting the dashboard, and step 2 with `SSE_DISABLE_AUTO_IMPORT=1` → fresh saved data (`targetSurface` `commissioning` kept); `seedSavedWorkspace` (the Console first, then Lighting in the `settings.update` reply); `publishWithOverride` (the same request and assertion); both engines on `await acceptanceEngineEnv()`, now hardened; the archive, restart and restore checks on `SEEDED_WORKSPACE` (still `lighting`). _Why:_ the import is retired; the seed goes through the app's own request, and the added check of the fresh page keeps the seed visible.
- `scripts/native-packaged-acceptance.mjs` steps 1–5: a first smoke `import` with `SSE_LEGACY_DB_PATH` expecting commissioning, the imported page asserted, an inline override publish, `SSE_DISABLE_AUTO_IMPORT` on the later steps → a first smoke `first-launch` on fresh saved data, still expecting commissioning; `seedSavedWorkspace` and `publishWithOverride` in step 2; every smoke and engine hardened; the restart and restore page checks keep `lighting`. _Why:_ the import is retired; the lanes are hardened.
- `scripts/native-installer-acceptance.mjs` the install and reinstall, step 2 and step 5: a smoke `installed-import` with `SSE_LEGACY_DB_PATH`, the imported page asserted, `commissioning.update { stage: "ready" }` without `overrideProbes` asserting the dashboard, install and reinstall with no app data of their own, `SSE_DISABLE_AUTO_IMPORT` elsewhere → a smoke `installed-first-launch` on fresh saved data expecting commissioning; `seedSavedWorkspace`; `publishWithOverride` (the dashboard asserted); install and reinstall with `installerRunEnv`; every smoke and engine hardened; the reinstall's page check keeps `lighting`. _Why:_ the import is retired; the publish without the override and the first-launch check's app data are found bugs (Found, fixed).
- `scripts/native-delivery-acceptance.mjs` steps 1–4: a smoke `install-import` with `SSE_LEGACY_DB_PATH`, the imported page asserted, a publish without `overrideProbes`, `SSE_DISABLE_AUTO_IMPORT` on the later smokes and engines → a smoke `install-first-launch` on fresh saved data; `seedSavedWorkspace`; `publishWithOverride`; every smoke and engine hardened; the update and reinstall page checks keep `lighting`. _Why:_ as the installer lane.
- `scripts/native-control-surface-qualification.mjs` step 1 and `bridge-http-routes`: the engine had `SSE_LEGACY_DB_PATH` and a port reserved in the lane, the deck context and the workspace LCD were compared with `IMPORTED_WORKSPACE`, and the summary carried `fixturePath` → the engine gets `hardenedLaneEnv()` (its reserved port becomes the expected bridge address; the safe start and the simulated console join), `seedSavedWorkspace` right after the start, the context and the LCD compared with `SEEDED_WORKSPACE` (still `lighting`); `fixturePath` goes, the `packaged-engine-start` step gains `scopeChanged`, and the `DECK_ROUTES_CHANGED` note is reworded. _Why:_ the import is retired; `reserveLocalPort` moved to the harness; the lane is hardened (it runs no audio probe, so with the simulated console it stays on its "not verified" branch).
- `scripts/native-package.mjs` the `dashboard` and `clean-start` smokes: `dashboard` set `SSE_LEGACY_DB_PATH=dashboard-ready-db.json` when the file existed and the packaging asserted it existed (twice); `clean-start` set `SSE_DISABLE_AUTO_IMPORT=1` → `dashboard` publishes first through an `EngineHarness` on the packaged engine and the smoke's app data (`publishWithOverride`), then runs the shell smoke, which still expects the dashboard; `clean-start` adds nothing; both smokes through `laneProcessEnv(await hardenedLaneEnv())`, and the smoke function is async. _Why:_ the import is retired; the lanes are hardened.
- `scripts/legacy/tauri-package-candidate.mjs` the `dashboard` and `clean-start` scenarios: `dashboard` asserted `dashboard-ready-db.json` existed and set `SSE_LEGACY_DB_PATH` to it; `clean-start` set `SSE_DISABLE_AUTO_IMPORT=1` → as the packaging smoke: a `publishWithOverride` seed on the packaged engine before the shell smoke, which still expects the dashboard; nothing for `clean-start`; both through `laneProcessEnv(await hardenedLaneEnv())`. _Why:_ the import is retired; the scenario is re-seeded, not dropped (the assistant's decision).
- `scripts/tauri-setup-support-qualification.mjs` every launch, and step 8's arm run: every launch — the second instance's binary included — set `SSE_DISABLE_AUTO_IMPORT=1` with no reserved port or simulated console, and the arm run set no `SSE_SAFE_START` → every launch through `laneProcessEnv(await hardenedLaneEnv())` (both launch functions async, every call awaited; the bridge-port step's own held port replaces the reserved one, as before); the arm run passes `SSE_SAFE_START=0` with `envCheck: { safeStart: false }`, the only waiver the guard allows; the comment on the armed row (already found by its action) is reworded. All assertions are unchanged. _Why:_ the lanes are hardened; the arm run must start without a safe start to keep proving that the hold outlives the launch that made it.
- `scripts/tauri-workspace-qualification.mjs` every launch: `SSE_DISABLE_AUTO_IMPORT=1`, no reserved port, safe start or simulated console → `laneProcessEnv(await hardenedLaneEnv())`, the launch function async and awaited; all assertions unchanged; the comments that asked for a live TotalMix now say the audio block runs against the simulated console when `SSE_TAURI_QUALIFICATION_SKIP_AUDIO_PROBE` is not set. _Why:_ the lanes are hardened.

Found, fixed:

- **The installer and delivery lanes could not publish.** They sent `commissioning.update { stage: "ready" }` without `overrideProbes`, which the hardware link refuses while a probe has not passed (`COMMISSIONING_PROBES_INCOMPLETE`, since the 2026-09 audit's Slice 8; `tests/end_to_end.rs` shows it), and no lane host runs the probes — both lanes, which run only at a release, would have stopped at that step. Now `publishWithOverride`; "every publish a lane sends carries the probe override…" fails on the old scripts.
- **A restore of a stray JSON file reset the setup.** Any `.json` in the backups folder that was not a support archive went to the import with `force`, after the rollback archive; with the import's defaults a `{}` wrote the setup as not complete, the runner back at `import` and the page as the Console — while Verify called the same file "not a Studio Control backup archive". Now Restore refuses it with Verify's sentence before anything is written; `a_restore_of_json_that_is_not_a_backup_archive_changes_nothing` fails at `f8e736b`.
- **The installer lane's first-launch check had no app data of its own.** QtIFW's install script runs `sse-exed-tauri-shell --smoke-test` (`native/installer-templates/tauri-installscript.qs`) with the lane's environment, which set no `SSE_APP_DATA_DIR`; on Windows that means `%APPDATA%\ExEd Studio Control Native` — on this workstation the live data, where the live app's lock would have refused it (the lane's own `HOME` does not move `%APPDATA%`). The install and reinstall steps now run with `installerRunEnv`: a scratch `installer-first-launch` folder under the lane's root, and the hardening.
- S2's Found, not changed — the acceptance engine set neither `SSE_CONTROL_SURFACE_PORT` nor `SSE_SAFE_START`, so on the workstation its bridge tried the live app's 38201 — is closed by the hardening.
- **Loading a release script did its work — the live app's folder was the target.** During this slice's review (2026-09-25) a reviewer agent loaded `scripts/native-package.mjs` to check its exports (`node -e "import(…)"`), against the brief's rule never to run a packaging script. The script did its work at load: on Windows it removes and rebuilds `release\native\windows`, the folder the live app runs from. The removal stopped at the running shell's locked executable; afterwards both executables there were byte-identical to the `98bbb06` build's recorded hashes (the production readiness ledger, After the program: shell `f565bf05…`, engine `f7796fc9…`), the zip and the rollback folders untouched, and the live app still held 38201 as PID 12268. The operator was told. Fixed at the root: 35 scripts under `scripts/` that did work, exited or started processes at load now do it only as the main module (`isMainModule`, a comparison of real paths that throws rather than skips when the file names match but the paths do not; `tauri-before-command.mjs` also accepts its two `native/` shims, the way `tauri dev` and `tauri build` start it), and `native-package.mjs` refuses, before it removes an existing output folder, while an executable inside it is running or anything answers on 127.0.0.1:38201, naming the keep-and-restore procedure. In a sandbox that refused every process start, network call, exit and outside write, the 31 scripts changed after the first four did something at load in their `HEAD` form (among them: `native-sign-windows.mjs` exited the loading process, or with a certificate set started signing the live app's shell; the old candidate packager deleted its output) and nothing in their new form. `scripts/native-lanes.test.mjs` holds it: every script under `scripts/` does nothing when loaded, every guarded script's check compares real paths, every importer of a guarded script outside `scripts/` is a test or an entry point its check names, and the packaging refusal comes before the removal. Every agent brief since names the scripts that must never be loaded or run in the repository.

Review (2026-09-25, started at the push of `e80b5c7`, with its CI run: six reviewers in parallel — the hardware link, the lanes, the fixtures and the double, the tests, the records, safety — and a skeptic for every finding): 24 findings, 22 upheld (all low), 2 refuted. Corrected in `Pages S2b (review): …`: a `db.json` that is not UTF-8 is refused by name too, and Restore answers any file it cannot decode with `INVALID_PARAMS` and Verify's sentence, keeping `STORAGE_ERROR` for a file that cannot be read (the backup is read as bytes; UTF-16 cases in the two refusal tests, which fail at `e80b5c7`); the start-up line names a file only when one is there, and both when both are (the variable pointing at a missing file named a path where nothing was, and hid the staged file); the lanes' check refuses a missing, relative or default app-data or log folder (compared as real paths, so a junction or a short 8.3 name cannot slip through), the installer lane's QtIFW runs build their environment themselves, and only `acceptanceEngineEnv` may leave the simulated console; the fixture double's two sentences are read from `support.rs` by its test instead of copied; the lighting payload's markers refuse a field they do not read; `native-package.mjs` does nothing when loaded and refuses to replace a running app's folder (Found, fixed above); the records (the counts, the Status lines, the start-up line's wording in OPERATIONS, `native/README.md` and CHANGELOG, RELEASE's packaged-acceptance sentence, the skip-audio-probe reason in `dev-checks.yml` and AGENTS, the double's name-only detection described as it is). Refuted: that the reserved bridge port's gap is wider for the Tauri lanes than recorded; that the live-console opt-in reaching the packaged lane breaks the recorded exception. The review of the load-time guards themselves (three reviewers and skeptics, 8 findings, 7 upheld) found that `tauri-before-command.mjs` refused to run through its `native/` shims — every `tauri dev` and `tauri build` would have failed, CI's `tauri-foundation` and `qualification` first — and four ways the guards could pass vacuously; all corrected before the push, with a test that fails if a shim is deleted or dropped. CodeQL on the pull request (at `c549e9e`) raised seven new alerts, as unresolved review conversations that `main`'s rule on conversations turns into a block: six "clear-text logging" — the lanes' refusal messages echoed values from the environment (a port, a path, a flag) into what a failed lane prints — and one incomplete escaping in the importer guard's name pattern. Fixed in `Pages S2b (review, 4 of 4): …`: a refusal names the variable and the rule, never its value, and the pattern escapes every special character.

Found, not changed: CI's `SSE_TAURI_QUALIFICATION_SKIP_AUDIO_PROBE=1` (`dev-checks.yml`) is no longer needed to keep the workspace lane off a TotalMix, since its shells run the simulated console — dropping it would run the audio block on CI against the simulation, a later slice's choice; the reserved bridge port is released before the engine binds it (the bridge lane's pattern before this slice), so another process could take it in between (38201 lay in Linux's ephemeral range too, so CI is no worse off); the old candidate packager leaves its temporary smoke runtime behind when the `dashboard` seed fails (the seed runs before its `try`; a spawn error did the same before); the hardware link recognises an old export by its name or its content, a support archive named `…db.json` passing as an archive, while the double goes by the name alone — it refuses every listed `…db.json`, a support archive so named included, and takes every other listed `.json` for an archive (it cannot read what a file holds; the scenarios list no such file, so no board or case depends on it); `support.snapshot` still lists a `db.json` in the backups folder as an archive, which Verify and Restore refuse; in recovery mode a `db.json` restore is refused first by the existing "The saved data could not be opened…" sentence, the storage check coming first (nothing is written either way); `v1.md` does not document `commissioning.update`'s `overrideProbes`, which every lane's publish now carries (older than this program); the fixture double's database Verify still says schema 6 (S2 found it); `docs/redesign/lighting-direction-d-implementation-plan.md` still names `lighting/legacy_import.rs` (a design record, left as history); comments in `app.rs`, the new app test and `tests/contract.rs` name `storage.importLegacyDb` to say why it is gone (no guard reads comments).

Validation so far (workstation, 2026-09-25, by the agents, before the assistant's own run): `npm run protocol:generate` and `protocol:check`; `npm run native:test` (shell 20; engine 414 passed, 1 ignored — 421 before, less the 11 import tests, plus 4; contract 7; end-to-end 4); `npm run native:test:dev-fixtures` (clippy with the feature clean; engine 420 passed, 1 ignored; contract 7; end-to-end 4; the plain engine rebuilt afterwards); `cargo test -p studio-control-engine --features dev-fixtures parity` 7 passed and without the feature 1; `console_confidence_has_one_writer`; `rust:clippy` and `rust:fmt:check` clean; `npm run native:engine:build && npm run native:acceptance` passed on the hardened lanes in a scratch folder ("seeding, restart, and rollback are deterministic") — the only engine process running afterwards was the live app's, as before; `node --test scripts/native-lanes.test.mjs` 9 of 9; the engine client's tests 122 (118 before, plus 4), its typecheck, `frontend:fixtures:check` (22 scenarios), `frontend:typecheck`, the app's Vitest 111; ESLint and Prettier on the changed files; `file:health` (the largest changed file, `support/tests.rs`, about 1,890 lines). Not run: the packaged, installer, delivery and bridge lanes, `native-package.mjs` and the old candidate (read, `node --check`ed and held by the guards; they run at the close-out's build), the Tauri qualification lanes (CI only, since Slice SF — their hardened launches are proved first by the branch's `qualification` job), and Playwright (the assistant's validation).

Validation (the assistant, workstation, 2026-09-25, after the Tauri smoke's hardening): `npm run dev:check` passed (10 steps, 18.2 s); `npm run native:test:dev-fixtures` (engine 420 passed, 1 ignored; contract 7; end-to-end 4); `npm run native:engine:build && npm run native:acceptance` passed ("seeding, restart, and rollback are deterministic"); `npm run tauri:smoke` passed on its hardened engines; `scripts/native-lanes.test.mjs` 9 of 9, and it fails on the old `tauri-smoke.mjs`; the live app's engine (PID 12268) held port 38201 before and after every run; the full Playwright lane at eight workers, 95 s: 378 passed and exactly the 13 known other-size failures of Slice SF's runs — no board moved, so no capture is refreshed here.

## Slice 3 — No keyboard shortcuts

Status: landed. Landed by #216, squash `da18125` at 16:14 UTC on 2026-09-26, on the operator's go-ahead given at the push ("Yes, merge when green"); the commits under the tag `archive/new-pages-s3-2026-09` (`88083b4`), whose tree `git diff` finds identical to `da18125`. The runs: `9e07ffb` 36235532154 and `9f88c88` 36251058503 (nine green, `frontend-e2e` red on the linux captures), `b1076ef` 36253234254 and `88083b4` 36253892912, all ten green (7.7 minutes). `main`'s run on `da18125`, 36254767725, all ten green in 9.2 minutes. Decided 2026-09-26: the operator accepted all twelve of the inventory's recommendations ("I would like to go with your recommendations"). Worked on 2026-09-26 on branch `new-pages/s3-no-shortcuts`, cut from `new-pages/c0-boards` at `8070ad3` (C0's boards, the inventory and the decisions, unpushed; they go with this slice), from `main` at `f3b588c`. The implementation ran as four agents at once, each in files of its own — the shell with the design system and the native shell; the Console; Lighting; Setup with the startup and recovery screens — then the assistant's integration: the guard, the page test, the docs, the fixes below, the Playwright lane, the captures and these records. Not split into S3 and S3b: both halves would move every board (the tab hints, the footers), so a split meant two rounds of captures and one more CI cycle for no gain in safety; the review is divided by area instead. The scope is what landed, so there is no **Rescope:**.

First step, shown to the operator before any code changed (D6): the inventory — every key the program binds and every palette command, beside the on-screen control that does the same. One without a twin gets a control or is dropped with the operator's OK. Drawn up on 2026-09-26 while the operator was away (six read-only surveyors, one per area, and a completeness critic; nothing changed): [`new-pages-2026-09-s3-inventory.md`](./new-pages-2026-09-s3-inventory.md) — twelve decisions, each with a recommendation, then every key that goes with its on-screen twin, what stays under D6, the hints and the tests. It also found an Enter in Lighting's empty search field that recalls a scene live while the list is closed, and that only Ctrl+Shift+R of the web view's own reload keys is blocked today, by accident.

Scope:

- **The shell**: Ctrl+1–3; Ctrl+K and the command palette with its per-workspace registrations (`shared/paletteContext`, the design system's `CommandPalette`, `buildAudioPaletteActions.ts`, Lighting's); the plain `A`; Shift+S; Ctrl+Shift+R; `?` with `shared/ShortcutOverlay.tsx` and `shared/shortcutGlyphs.ts`; the tab hints. The window commands become keys in Setup / Support › Workstation.
- **The Console**: `useAudioKeyboardShortcuts.ts`; the T key in `useMomentaryTalkback.ts` (D7); the hints "Hold · T", "(M)" and the footer's.
- **Lighting**: `useLightingCommands.ts`'s two handlers; F2 (D8); `P` for patch mode; the copy "Press P…" and "Press Esc…"; the hints in the footer, the DMX strip, the stage plot and its controls, the search field and the editors.
- **Setup**: `useSetupPilotShortcuts.ts`; the hints in the cluster, the footer and the recovery surface.
- **Startup and recovery**: the hints on the pre-ready, startup and recovery surfaces.
- **The design system**: the `Tab` hint, the `Footer`'s hint slot, `HealthBar`, and the arm word "ARMED · press again · Esc cancels", which loses its last clause; Esc itself stays in `useArm`, `Dialog`, `Drawer` and `ShellDialog`.
- **Tests**: the ten specs that press keys, the Vitest cases of the hooks, `CommandPalette.test.tsx`, `shortcutGlyphs.test.ts`.
- **Docs**: OPERATIONS, DEVELOPMENT, HANDOFF, the system doc, CHANGELOG.

Validation: as S1 (every board moves), plus the new source-scan guard.

What landed (the twelve decisions are the inventory's; the numbers here):

- **The shell** (`OperatorShell.tsx`): the palette, its registrations and its provider; the shortcut guide (`ShortcutOverlay.tsx` with its stylesheet) and the glyph helpers (`shortcutGlyphs.ts`); the whole window key handler (Ctrl+1–3, Ctrl+K, `A`, Shift+S, `?`, Ctrl+Shift+R and the Esc it held); the tab hints; `isEditableTarget`, which lost its last caller. `ShellDialog` ("Close Studio Control?", "Restart the hardware link?", "Retry startup?") listens on the window while it is open, so Esc closes it wherever focus is and Tab brings focus back in, and it sets itself up once per opening (Found, fixed). Studio Preview opens only from `?operatorReview=studio`; the app no longer remembers it and removes the old stored choice once (decision 1). The three window commands in `shellCommands.ts` reject with an `Error` whose message is the shell's sentence.
- **The native shell** (decision 12): `switch_off_browser_keys`, first in `.setup` and Windows only, switches WebView2's browser accelerator keys off (`ICoreWebView2Settings3::SetAreBrowserAcceleratorKeysEnabled(false)`): reload (F5, Ctrl+R, Ctrl+Shift+R), find, print, zoom, back and forward and the developer tools do nothing, while the editing keys in fields keep working; a failure is one line in `shell.log` and the shell carries on. The one guard binds no function and the page sees nothing of it. The COM calls are `unsafe` in the `windows` bindings, and the workspace forbids `unsafe_code`; a `forbid` cannot be lifted even for one item, so `tauri-shell/Cargo.toml` copies the workspace's lints with `unsafe_code = "deny"`, and exactly one `#[allow(unsafe_code)]` sits on `set_browser_accelerator_keys_off` (the guard below holds both). `webview2-com 0.38.2` and `windows 0.61` join the shell's Windows dependencies; both were already in `Cargo.lock` through Tauri and wry, which gains two dependency edges and no package. The three window commands answer in the operator's words — "Studio fullscreen did not start.", "The windowed layout did not start.", "The window layout was not reset." ("No monitor is available for studio fullscreen." as before) — with the detail in `shell.log` (`window_command_refusal`; before, "Main Tauri window is unavailable." and "Failed to …" sentences with paths, which the palette's callers never showed; the keys below do).
- **The design system**: `CommandPalette` and its exports go; `Tab`, `AppShellFrame`'s workspaces, `Footer` and the retired `HealthBar` lose their hint props (the footer's action key keeps the right edge); `ArmKey`'s word is "ARMED · press again"; `NumberEntryDialog` gains `resetValue`/`resetLabel`, a "Reset to <default>" key beside Cancel and Set value (decision 8); `ScrubSlider`, `ScrubLabel`, `Slider` and `MultiValueSlider` read no modifier key — no Shift ×5 step, no Backspace/Delete or Alt+double-click reset, no Ctrl/Shift fine or coarse drag, no Shift+press to unity (decisions 8–10); a plain double-click does what it did. The stories lose every hint; three token descriptions lose "kbd"; `primitives.css` drops `kbd` from its font reset.
- **The Console**: `useAudioKeyboardShortcuts.ts` goes (1–8, M, S, U, Shift+1–8, Ctrl+S, Alt+C, Alt+S, `[` `]`, the page-wide arrows, the plate's section keys P/Q/E/D/R — decision 4 — and the Esc that let a channel go — decision 6), with the palette's two files; the Esc that cancels an armed 48 V, recall or save moves into `useAudioArming.ts`, registered only while something is armed, and the strip's right-click menu closes on the design system's own Esc. D7: the T key goes; the Talkback key reads "Hold", its hover loses "Or hold T.", and the refusal reads "Release Talkback and press it again.". Decision 3: the Inputs heading carries ‹ Previous bank and Next bank › (`audio-bank-previous`, `audio-bank-next`; 24 px, dimmed at the first and last bank, shown whenever there is more than one bank), which page both rows as `[` `]` did and reach Line 1–8; the heading prints its bank readout on every bank, bank 1 included — "Bank 1 / 3 · ch 1-4 of 12" — so the keys sit beside it and "sends into Main Out" keeps its room (with the description it did not fit), and the readout counts the row's own channels (Found, fixed). Decision 9: the knobs, the plate's sends and its monitor level take the arrows, Home, End, Page Up and Page Down, and the strip faders and the Main level (the design system's `Groove` and `Slider`) the arrows, Home and End, none with a modifier; the strip's Gain key takes no keys (it opens typed entry). Decision 10: a plain click switches a group chip on or off and several can be lit; Shift+click to unity, the fine drags and Alt+double-click go. Decision 8: typed entry offers "Reset to <default>" on every knob that had a default, the Gain key (24 dB) and the strip faders (0 dB). The hints: the footer's four, "Shift 1–8", "(M)"/"(S)", the empty plate's "Press 1–8 …", the Gain key's "arrows to nudge", the armed row's "Esc cancels".
- **Lighting**: `useLightingCommands.ts` goes (both window handlers — P, B, S, Ctrl+S, Ctrl+Shift+S, 1–9, T, H, Shift+H, Shift+I, Ctrl+Shift+M, Ctrl+Shift+1–3, Shift+1–3, the arrows, Ctrl+A, Ctrl+F, Ctrl+Z, Ctrl+Shift+Z, Ctrl+Shift+P and the page-wide Esc — and the palette entries), with F2 (D8) and the quick palette panel (decision 7). Decision 5: an Undo key in the Rig section (`lighting-undo`) whose small print names the step it undoes (the undo stack's own label), in the system's locked form, dashed and dimmed with its reason, when there is nothing to undo; the add-fixture message has an Undo too; redo goes; an undo says "Undid ‘X’.". Decision 6: the Find key reads Stop while a Find runs and stops it, the flashes still waiting included; the narrow-window inspector (below 2560 only) is the design system's `Drawer`, with its own Esc (Found, fixed). Decision 10: the plot toolbar's "Add to selection" toggle (`lighting-add-to-selection`): while it is lit a click on a fixture adds it or takes it out, and a box adds; Shift+click, the Shift box, Shift+click on a bulk chip, the Alt drop without snap and Shift+Enter go. Decision 9: talent marks keep their arrows at 0.1 m. Decision 11: Enter in the empty search field recalls a scene only while the Recent list is open (Found, fixed). Decision 8: typed entry offers "Reset to 100 %" for intensity, the middle of the range for CCT and a control's default. The hints: the footer's, "(Ctrl+Shift+M)", the view slots' "· Shift+N", the stage plot's spoken arrow text, "Press P …" (now "Press Patch …"), "Press S …", "Press Esc …" (now "Could not clear Highlight and Solo. Open Lighting and press the lit Highlight or Solo key.", which shows only after Lighting is left, and "Press Stop again."; when Lighting is left mid-Find, "Could not stop the Find sequence. It ends by itself."), the bulk plate's Shift-click line.
- **Setup, startup and recovery**: `useSetupPilotShortcuts.ts` goes (Shift+S, Tab and Shift+Tab stepping the runner, Enter running the step's key wherever focus was, J/K, 1–4). Tests changed (old → new → reason; from the four agents' records, checked against the diff):

- Deleted, because they tested only a shortcut: `shell.spec.ts` "keyboard overlays and switching", "palette focus", "palette vs guide", "labels follow the platform"; `audio.spec.ts` the palette-and-guide and palette-recall cases; `audio-arm-countdown.spec.ts` the held Shift+digit case; `audio-talkback.spec.ts` the hold-T case (D7); `audio-metering.spec.ts` the palette signature case; `lighting.spec.ts` the palette Studio Preview, the page-wide nudge and the DMX monitor by key; `lighting-keyboard.spec.ts` F2 (D8); `CommandPalette.test.tsx` (8), `shortcutGlyphs.test.ts` (4), `useMomentaryTalkback.test.tsx` "holds while T is down" (D7), `HealthBar.test.tsx`'s two hint-only cases; the helpers `modifier-shortcut.ts` and `view-models.ts`.
- `shell.spec.ts`: the crash case raises "Close Studio Control?" with `__SSE_TEST_REQUEST_CLOSE__` instead of Ctrl+Shift+R, then Esc; the lazy-load case clicks "Audio" instead of Ctrl+3.
- `audio.spec.ts`: the main case's `]`, `1`, `M` and Shift+3 → the bank key, a click on the strip, its M key and the recall key pressed twice; Esc to deselect → a click on the Inputs heading; the E/D/R/P section lines go (decision 4); the footer check → no hint slot and no `kbd`; the group filter's Shift+click → a plain click, and "click FX to clear" → FX, then Bed (each a plain toggle); the page arrows and the Gain key's arrow nudge go (decision 9); recall by Shift+3 → two clicks; `U` → right-click › "Reset to unity"; `[` `]` → the bank keys; the dialog's `{ name: "Set" }` → `{ name: "Set value" }` (five places: "Set" also matches "Reset to …" in strict mode).
- `audio-talkback.spec.ts` and `audio-hierarchy.spec.ts`: "Hold · T" → "Hold", with a check that "· T" is gone; the dimmed-button case presses the button instead of T (D7). `audio-inspector-polish.spec.ts`: "Mute Host (M)" → exactly "Mute Host"; the bank pill case now expects "Bank 1 / 3 · ch 1-4 of 12" on the Inputs row and the Playback description (it expected the Inputs description on bank 1, and its comment wrongly said one bank). `audio-render-budget.spec.ts`: the section keys → the plate scrolled to each section, at most 3 renders (was 1–7 after the keys). `audio-file-structure.spec.ts`: the deleted hooks and the palette builder leave its file lists.
- `lighting.spec.ts`: `S` → the New scene tile; Ctrl+F → a click in the search field; the view keys → right-click › "Save current view to 1" and the slot; Ctrl+3/Ctrl+2 → the header tabs; Shift+Enter → the Add to selection toggle; Ctrl+Shift+S → the plate's "Save as new"; the quick-panel half of the palette case → the plate's "Apply Studio"; the message's Undo → `exact: true` (the Undo key's name contains "Undo"); "Set" → "Set value" exact. `lighting-keyboard.spec.ts`: the rename by F2 → a double-click on the name. `lighting-rig-actions.spec.ts`: "Esc ends a Find" → the Stop key; Ctrl+1/Ctrl+2 → the tabs; `selectFixture`'s Shift+Enter → the toggle, keeping its wait for `aria-pressed`.

The app: `useMomentaryTalkback.test.tsx` (the disabled case without its T press); `SetupSupportPilot.test.tsx` and `SetupHardwareLinkWords.test.tsx` (no guide prop); `LightingCluster.test.tsx`'s empty Undo key (`disabled` → the locked form, `aria-disabled`, with its reason — decision 5's "dimmed").

Found, fixed:

- Enter in Lighting's empty search field recalled the most recent scene live while the Recent list was closed (`LightingSearchField.tsx`); it recalls only from the open list now, and the list no longer reopens on every lighting refresh after Esc closed it (the effect was keyed on a new array each time).
- Adding a fixture appends it to every scene (in the hardware link and the fixture double), so the add-fixture undo's guard, which counted every scene holding the fixture, refused whenever a scene existed; it refuses now only for scenes saved after the add — against the real hardware link too since the review (#15 below: the link's replies carry intensity and colour temperature differently from the fixture double, which had hidden it). Needed for decision 5's Undo on "Fixture added.".
- `ShellDialog`'s focus effect depended on a callback the shell made anew on every draw, so every shell render (the clock every 15 s) pulled focus back to the dialog's first key. The design system's `Dialog` had the same fault for every dialog built on it whose owner passed an inline close handler — typed entry among them, where it took focus off the new "Reset to …" key within seconds so that Enter set the typed value instead (the review), and Setup's "Publish with failing probes?". `ShellDialog` sets itself up once per opening, and since the review the `Dialog` keeps the latest close handler in a ref and does the same, for all its users. ("Skip ahead?" had no focus handling at all before this slice; it is the design system's confirm now — below.)
- "Skip ahead?" ignored Esc and did not take focus (decision 11).
- Lighting's narrow-window inspector was a hand-built modal that only the page-wide Esc closed; it is the design system's `Drawer` now (below 2560 only).
- The Inputs heading's bank readout printed the Inputs bank's first channel, both rows' strip count and every channel ("ch 5-16 of 18" on bank 2); it counts the row's own channels now, and shows on bank 1 too.
- The window commands' refusals were developer sentences; they reach the screen now, in the operator's words.
- With the keys went the faults the inventory found (§7 note 4): a capital S typed in any Setup field flipped Runner and Support and was swallowed; Tab never moved focus in the Runner; Enter ran the step's main action whatever had focus, behind an open dialog too; Lighting's S saved a scene while the Save key was locked; the Console's Shift+S collided with the shell's; two Lighting hints were wrong ("Shift+S", "Press ⇧S").

Found, not changed:

- Lighting's "Save · press twice" saves on the first press, as a new "Scene N" (inventory §7 note 10, confirmed in the code). Not a key; for the operator.
- Undoing Delete fixture recreates the fixture with a new id, without its levels in the scenes.
- The locked Console's Inputs heading (not verified, offline) still cuts its parts short at 2560 — the readout, "sends into …" and the lock note — as it did before this slice; the state display carries the reason in full.
- The design system's `Dialog` and the Console's right-click menu close on Esc only while focus is inside them (both take focus when they open).
- The design system's `Slider` and `Groove` have no Page Up and Page Down, while the Console's knobs and send sliders do (decision 9 keeps them "where a slider has them"); the plate's sends and monitor level have no typed entry, so no Reset key (a drag settles on unity, and right-click resets a strip's send).
- The Console's `.canvasWarningBand*` styles look unused.
- One full local Playwright run (8 workers) failed the Storybook story "Direction D · ColorPicker (cleared)" at its paint check (the story root had not painted, height 0); 16 of 16 on repeat, and this slice changed neither the picker nor its stories.
- Not verified on the workstation: that WebView2 takes the setting before the screen's first navigation (the shell sets it before the window shows). Appendix B item 2 checks F5 on the live build.

The push (2026-09-26): the eight commits `26d4a46`…`9e07ffb` pushed to `new-pages/s3-no-shortcuts` with pull request #216 opened at the push and the operator's go-ahead asked in it. Its run, 36235532154: nine jobs green (`qualification` 8.4 minutes); `frontend-e2e` red — 62 capture diffs, which are the linux captures every board moves (expected, refreshed from a later run), and one case, `audio.spec.ts` "keeps the full audio workspace visible at the 1920x1080 fallback size": the Inputs lanes scrolled sideways, 405 px of content in 402 (Found, fixed below).

Review (2026-09-26, workflow `wf_ee78041c-24a`, right after the push): six area reviewers — the shell with the native shell and the guard; the design system; the Console; Lighting; Setup with the startup and recovery screens; the records and completeness against the inventory — and one skeptic per finding, told to refute it. 35 findings: 30 upheld (7 medium), 5 refuted (the guard's allowlists are per file; listeners attached through other means; the WebView2 call's timing; the narrow drawer's Esc under a dialog; Appendix B item 2's coverage). The fixes ran as four agents again, each in files of its own, and the assistant's; every added test is proved failing without its fix (Vitest against the old source, restored byte for byte; Playwright against a build of `9e07ffb` made from a stash of the fixes, or, for a changed case, against a build with the fix sabotaged).

- **Medium — typed entry's Reset key lost focus within seconds** (#9, #25). The design system's `Dialog` ran its focus effect on `[onClose]`, and every owner passed an inline close handler, so any redraw (the header clock every 15 s, a refresh) put focus back in the number field and Enter set the typed value instead of the reset. The `Dialog` keeps the latest close handler in a ref and sets itself up once per opening. Test: `NumberEntryDialog.test.tsx` "keeps focus on the Reset key when the consumer draws again" (fails on the old `Dialog.tsx` and on a naive fix that calls the first handler).
- **Medium — the Undo after Add fixture never worked against the real hardware link** (#15). Its guard compared the create reply's shape with the scenes' (the link's reply keeps intensity and colour temperature in its control values, and a fixture without colour temperature reads 0 there and 2000 in a scene), so it always counted every scene; the fixture double hid it. It now compares the colour temperature only for a fixture that has one and leaves those two keys out. Tests: `lightingWorkspaceModel.test.ts` (2, the link's own shapes for an Astra and a PB12 in pixel mode; "expected 3 to be 0" on the old model) and three end-to-end cases in the new `useLightingEditor.test.tsx`.
- **Medium — an Undo step that could never succeed stuck on top** (#17): a scene held a fixture deleted since, or a step named an object brought back under a new id, so every press failed ("The step is still in place.") and the older steps were out of reach, or the step undid a different scene that had taken the old id. Each step now holds targets (`lighting/undoTargets.ts`: one per scene or fixture, following it through a restore under a new id, marked gone when deleted or when its id goes to a new object) and reads the rig from the store: a step whose target is gone is refused with its reason ("Cannot undo ‘Save scene X’: the scene has been deleted.", "… the fixture has been deleted.", "… every fixture it held has been deleted.") and the key moves on; Delete scene comes back without the fixtures deleted since; Delete fixture comes back without its group when the group was deleted. Tests: seven cases in `useLightingEditor.test.tsx` over the real hook with a store that hands out ids as the link does (all fail on the old sources); Playwright `lighting-rig-actions.spec.ts` "Undo brings back a fixture, then a scene that held it, under the fixture's new id".
- **Medium — the window keys vanished in the window they make** (#22): Windowed (or a missing studio monitor, or display 2 at 125 %) gives a window narrower than 2200 px, where Setup's plate — with Workstation's Window row — is hidden, so nothing reached Studio fullscreen or Reset the window layout again. The row is one component now, drawn also under the Support screen while the chrome is compact (hidden at the studio surface, so no 2560 board moves), wired to the same actions (a refusal in Setup's message line). Tests: three Vitest cases in `SetupSupportPilot.test.tsx`; Playwright `setup.spec.ts` "in the windowed layout (1600x960) …" and "in display 2 at 125 % (2048x1152) the window keys are on the Support screen" (both fail on `9e07ffb`'s build).
- **Medium — no CI job compiles the Windows-only shell code** (#26), and its two crates must stay the versions Tauri's wry uses. Dependabot now ignores `windows` and `webview2-com` (they move by hand, with a Tauri update), and a new guard case holds `native/Cargo.lock` to one of each, named without a version by both the shell and wry (fails with a second `windows` planted). The `dev:check` on this workstation compiles the function (clippy and the tests run on Windows).
- **Medium — a held Enter confirmed an armed key** (#27): on a focused 48 V, recall or save key, Windows' key repeat pressed it again once the 350 ms dwell had passed. Both arm hooks (`useArm`, `useAudioArming`), whose window listener exists only while something is armed, now cancel a repeated Enter; a fresh Enter still confirms. Tests: `APrimitives.test.tsx` and `useAudioArming.test.tsx` (each fails on the old hook), Playwright `audio-arm-countdown.spec.ts` "a held Enter on the focused snapshot key …" and "… on a focused 48 V key …" (both fail on `9e07ffb`'s build).
- **Low**, fixed: Esc on "Close Studio Control?" also cancelled a Console arm under it (#1, #13) — `ShellDialog` listens in the capture phase now (test with the real `useAudioArming`, fails on the bubble-phase listener); the guard missed a key hint with the key interpolated, `Shift+${n}`, the view slots' old shape (#2) — a template part ending in a modifier and a plus before a placeholder is a hit now (fails on the old guard); the guard's `unsafe` check counted one spelling in three named files (#4) — it counts the lint's name across every Rust file of the shell and one `unsafe` block (fails with an `#[expect(unsafe_code)]` planted); "Esc and Cancel keep the link" did not check the link (#6) — it counts the startup handshake (fails with Cancel wired to the restart); a lit Inputs chip vanished on a bank without its strips and a click there switched it off (#12) — the lit chips stay in the heading and a click changes only its own (Vitest and Playwright, failing on the old code); in Preview a lit Highlight or Solo could not be switched off (#16) — a lit key stays pressable (Vitest and Playwright); the unmount message pointed at a key that is gone (#19) — "Could not clear Highlight and Solo. Open Lighting and press the lit Highlight or Solo key."; the view-slot case could not see a failed recall (#20) — it compares the zoom readout (fails with the slot's click a no-op); the marquee's "off" case pinned nothing on the old hook (#21) — it drags with Shift held; the window-key tests refused with sentences the shell no longer sends, and the recovery band said "not reset" twice (#23); `MultiValueSlider`'s reset doc (#11). Records corrected: the slider keys (#10, #14, #30), the dialog focus history (#24), the page test's coverage (#28), three test claims (#8, #29), DEVELOPMENT's preview port (#31), the product brief (#32), `lighting.md` §6 (#33), this record's validation (#35).

Found, fixed after the push: at the 1920 fallback the strip's Gain key, 86 px wide, held "GAIN" and "36 dB" in about 85 px of content with 12 px of padding and an 8 px gap, so the value ran 6 px past the key and past its strip; the Inputs column used to be wider than its strips (its heading was) and hid it, and since the bank keys and the readout it is exactly as wide as the strips, so the spill made the tier scroll — 1 px on Windows, inside the test's tolerance, 3 px on the linux runner. Below the studio surface the key's padding and gap are 4 px (`AudioStripGainKey.module.css`); the studio surface is unchanged. The failing case itself is the proof.

Found, not changed (the review):

- Below 2200 px (Studio Control in a window or on display 2), Setup's plate is hidden and with it the theme, the UI scale, Restart the hardware link… and the light outputs' Armed and Held; before this slice the palette reached the first three at any width. Studio fullscreen, now on screen at every width, brings the plate back (OPERATIONS says so).
- Delete scene and Delete fixture undos are not safe to repeat: if the follow-up (the pin and thumbnails, the level update) fails after the object came back, the step stays and a second press makes a second copy. Older than this slice.
- A scene the deck deletes, whose id the deck then gives to a new scene between a step and its undo, is still not noticed.
- A DMX address overlap on Delete fixture's undo stays a plain failure; the step is kept and works once the other fixture moves.
