Before the review: `dev:check` all 10 steps (27.5 s); Vitest app 29 files and 162 tests, design system 23 and 175, engine client 12 and 123, tokens 1 and 15; `scripts:test` 196 (190 pass, 6 skipped); `native:test` 447 passed, 1 ignored; the lane 374 passed of 398 with the 23, and one run in which the Storybook story "Direction D · ColorPicker (cleared)" failed its paint check (16 of 16 on repeat); the UI census re-seeded with no ratchet changed. No `native:acceptance`: the engine did not change.

The review's fixes pushed (`eab3f70`, `c7c5012`, `80f4371`, `9f88c88`): run 36251058503, nine jobs green and `frontend-e2e` red only on the 62 linux captures (343 passed — the 1920 case among them). Those 62 are refreshed from that run's `playwright-test-results` artifact, the pairs taken from the `frontend-e2e` job log: each `*-actual.png` copied over its `*-linux.png`, each 2560 capture compared with its win32 sibling after an 8× box reduction (mean difference at most 2.1, font rendering; none a single colour) and the Console's looked at. The last linux refresh: Slice SW takes the screenshot comparisons off CI (D22). The operator's go-ahead for #216, asked at the push and given 2026-09-26: "Yes, merge when green".

CodeQL on the pull request: its check failed at every push from `9e07ffb` on, on two alerts in C0's teleprompter boards, and they were missed until the run on `b1076ef` (36253234254) — this time CodeQL left them as annotations on its check, not as review conversations, so the rule on conversations did not hold the merge, and CodeQL is not one of the ten required checks. High: an incomplete multi-character sanitisation — `A-teleprompter-1.html`'s `plain` removed tags in one pass, and its text goes back into the page's markup. Medium: a prototype-polluting assignment — `A-teleprompter-3.html` looked its state up by the name in the address, so `?state=__proto__` handed back `Object.prototype` and the board wrote into it. Fixed in `Pages S3 (CodeQL): …`: the tags are removed until none is left, and the five boards that pick a state by the address's name (the three teleprompter boards, cameras 2 and 3) compare it with their own states' names and never use it to read one. Proved on those five boards at 2560×1440, the page's text and element count compared before and after: each of their 66 states and an unknown name the same; `__proto__` and `constructor`, which crashed the old boards (eight page errors), now show the default state with no error.

## Slice SW — Windows at 2560×1440, and nothing else

Status: landed. Landed by #221, squash `f28909e` at 19:56 UTC on 2026-09-26, on the operator's go-ahead given at the push ("Yes, merge when green"); the commits under the tag `archive/new-pages-sw-2026-09` (`8e66fa7`), whose tree `git diff` finds identical to `f28909e`. The runs: `896d43c` 36258451075 (8.5 minutes) and `8e66fa7` 36267232183 (8.1 minutes), all ten green, CodeQL clean. `main`'s run on `f28909e`, 36267762867, all ten green in 8.3 minutes. No hardware exposure, so it is verified at the studio walk with the rest (Appendix B item 2's window checks). Worked on 2026-09-26 on branch `new-pages/sw-windows-2560-only`, cut from `new-pages/s3-no-shortcuts` at `88083b4` while #216 was open (Tracking: the next slice may be started locally) and moved onto `main` at `da18125` (the same tree) before its first commit. The operator's ruling D22 (2026-09-26): Studio Control always runs at 2560×1440 on an external display, on a Windows machine; any other size or system is not to be considered. Asked the same day, the operator took every recommendation: Slice 3's linux captures are refreshed once and then this slice ("Refresh once, then SW"); on CI, `frontend-e2e` keeps every behaviour case on the Linux runner but compares no screenshots and samples no contrast there — the win32 captures at 2560×1440 and the UI contract are checked on the workstation before each push, and the required checks keep their names ("Local win32 only"); and the slice removes everything that exists only for another size or system, the dormant code included ("Everything").

Scope, from a read-only survey of 2026-09-26 (the session's `survey`; to be re-verified when the slice starts): the linux and darwin captures and the win32 captures at other sizes (169 files, about 49 MB; 59 win32 captures at 2560 stay); the size-only Playwright cases (`visual-review.spec.ts`'s other sizes and Studio Preview, `viewport-contract.spec.ts`, the 1920, 1280, 1600 and 2048 cases in the audio, lighting, startup and setup specs — their 2560 guards re-homed, not lost) and the `audio-1920-fallback` fixture with its three boards; CI's screenshot comparisons on Linux (the job and the required checks stay); the fallback layouts (the layout modes below `studioFull`, compact chrome and density, their CSS, the Console's compact banks, Lighting's narrow drawer), Studio Preview, the Windowed key and the window keys' compact copy, the shell's windowed layout where it is only a size fallback; the macOS scripts, packaging paths, signing, release assets, the release-evidence macOS job, `icon.icns`, the macOS schema, macOS strings and paths on screen and in fixtures; and every doc that describes another size or system. Kept: the Linux CI runners as infrastructure (and the code paths they need to compile and run), the engine's `shell.window.*` settings (protocol and saved data), the Prompter XL as a 1920×1080 second output screen (Part C), and whatever the 2560 build draws — every 2560 capture and the UI contract must stay unchanged unless a change is meant.

How the slice reads "Everything" (the assistant's decisions within the operator's answers, 2026-09-26):

- **SW-1 One layout.** The front end has one layout, the studio layout at 2560×1440. The layout modes, compact chrome and density, the Console's compact and touch banks, Lighting's inspector drawer and every rule for another size go; the layout provider keeps the UI scale and the theme only. A rule that always applies at 2560×1440 becomes unconditional only if it applies at every UI scale (90–125 %) — the UI scale is an operator preference, not a size, and stays. Lighting's saved column widths and zoom keep their `studioFull` storage names, so the operator's saved layout survives.
- **SW-2 Studio Preview goes**, with its address (`?operatorReview=studio`), its badge and its CSS.
- **SW-3 The window.** The Windowed key and the shell's windowed layout go; the shell always shows the screen fullscreen — on the display it was last on when that display is there, else on the 2560×1440 display, else on the display the window is on (today it fell back to a 1920×1080 display, then to a 1600×960 window). Studio fullscreen and Reset the window layout stay; the window keys' copy for screens under 2200 px goes. A saved window file from an older build still loads; a windowed one opens fullscreen.
- **SW-4 CI compares no pixels on Linux.** Playwright's `ignoreSnapshots` is on off Windows, the UI contract samples no contrast off Windows, and the Storybook spec's captures go with the rest while its load-and-paint checks still run (the review's correction: at first the whole spec was skipped off Windows); every job and the ten required checks keep their names.
- **SW-5 macOS and Linux as products go:** the macOS scripts, packaging, signing, notarisation, release assets and release-evidence job, `icon.icns`, the macOS schema, macOS copy on screen and macOS paths in fixtures (they become the Windows paths the build uses). The Linux code paths CI compiles and runs stay.
- **SW-6 Records are not rewritten:** CHANGELOG's past entries, the ledgers' past slices, audits, landed plans and design records keep their history; the living docs change.

Meant to move at 2560: the Setup screens that show the Support plate (the Windowed key goes) and the screens that show a macOS sentence or a fixture path. Built as seven agents at once, each in files of its own — the layout provider with the Console, the design system and the shared styles; Lighting; Setup with the start-up and recovery screens, the shell commands and the fixtures; the native shell and engine; the macOS packaging and scripts (tested only as copies in a scratch folder); the Playwright tests, captures and CI; the living docs — then the assistant's integration.

Gates before the change (`da18125`, the same tree as S3's last push): the local Playwright lane had 405 cases (382 passing and the 23 win32 captures at other sizes stale by the rule of 2026-09-22), CI compared the 82 linux captures, the UI contract measured 66 boards (the `audio-1920-fallback` fixture's three among them), `viewport-contract.spec.ts` pinned the four layout modes' minimums, visual-review drew six sizes and Studio Preview, and the Console's lane-overflow guard ran only in its 1920 case, at the compact banks — no test measured the Playback lane at 2560 (the Studio Preview case measured the plate, not the lanes). Vitest: the app 189, the design system 179, the scripts 197.

What landed (the working tree, 2026-09-26; seven agents, each in files of its own, then the assistant's integration):

- **One layout (SW-1, SW-2).** `operatorLayout.ts` keeps the UI scale only; the layout provider measures nothing and carries the UI scale and the theme (its root keeps `data-operator-layout-root` and `data-ui-scale`; `data-chrome`, `data-layout-mode`, `data-review-*`, `data-layout-width/height`, `data-device-pixel-ratio` and the viewport wrapper go, the root taking the wrapper's background and `position: relative`). Studio Preview goes with its address, badge, transform and the Studio-Preview-only shell backdrop tokens (regenerated). The Console has no density: its banks are the studio ones (4 inputs, 6 playback pairs, 3 outputs), the short mix-target name goes, and the 416 px plate is the base rule — the container query that gave it held at every UI scale in all 108 measured screens. Every rule keyed on compact chrome or density or a layout mode, and every media or container query that never matched at 2560×1440 at any UI scale, goes (about 30 stylesheets), with 11 classes that only those queries named. Lighting's inspector drawer, its Inspector key, the other modes' column specs and the Fit room default below the studio layout go; the saved widths and zoom keep their `studioFull` names, read back unchanged.
- **The window (SW-3).** The shell shows the screen fullscreen only: the display it was last on when that display is there, else the 2560×1440 display, else the display the window is on (it used to fall back to a 1920×1080 display, then to a 1600×960 window). The Windowed key and command, the windowed layout and restore, and the window keys' copy for screens under 2200 px go; Studio fullscreen and Reset the window layout stay, Studio fullscreen now across its row. `shell-window-layout.json` no longer holds the windowed fields; an older build's file loads, a windowed one opens fullscreen, and a file this build writes is ignored by an older build, which then opens on the 2560×1440 display. The first window is 2560×1440 with no minimum. A launch with no monitor now writes a shell.log line (it was silent). The recovery band's sentence loses "and press Support" (the plate's keys show in every mode at 2560).
- **CI compares no pixels on Linux (SW-4).** `ignoreSnapshots` off Windows; `SAMPLES_CONTRAST` in the UI contract (off Windows no screenshot and no contrast; the ratchet skips it by name and refuses a missing measure on Windows); the Storybook spec's stories still load and paint there. Jobs, steps pinned by `ci-workflow.test.mjs` and the ten required checks are unchanged.
- **macOS and Linux as products (SW-5).** 32 macOS npm scripts; `native-sign-macos.mjs`; the macOS branches of about 20 packaging, installer, update, acceptance, checksum, SBOM, continuity and release scripts (`REQUIRED_ASSETS` are the three Windows assets; the continuity check compares the Windows payload name only, because the v2.2.0 and v2.2.1 tags' identity files still name a macOS payload — proven in a scratch git repository); the release-evidence macOS job; `icon.icns`; the macOS schema; the macOS copy on Setup / Support and the recovery screen; the macOS paths in the fixtures and the fixture double, which now show the Windows paths the build uses under `C:\Users\Studio`. In Rust, the macOS arms of the shell's and the engine's platform maps, the Unix-only token file mode and the macOS and XDG folder openers (a non-Windows arm refuses, for CI's Linux compile). `deny.toml` loses the darwin target. The Linux code CI compiles and runs stays.
- **Tests.** 45 cases that tested only another size or Studio Preview go (`viewport-contract.spec.ts` whole); their 2560 guards move into four 2560 cases (the Console's workspace and boxes, Lighting's layout, the recovery screen's no-scroll, Setup's C4 case); the context-menu clamp moves to 2560's edges; the `audio-1920-fallback` fixture and its three boards go (66 → 63 boards). 169 captures are deleted (82 linux, 64 darwin, 23 win32 at other sizes); 59 win32 captures at 2560 stay.
- **Docs (SW-6).** 17 living docs, the native and scripts READMEs, the SBOM tool's description and `v1.md`; records keep their history.

Found, fixed:

- **A scrollbar under the Console's Playback lane at 2560×1440, and tooltips that spilled their words** (on `main` before this slice: measured on builds of `8070ad3` and `9e07ffb`). The re-homed 2560 case found `audio-tier-lanes-software-playback` scrolling 764 of 760 px. The Console's `.stripKeys > * > *` rule, meant for the key in each tooltip wrapper, also caught the tooltip and made it the key's 48 px, so "Playback" spilled out of the bubble — at the last strip, 4 px past the lane (WebView2 draws a thin scrollbar there; headless Playwright hides scrollbars, so no capture showed it). Now the rule takes the key only and a key's tooltip opens inwards across its row, as wide as its words up to the row. The lane holds at every UI scale, also while a tooltip shows. Proof: the new case in `audio-inspector-polish.spec.ts` and the 2560 Console case fail with the old rule and pass now.
- `expectSnapshotActionsDoNotOverlapContent` passed a raw bounding box (x, y, width, height) to a check that reads left, right, top, bottom, so it never found an overlap; it now reads the boxes it compares.
- Dropping the macOS payload name from the release identity would have failed the Windows release gate's continuity step against the v2.2.x tags; it compares the Windows payload only.

Found, not changed:

- At UI scale 110 and 125 % the Console's Outputs lane does not fit its three strips at 2560 (376 of 330 and of 262 px) and scrolls, as before; the operator runs at 100 %.
- Dead CSS not tied to a size (about 40 unused classes in `OperatorShell.module.css`, some in `SetupSupportPilot.module.css`), the design system's unused `OperatorFrame`, and its `Drawer`, which now has no app consumer (its stories and tests keep it).
- `container: operator-root / size` on the root: no query names it now; kept, since containment has layout effects.
- The release manifest keeps `schemaVersion: 1` (the fields that went were always null on Windows); `HOME` and `XDG_CACHE_HOME` stay in the installer acceptance lane's environment, part of what the Windows runs get.
- The fixture's log file stays `studio-control.log` (the build's `logs\engine.log` would put the word on the recovery boards).

Tests added (each fails without the slice): `audio-inspector-polish.spec.ts`'s "a strip key's tooltip holds its words inside the strip, and the lane does not scroll" and `audio.spec.ts`'s 2560 Console case ("keeps the full audio workspace visible and inside its boxes at 2560x1440") both fail with the old `.stripKeys > * > *` rule (764 > 760) and pass now; the shell's `fullscreen_display` tests and `shell_window_preferences_from_an_older_build_still_load`; `tauri-visual-review.test.mjs`'s win32-only parser case; `OperatorLayoutProvider.test.tsx`'s three cases of what the provider still does (the stored scale and theme on the root, the body and `<html>`; an unknown scale reads 100 %; `?theme=` wins; a new choice is saved and re-stamped). Tests that had to change (old → new → reason): the window keys' Setup cases ("Studio fullscreen", "Windowed", "Reset the window layout" → "Studio fullscreen", "Reset the window layout" → the Windowed key went); the Studio Preview and other-size cases → gone, their 2560 guards moved into the 2560 Console, Lighting, recovery and Setup cases (the plate's 416 px, the lanes' no-scroll, the lane cards in their tiers, the side rails, the processing stack, the square knob, the snapshot actions, the inspector panels, the footer facts, the stage's minimum, the primary controls) → D22; the context-menu clamp's probes from 1280×800's edges → 2560×1440's → the only screen; `audio.spec.ts`'s `data-density="desktop"` and the modes' `data-layout-mode` asserts → gone, a C4 case asserts the 2560×1440 viewport itself → one layout; `audioViewModel.test.ts`'s compact and touch banks → gone, the cap case for three strips reads 3 / 6 / 3 → the studio banks only; the release scripts' macOS cases → Windows cases where they were the only test of a branch (the depth-5 app search in `clean.test.mjs`, the checksum manifest lines) → SW-5; `SetupSupportPilot.test.tsx`'s bay-copy cases → one case that Support mode shows exactly one Window row, the plate's → the bay copy went; `PreReadyKeys.test.tsx` pins the recovery band's new sentence.

Validation (the working tree, 2026-09-26): `dev:check` passed, 10 steps in 24.0 s — the scripts 189 (185 passed, 4 skipped), the app 186, the design system 179, the engine client 123, the tokens 15, the shell 22, the engine 414 (one ignored: the live TotalMix round trip), the contract 7, end to end 4. ## Part C — the new pages

**Rescope:** (2026-09-27, the first cloud session under D23; the operator's answers to the re-cut it put, with options and costs.) Part C's slices after Slice 4 are cut into the cloud's and the workstation's. The slice numbers stay, so the drift guards, the Common rules and Appendix B still point at the right slice.

- **The cloud, in this order:**
  1. **S5a**, the glass and the Prompter XL's state in the hardware link: the front-end half of Slice 5 and its contract (`checks.prompter`, the shell's report, `PLAY`'s lock);
  2. **S6**, the Teleprompter page, as **S6a** (the page) then **S6b** (the editor, Rename, New script, Paste and the clipboard permission), split at its first steps (Slice 6a's **Rescope:**);
  3. **S8**, the cameras' model and the simulated cameras;
  4. **S9**, the Cameras page;
  5. **S7 and S12 as one slice**, the two deck pages, after S9. They share the `exports.rs` split, the page chain of D5 and Setup's deck steps, which then change once.
- **The workstation, once the cloud's slices have landed (serial):** the catch-up (below), then **S5b** (the Prompter XL's window: the shell's second window, Windows' display configuration, the live app of rule 4, the `main.rs` split, the shell's report of the screen), **S10**, **S11**, **S13** (gated on D18) and the close-out.
- **The shell.** A cloud slice may change `native/tauri-shell`, `cfg(windows)` code included, only when `cargo check` and `cargo clippy -- -D warnings` of `sse-exed-tauri-shell` pass for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` in that session (`rustup target add x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`; proven from Linux on 2026-09-27 — a type-check, nothing linked or run). What such a change does on Windows at run time is a catch-up item. Slice 6 (6b since the split) therefore keeps the clipboard permission.
- **The UI contract** (the session's procedure). A new or moved board is seeded in the cloud with `node scripts/ui-census.mjs --fixtures <its fixtures> --write-ratchets` (from `frontend/app`), so the boards seeded on Windows keep their figures. A board seeded off Windows has no contrast figure, which Windows' check reads as no failure allowed; every board seeded off Windows is re-seeded with contrast at the catch-up. No `*-win32.png` is made, refreshed or deleted in the cloud.
- **Not taken:** six cloud slices in D21's strict order (S7 after S6 and S12 after S9: a sixth pull request, and Setup's deck steps and the page chain changed twice); three large slices (each larger than Slice 4's 18,842 added lines, and harder to review lean); a cloud that never touches the shell (the clipboard permission would have gone to S5b); the two streams run side by side (the operator chose serial).

**Rescope:** (second; 2026-09-27, evening, the operator's handoff from the cloud back to the studio workstation, as Slice 8 waited on its last checks; D23 amended) the cloud's part ends with Slice 8. The rest of the program is the workstation session's, **S9** (the Cameras page) and **S7+S12** (the two deck pages) included, which the re-cut above gave to the cloud. A slice that moves boards is finished on the workstation again before it merges — its win32 captures made or refreshed and inspected, its UI-contract boards measured with Windows' contrast and ratcheted (Common rules, the Baseline refresh procedure); the cloud's arrangement (its own renders reviewed, the captures at one catch-up) covered S5a, S6a, S6b and S8, all landed. The order, put to the operator and approved on 2026-09-28 ("Go with your recommendations on all of it"), serial: **the catch-up (below) → S9 → S7+S12 → S5b → S10 → S11 → S13** (gated on D18) **→ the close-out**. The catch-up comes first although S9 moves every capture again right after it: it checks on Windows what the cloud's slices changed, apart from what S9 changes. The operator's Windows checks at run time are made on a check copy of `main`'s build under an identifier of its own (the catch-up, below), since the shell's single-instance guard, keyed on the identifier, would otherwise hand a second copy's start to the live app.

### C0 — Design (no code)

Status: verified (2026-09-27; no hardware exposure). Landed by #224 as `9af8028` (squash, 2026-09-26 23:28 UTC). Runs: the pull request's run 36278544924 on `aab9a8d` had all ten jobs green and CodeQL clean (its only annotations were a runner notice), and main's run 36279532554 on `9af8028` had all ten green in 7.6 min. The merge's commits are kept under the tag `archive/new-pages-c0-2026-09` on `aab9a8d`, whose tree is `9af8028`'s. The tag was pushed on 2026-09-27 from `refs/pull/224/head`, because GitHub had already deleted the branch at the merge (deleteBranchOnMerge). Main's one red CodeQL run, on `e9bd9a1` (36268966091), was a failed upload ("Code scanning is not enabled"), and the next run passed. Worked on 2026-09-26 in a cloud session on branch `claude/serene-goldberg-ydfpfc`, cut from `main` at `e9bd9a1`. The research ran on 2026-09-24; D17 was decided the same day. On 2026-09-26, while the operator was away, the boards were drawn (step 3): three Concept A boards per page at 2560×1440 in the three themes — `A-cameras-1.html` (three monitors), `-2` (hero and two), `-3` (camera console), `A-teleprompter-1.html` (live mirror), `-2` (editor first), `-3` (rundown) — and the functionality proposal, [`docs/redesign/teleprompter-2026-09.md`](../redesign/teleprompter-2026-09.md). Each board was drawn by a designer agent, critiqued against Concept A and the decisions by another, and revised; each fits 2560×1440 in all its states and themes (measured in the browser pane) with no console error. Nothing in the app changed.

Answered on 2026-09-26, in a cloud session away from the workstation: three rounds of questions, each asked with a recommendation, and the operator took every one — D19 (Cameras from board 2, Teleprompter from board 1) with the ten decisions the boards raised, D20 with the proposal's §14, D18 with its two checks made the gate of the BGH1 slice alone, and D21 (the Teleprompter first); D12 amended for the page's own copy of the glass. The rows above carry them; the boards' decisions are also recorded in [`docs/redesign/new-pages-boards-2026-09.md`](../redesign/new-pages-boards-2026-09.md) and D20's answers in the proposal's §14. The boards were shown as captures rendered in that session at 2560×1440 in Studio, with Inter and JetBrains Mono served from local copies (the session's browser could not reach Google Fonts, and its first renders fell back to another face). Step 4 the same day: Slices 4–13 below, in place of the provisional list; the operator approved them as written ("Approve; push and open the PR").

Review (2026-09-26, at the push of `4cb0ed9`, #224 opened at the push): two reviewers — the record against the operator's answers, and the slice plan against the code — found 3 high, 9 medium and 8 low findings. The assistant checked each high and medium finding against the text or the code it cites, in place of separate skeptics, to keep the run lean (the operator's usage limit), and every one held. Fixed in `Pages C0 (review): …`. The slices gained what the code requires: `teleprompter` and `cameras` join the pages the hardware link accepts (it refuses any other, while the fixture double took any word); the scroll's clock runs in the layout the front end reports; the glass window's capability, entry point and closing, and the live app of rule 4 defined in code; finding the Prompter XL through Windows' display configuration, with the shell's `unsafe` guard widened by name; `checks.prompter` and `checks.cameras` for the lamps; the pictures' three routes and NDI's discovery; where the SDKs' calls live, their DLLs loaded at run time, packaging and licences; the editor's keys, and how a file and pasted text arrive; whether the LUMIX SDK connects without searching; the file-health splits, the archive formats, the migration tests and `v1.md`; and the CAMERAS deck page moved ahead of the BGH1s' link (Slices 11 and 13 swapped), so D18's checks hold up nothing else. The record's own slips were fixed too: Slice 12's `REC` is a red lamp on a dark key, not "lit red"; `PLAY` at a script's end in Slices 4 and 7; D11 names Update as armed; D19's loupe sits beside the small pictures; the proposal's §6.1, §7, §9, §12 and §13 and its lines that still read as open; the boards index's notes; the handoff's rule on sending to devices.

Findings (the deciding ones checked against their source on 2026-09-24):

- A DeckLink input belongs to one program at a time: "EnableVideoInput will provide an application exclusive access to the hardware input" (DeckLink SDK, `IDeckLinkInput`). Studio Control therefore never opens the inputs vMix uses (D12).
- The BGH1's RTP/RTSP streaming stops recording to its cards, its menus and LUMIX Tether's live view (Panasonic's BGH1 manual, streaming chapter). Not used (D17).
- Panasonic's LUMIX SDK (LumixRemoteControlLibrary, Beta 2.01, 2022-08-08; Windows 10 64-bit, Visual Studio 2015, a C++ library) supports the BGH1 over Ethernet: ISO, shutter, aperture, white balance, exposure, autofocus, recording mode, recording, zoom, manual focus, AE lock, live view. Picture profile / V-Log / LUT and card time are not listed. The download asks for the camera's 11-digit code from the battery holder and the acceptance of a licence agreement; beta, no support.
- A BGH1 pairs with one controller; Cyanview's integration notes say going back to LUMIX Tether needs a camera settings reset. Whether that holds for the SDK is D18's open question (it decides what Release can promise, D13).
- The Pocket 6K Pro's Bluetooth protocol is published in its manual (service `291D567A-6D75-11E6-8B77-86F30CA893D3`; messages as in Blackmagic's SDI camera control protocol): focus and one-shot autofocus, iris, shutter, gain / ISO, ND, white balance and tint, dynamic range, display LUT, video mode, recording format, codec, record. Battery and card time are not in it. A write to the camera's status characteristic can switch the camera off: Studio Control never writes it (a guard test, Part C). Pairing uses the camera's 6-digit PIN; the Rust BLE library btleplug does not pair on Windows by itself.
- The Micro Converter HDMI to SDI 3G has two SDI outputs (Blackmagic's tech specs); not used — the operator rejected a second copy of each signal.
- The Prompter XL: 15.6-inch, 1920×1080, 60 Hz, 600 nits; one USB-C cable carries picture and power (15 W required); a DisplayLink screen Windows treats as an extra monitor; it flips whatever it shows (Newsshooter, Elgato's pages), so Studio Control draws the script unmirrored — Appendix B item 13 confirms it in the glass.
- vMix: "This option can be enabled to automatically convert any cameras, calls and audio inputs in the current vMix session into live NDI outputs" (vMix 29 help, Settings › Outputs). The NDI SDK is free with attribution conditions (a link to ndi.video where NDI is used, the trademark line, the DLLs in the app's folder).

1. Research, started 2026-09-24: whether two programs can share a DeckLink or Cam Link input; a DeckLink input in WebView2; the BGH1's outputs, network streams, control protocol and any SDK; the Pocket 6K Pro's Bluetooth protocol; the Prompter XL; NDI from vMix and OBS; the converters' outputs.
2. D17 and D18 put to the operator with options and costs.
3. Boards: two or three Concept A variants per page at 2560×1440 in Studio, Graphite and Bone, as self-contained mocks like `docs/redesign/assets/concepts/A-*.html`, for the operator to pick from (D19); the prompter's functionality written out with them (D20).
4. Part C's slices written into this ledger in place of the provisional list (now Slices 4–13 below), with Status lines, and approved (D21).

### Slices 4–13 (written 2026-09-26 in place of the provisional list)

Written from the operator's answers of 2026-09-26 (D18–D21 and the boards' decisions), Teleprompter first (D21), and approved by the operator the same day; the review at the push (C0, above) added what the code requires and moved the CAMERAS deck page ahead of the BGH1s' link. The provisional list had six slices. Its Teleprompter slice is split in four (the hardware link; the glass and its window; the page; the deck page), so the work that moves no board can be done away from the workstation. Its Cameras model slice is split in two (the model; the page), the pictures come before the two cameras' links, and the Pocket's link and the CAMERAS deck page come before the BGH1s', the one slice D18's checks hold.

Common to every slice:

- One pull request from `main` under the Tracking rules; the Hardware safety rules and D15's six rules in force before the first line of camera or prompter code; the front end shows only what the hardware link reports.
- A slice that adds or moves a board: on the workstation (5b, 10, 11, 13) it is finished there before it merges — its win32 captures made or refreshed and inspected, its UI-contract boards measured and ratcheted, the Baseline refresh procedure. In the cloud (D23, Part C's **Rescope:**; S5a to S8 only, since the second **Rescope:** brought the rest back to the workstation) it merged once its ten checks were green, its own renders at 2560×1440 were reviewed in the three themes and the operator had given the go-ahead; its captures and contrast went on the catch-up list. A slice that changes only the hardware link and the contract, in code CI compiles, can be done away from the workstation (Slices 4, 5a and 8).
- CI's ten jobs run on Linux and never compile Windows-only code: a slice that brings some — the screens' names (Slice 5b), the NDI receiver (Slice 10), Windows' Bluetooth pairing (Slice 11), the LUMIX SDK (Slice 13) — is built and tested on the workstation (`dev:check`) before its push.
- The saved data goes from schema 8 to 9 (Slice 4) and 10 (Slice 8), and the backup archive from format 5 to 6 (Slice 4) and 7 (Slice 8). Each schema step has a migration test that starts from the live data's schema 7 (`seed_v7_database`, `storage/tests_schema_8.rs`) and makes the whole jump in one start, as the live data will at the close-out, behind the pre-migration backup.
- Each slice that changes the contract brings `native/protocol/v1.md` up to date with it (a protocol source of truth, AGENTS.md), not at the close-out.
- A file near `file:health`'s 2,000 lines is split before a slice takes it past them: `native/tauri-shell/src/main.rs` (1,790 lines, 1,828 after Slice 5a's guard; split in Slice 5b, and Slice 6b's clipboard change keeps it under 2,000 or splits it first), `native/rust-engine/src/support/tests.rs` (1,933; Slice 4's format-6 tests) and `native/rust-engine/src/exports.rs` (1,769; the two deck pages, as `exports_audio.rs` was split out).
- The NDI SDK (C) and the LUMIX SDK (C++) need `unsafe` code, which the engine forbids workspace-wide (`native/Cargo.toml`, held by `scripts/check-no-shortcuts.test.mjs`): their calls live in a crate of their own that names each allowance under a guard like the shell's, or in a helper process, decided in Slice 10's first step. Their DLLs load at run time, so every build, CI's and the release-evidence runner's included, works without them installed. A new crate is checked against `native/deny.toml`'s licences first (libloading, the usual DLL loader, is ISC, which the list lacks), and Windows' APIs stay on the one `windows` crate the lockfile holds (0.61.3).
- Nothing sends to a camera, a Bluetooth device or the Prompter XL outside the live app (D15).

### Slice 4 — Teleprompter: the scripts and the prompter in the hardware link

Status: verified (2026-09-27; no hardware exposure). Landed by #225 as `e6244e6` (squash, 2026-09-27 09:34 UTC). Runs: the pull request's last run 36309167146 on `e9155fd` had all ten jobs green (its first, 36306473342 on `8ae1731`, failed in `rust`'s `native:acceptance` on the archive format, fixed in `61d97d3`), and main's run 36309786407 on `e6244e6` had all ten green in 8.5 min; main's CodeQL run 36309786326 passed. The merge's commits are kept under the tag `archive/new-pages-s4-2026-09` on `e9155fd`, pushed from the workstation. Branch `new-pages/s4-teleprompter-link`, cut from `main` at `9af8028` on 2026-09-27.

The scripts and the prompter in the saved data (schema 9): each script's text as paragraphs (bold, italic, underline, cues), its name, the file it came from, when it was made and changed, and its own speed and place; its last 20 versions; the removed scripts; the one look; what the glass shows (the script and its text as it went on, so an edit waits for Update). The `prompter.*` methods and a `prompter.changed` event: put on, replace, update, clear; play, pause, speed, the jumps (line, paragraph, cue, `BACK`, `TOP`), the text size and the look; new, rename, edit, remove, restore, delete for good, bring a version back. The place is words (a paragraph and a word), never pixels. The prompter is paused after every start (opening, a restart, a crash, a restore); nothing but the operator's controls moves the place; at a script's end play is refused until a jump moves the place back, and play never jumps (D12, D19). Recent actions: put on, replaced, updated, cleared, with Screen or Stream Deck. Backups: the archive goes to format 6 with the scripts, their versions, the removed ones, the look and each script's place and speed; Verify counts the scripts; a restore adds scripts and never removes or overwrites one (a differing text comes back as an earlier version). The fixture double gains all of it, so the page can be built on it.

`teleprompter` joins the pages the hardware link accepts: `shell_settings.rs` takes only lighting, audio and setup today and refuses the rest, so the tab could not open in the real app, while the fixture double takes any word. Its refusal sentence, its tests and `v1.md` follow, and the fixture double refuses an unknown page the same way, under a guard that reads the list out of `shell_settings.rs`, as S2b's guard reads `support.rs`.

The import, read by the hardware link itself: `.docx` (the proposal's §3.2: what is kept, what is dropped and counted in the import sentence; `.doc`, a password-protected document and a script over 30,000 words refused), `.txt` (UTF-8, UTF-16 with its byte-order mark, else Windows-1252) and pasted text, plain or formatted. How a file and pasted text reach the hardware link is settled before the contract, since it depends on it: the shell has no file dialog and no clipboard today, and a button cannot make a paste — the file's bytes from the page or a path from a shell dialog; the clipboard read by the shell, or text pasted into the editor.

First step, before any code, recorded under this heading with its boundary impact (AGENTS.md): the scroll's clock. Proposed: the front end reports the layout of each script version in each look (every line's paragraph, first word and height, and where `END` falls), since only it has the fonts; the hardware link runs the clock in those units and sends an anchor with every change — the position, the speed in pixels a second, playing or not, and the milliseconds since the anchor, since the two processes share no clock — from which the glass and the page's copy both draw the motion; from the same anchor it derives the saved place, the time left, a line step and the stop at `END`, so a pause from the deck never snaps the text back. With no layout reported yet (the Prompter XL unplugged and the page closed since the start), the time left is estimated from the words and the line steps wait. A clock that advanced in words would make the scroll's speed change with each line's length: a change to the proposal's §5.1, which would go back to the operator. Boards: none.

**First steps (2026-09-27), before any code.** Each was put to the operator with its options, costs and a recommendation.

1. **The scroll's clock.** (a) **Layout units, the proposal (recommended).** Whichever front-end view draws the glass (the glass in Slice 5, the page's copy in Slice 6) measures the script version the prompter shows, in the look it shows, at the glass's own 1,920 px width, so both views report the same thing. It sends the measurement to the hardware link with a new request, `prompter.layout.report`: the version, a key for the look, every line's paragraph, first word, top and height, and where `END` falls. The hardware link runs the clock in those pixels. The speed in pixels a second is the pace times the layout's height per word (§5.1), so the whole script's length at a pace stays words ÷ pace in any look. Every change sends an anchor in `prompter.changed` and the snapshot. The anchor carries: the position; the speed before and after, with the 0.3 s ease; playing or not; the start of a jump's 0.2 s move; the layout's key; the place in words; and the milliseconds since the anchor, because the two processes share no clock. The glass and the page's copy both draw the motion from it. The hardware link uses the same anchor to save the place in words (about once a second while playing, and at every stop, jump and change), count the time left and step a line. It stops at `END` on its own timer, so a pause from the deck never snaps the text back. A new look or an Update keeps the words at the reading line: the hardware link holds the place in words until the new layout arrives, then re-anchors. With no layout yet (nothing drawn since the start), the time left is estimated from the words, and a line step is refused with a sentence. **Boundary impact:** the front end measures and draws, and holds no prompter state. The script, the place, the pace, playing, the clock and the stop at `END` are the hardware link's. The layout is the one thing the front end reports: a fact about the fonts, which only the front end has. It is kept in memory, not in the saved data, and reported again after a start. No device I/O moves, and the contract gains one request from the front end. (b) **A clock in words.** The hardware link advances the place N words a minute, and the glass turns the place into a height line by line. The contract is simpler, with no layout report. The cost: the text rushes over short lines and slows on long ones (3 to 8 words a line at about 32 characters), which changes §5.1. (c) **The hardware link lays the text out itself**, with Inter's metrics in Rust, and sends each line to the glass. The cost: new crates for font metrics and line breaking, and Inter's font files in the hardware link. Its measure and the browser's drawing can differ slightly, so a line can run into the margin. It is the most code of the three.
2. **How a file reaches the hardware link.** (a) **The page's own file picker (recommended).** An `<input type="file">` opens Windows' own Open dialog, which WebView2 carries, and the page sends the file's name and its bytes (base64) to the hardware link in one request. The hardware link reads the `.docx` or `.txt` itself: a zip reader over `miniz_oxide` and `quick-xml`, both already in the lockfile, so no new crate. The hardware link never sees a path, and it still reads nothing outside its own folder (F29's rule stays whole). The costs: the bytes cross the pipe (a 30,000-word `.docx` is about 0.2 MB; files over 20 MB are refused before they are read), and opening a file again is recognised by its name, as §3.3 has it. (b) **A shell dialog that returns a path.** Tauri's dialog plugin brings a new family of crates into the lockfile and `cargo deny`, and a new capability permission. The hardware link would then read files anywhere on disk, an exception to F29. (c) **A shell dialog whose file the shell reads**, passing the bytes on. There is no F29 exception, but the plugin comes with it.
3. **How pasted text reaches it.** The survey found that a button can make a paste. Tauri's `enable_clipboard_access` makes wry grant WebView2's clipboard-read permission. (a) **The page reads the clipboard (recommended).** The shell switches that permission on for the main window only. To do so it builds the window in code, from its entry in `tauri.conf.json`, and a test holds that no other window gets it. A press of Paste as a new script, or of the editor's Paste key in Slice 6, reads `text/html` or `text/plain` with `navigator.clipboard.read()` and sends it to the hardware link. The hardware link converts the formatting with a small reader of its own for paragraphs, line breaks, list items, headings (which become cues), bold, italic and underline; no new crate. (b) **A paste field.** The button opens a dialog with a field; the operator pastes into it with the browser's own editing (Ctrl+V, or Paste in the right-click menu, both kept by D6) and presses Add. It needs no shell change, but it takes two steps, and the editor's Paste key (D20) would still need (a), so it only postpones it. (c) **The shell reads the clipboard** through the `windows` crate (the plain text, and Word's `HTML Format`). The costs: `unsafe` code, which widens the shell's one allowance; Windows-only code that CI never compiles; and Word's raw markup to convert.
4. **Splitting `native/rust-engine/src/support/tests.rs` (1,933 lines).** (a) **A move-only split first (recommended).** Slice 2's archive-format section (about 530 lines: the format 4 and 5 tests and their helpers) moves to `support/tests_formats.rs`, and the shared helpers (`TestDir`, the seeders) move to `support/test_support.rs`, in a commit that only moves code (checked with `git diff --color-moved`). The format-6 tests then land in `tests_formats.rs`. The prompter's part of the archive lives in the new prompter module with its own tests, so `support.rs` (1,350 lines) grows only by its calls. (b) **A new file only.** The format-6 tests go into `support/tests_format_6.rs`, and `tests.rs` stays at about 1,933 lines, so Slice 8's format 7 meets the same wall. (c) **A directory module** (`support/tests/`, one file per topic). It is more churn than the slice needs.

**Answered by the operator on 2026-09-27, each as recommended:** the clock runs in layout units (1a); a file reaches the hardware link through the page's own file picker, as a name and its bytes (2a); the page reads the clipboard, with the permission granted to the main window only (3a); and `support/tests.rs` is split by a move-only commit first (4a). The shell's clipboard permission goes in with Slice 6's page (Slice 6b since the split), where a press first uses it; Slice 5's glass window never gets it, and a test holds that (corrected at Slice 6b's review: the test holds the shell's rule, but WebView2 saves the main window's answer in the profile every window shares, so Slice 5b keeps the glass's window out of it; Slice 5b's gate). Slice 4 brings the contract and the hardware link's side of both routes.

**Built (2026-09-27).** In the hardware link, `native/rust-engine/src/prompter/`:

- `model.rs`: paragraphs of runs, words, cues, read words and the limits.
- `look.rs`: the one look and its ranges.
- `clock.rs`: the layout, the motion, the anchor and the END stop, with the cases in `clock/motion-cases.json` that the front end's formula is held to.
- `runtime.rs`: one prompter for each saved data, loaded paused, and the live app's clock thread.
- `store.rs`: the SQL.
- `edits.rs`: a place across an edit or an Update.
- `commands.rs`: the twenty-two methods.
- `snapshot.rs`: the three reads.
- `archive.rs`: format 6's part.
- `import/`: `.docx` over a zip reader of its own, `.txt`, and pasted HTML or text.

Wiring outside that folder:

- storage: schema 9.
- `support.rs`: format 6.
- `app.rs`: the dispatch arms, the restore's pause, and `prompter.changed`.
- `action_log.rs`: the rows and the classification.
- `shell_settings.rs`: `WORKSPACES`.
- `main.rs`: the clock thread.
- `engine_events.rs`: the event.
- protocol and contract: the event and the twenty-two methods.
- `v1.md`: a Teleprompter section and the lines it changes.
- `CHANGELOG.md`.

The miniz_oxide, quick-xml and base64 crates, each already in the lock file, join the hardware link's dependencies.

Settled while building, each within the design:

1. The clock refuses `PLAY` and a line step before a layout is reported (`PROMPTER_NOT_LAID_OUT`). The ledger's text said only that the line steps wait. Without a layout the scroll's pixels do not exist, and the glass or the page's copy reports one as soon as it draws (Slice 5 locks `PLAY` while the Prompter XL is not connected).
2. Replacing what the prompter shows needs `replace: true` (`PROMPTER_REPLACE_NOT_CONFIRMED`). The page's second press sends it, so a one-press slip cannot change the glass (D11).
3. An archive restore brings back the look and the text size. The glass's text, and its script's place, it never touches. The prompter stops where it is, paused, and a look that came back keeps the words at the reading line.
4. A database backup's Verify sentence is unchanged; only the archive counts scripts.
5. The editor's own Paste (Slice 6b) will send what it reads to the editor, not as a new script. If it needs the hardware link's HTML reader, that is a contract change for Slice 6b.
6. The fixture double does not read a `.docx`; it answers with a sentence that says so. The hardware link's reader is tested in Rust.
7. The layout is kept in memory and reported again after every start. The key `g<text revision>-l<look revision>` is stored, so a report from before a restart cannot be taken for a later one.
8. A blank line in pasted HTML (`<br><br>`, as e-mail and web pages often part their paragraphs) ends a paragraph, as an empty line does in a `.txt`. A single break stays a line break, and a table cell or a heading keeps its lines. The two readers (hardware link and fixture double) agree on this. They also agree on round brackets inside a heading, and on counting a paste's pictures as left out.
9. For Slice 6: the page's own transport passes a refusal's sentence but not its code (`tauriTransport.ts` throws a plain `Error`, as before this slice). The fixture double's refusals carry the code. The page should therefore read what it locks from the prompter's state — `glass`, `atEnd`, `laidOut`, `notUpdated` — and not from the codes, unless Slice 6 passes the code through.

Gates before the change: `settings.update` refused `teleprompter` (`shell_settings.rs`'s test held the three-page sentence). The saved data was schema 8, with tests pinning 8. A new archive was format 5. The contract had no `prompter.*` method, and `tests/contract.rs` would refuse a dispatch arm the contract lacks.

- the three Recent actions rows;
- 182 engine-client tests (123 before, one of them failing on the hardware link's new recorded methods).

Where it differs, by design:

- script ids are a counter, so fixture runs repeat;
- the place is saved on each request rather than by a thread;
- a `.docx` is refused with "…the fixture double reads .txt files and pasted text; a Word document is read by the hardware link.";
- a malformed `paragraphs` or `lines` answers with the hardware link's opening words, without serde's own ending.

Validation before the push (2026-09-27, on the studio workstation):

- `npm run dev:check`: 10 steps in 53.3 s — engine 516 tests, contract 7, engine-client 182, scripts, lint, types, format, protocol, file health, clippy with warnings denied;
- `npm run native:engine:build`;
- `npm run frontend:playwright:test`: 364 cases passed, captures included, and no board moved;
- the live app (shell 17156, engine 12268 on 38201), TotalMix FX and Companion untouched throughout;
- `native:acceptance` and the qualification lanes left to CI, as the rules have them.

**Push and review (2026-09-27).** #225 was opened at the push of `8ae1731`, with the operator's go-ahead asked there; it was given the same day ("Yes, merge when green"). On the push run 36306473342, nine checks were green and CodeQL was clean (its only annotations are a runner notice). The tenth, `rust`, failed in `native:acceptance`: "support.backup.export reported format 6, expected 5". The lanes' expected format was fixed in `61d97d3`, and `scripts/native-lanes.test.mjs` now holds it to `support.rs`.

Three reviewers covered what CI does not: the prompter's rules and data safety, the import on hostile input, and the fixture double's parity with the hardware link. The assistant checked every high and medium finding against the code it cites, in place of separate skeptics, to keep the run lean. Every one held. They were fixed on the branch before the merge:

- **Rules and data:**
  - M1: Clear, Replace and the archive saved an unupdated script's place against its edited text; it is now carried across.
  - M2: Update landed in the wrong paragraph when an edit and a cut met; it now matches by likeness.
  - M3: a failed save turned PAUSE into an error while the text scrolled on; saves are now best-effort.
  - M4: a new size moved the reading line by up to a line. The anchor now carries `wordOffset` in place of `lineFraction`.
  - Lows: END unannounced when a request found it first; the pause saved before its ease stopped; `nextParagraph` went backwards; two views' layouts could drift a paused place (the first report wins); a script of cues alone ran through at once; the dangling `Update` wording.
  - The rework found and fixed one bug of its own: a no-layout motion inside a word was dropped.
- **Import:**
  - High 1 (`model.rs`): cue counting was quadratic, and a paragraph of 30,000 cue lines stalled the hardware link. Now one pass.
  - High 2: zip lookups were quadratic over thousands of header parts. Now indexed, capped at 64 parts, and a zip of over 10,000 entries is refused.
  - Mediums: the text size was unbounded (now collapsed white space and a 2 MB cap); Word's tracked deletions came in from pasted HTML; Word's footnotes and comments came in from pasted HTML.
  - Lows: attribute memory; lists sharing one definition; style bold, italic and underline; numbers without a stop; a non-Word compound file; hidden text; the paste's size sentence; embedded documents (`718dcb9`, 14 tests that fail on the old code).
- **Parity:**
  - High: the live transport dropped a refusal's code while the double kept it. Both now throw an `EngineRequestError` with the code.
  - Medium: the double's END timer slept without a layout.
  - Lows: END unannounced; three wrong claims in `v1.md`; name order by code point.

Commits: `6544927` (hardware link), `61d97d3` (lanes, D23, `v1.md`), `718dcb9` (import), `71fda9b` (docs) and `fe0d529` (the double).

Tests changed in the review (old → new → reason):

1. `prompter/clock/tests.rs` `a_new_look_keeps_the_words_at_the_reading_line`: "the line holding word 10" → word 19.4 kept to within 0.01 word through three new layouts. The anchor carries words now (M4).
2. `prompter/clock/tests.rs` `a_layout_arriving_mid_scroll_carries_the_motion_on`: the paragraph after 20 s → the word within 0.1 after a realistic 200 ms. Over a long wait, words and pixels drift apart by the paragraph gaps.
3. The clock tests' calls moved from `place_at(..).0`, `jump(place, fraction)` and `Motion { place, fraction }` to `place_at`, `jump(paragraph, word_offset)` and `Motion { paragraph, word_offset }`.
4. `prompter/import/tests.rs`:
   - `line_breaks_white_space_and_entities`: `keep   its` → `keep its` (white space collapsed);
   - `a_style_attribute_overrides_the_elements_own_emphasis`: `<ins>` is no longer underlined;
   - the bare compound-file header: password-protected → not a `.docx` (moved into the finding-10 test).
5. The fixture double's tests:
   - "steps one line": `placeAtPosition` → `wordsAtPosition`;
   - the new-look test: split in two, as in Rust;
   - "finds the text at END on the next request too": no `at-end` → `at-end` once (L1);
   - "goes where BACK…": the last `nextParagraph` → refused `PROMPTER_NO_PARAGRAPH`;
   - "reads the clipboard's HTML": a lone no-break space stays.

After the review:

- engine 539 tests;
- engine-client 206 tests;
- app 186, design system 179, tokens 15;
- script tests 186.

Known differences left in the fixture double, by design:

- it reads no `.docx`;
- its HTML paste reader is simpler: it keeps Word's tracked deletions, footnotes and comments, and does not number Word list items without a stop;
- its saves cannot fail;
- its ids are a counter.

The import, by one agent in a worktree of its own (`import/`, 49 tests: 42 cases, five property tests of 256 inputs each over the zip, the `.docx` built from Word's elements in any nesting, damaged bytes, `.txt` and HTML, and two worst cases — tables nested 50,000 deep, hostile pastes). Beyond the proposal's §3.2 it:

- trims white space at the ends of every line in all three readers;
- turns square brackets inside a heading into round ones, so the heading stays one cue;
- refuses a compound file that holds a `WordDocument` stream and no `EncryptedPackage` as Word's old format, since it is a `.doc` renamed;
- also reads Word's template and macro-enabled documents (their text only);
- keeps a numbered marker Word writes into pasted HTML (`2.`, `b)`), so numbered lists are not flattened to dashes;
- follows numbering set by a paragraph style, with its overrides;
- drops a table row marked deleted;
- counts formatting changes as tracked changes;
- reads only the first `mc:Choice`;
- skips equations and ruby text;
- puts a numbered heading's number inside its cue (`[1. Intro]`);
- caps the headers' and footers' unpacking at 32 MB and HTML nesting at 512.

### Slice 5a — Teleprompter: the glass and the Prompter XL's state

Status: landed (operator verification pending — the workstation catch-up: the glass's seven captures, and the shell's refusal of `prompter.screen.report` tried on Windows). Landed by #226, squash `5261565` at 11:47 UTC on 2026-09-27, on the operator's go-ahead ("Go ahead please"); its seven commits are to be kept under the tag `archive/new-pages-s5a-2026-09` on `590cd9f`, whose tree `git diff` finds identical to `5261565` (the catch-up pushes it from `refs/pull/226/head`: GitHub deleted the branch at the merge). The runs: the pull request's last, 36316171539 on `590cd9f`, all ten green, and its CodeQL 36316172111 with no new alert; `main`'s run 36316857142 on `5261565`, all ten green in 7.5 min, and `main`'s CodeQL 36316856819 passed. The first slice built in the cloud (D23), on branch `claude/gallant-goldberg-o8vsj2`, cut from `main` at `e6244e6` on 2026-09-27.

**Rescope:** (2026-09-27, Part C's re-cut) Slice 5's front-end half and its contract, done in the cloud; the Prompter XL's window, Windows' display configuration and the shell's side of the screen's report stay in Slice 5b, on the workstation.

The glass: one front-end component that draws the Prompter XL's 1920×1080 screen from the hardware link's state — the look (Inter at medium weight, 88 px standard, the line spacing, the margins, white or yellow text on black, the cue colour, the reading line 35 % from the top, text already read dimmed, `END`) and the scroll, drawn from the prompter's clock — unmirrored, since the Prompter XL flips it; it reports its layout to the hardware link (Slice 4). The glass lives in `frontend/app/src/app/teleprompter/glass/`, so Slice 6's page and Slice 5b's window draw the same one. It lays the text out at a fixed 1,920 CSS px and scales it, so every view breaks every line alike; it reports its layout for the current `layoutKey` once the fonts are ready, every paragraph (an empty one too) with a line and a height; and it draws the motion from the anchor with `motion.ts`. Its Storybook stories: paused mid-script, playing (the clock pinned), at `END`, yellow text, the largest size with no margins and the line across, paragraph numbers on, and blank.

The screen's states (connected, not connected, duplicated, low resolution, not showing) reach the hardware link from the shell, which alone reads Windows' display configuration, with a request of its own; the shell sends it from Slice 5b. They reach the header's lamp as a `checks.prompter` section of the health snapshot, which the fixture double carries too: the lamps read `checks.lighting`, `checks.audio` and `checks.controlSurface` today, and an entry in the health registry alone would light nothing and would turn the whole status to error. `PLAY` is refused while nothing is drawn on the glass. Boards: the glass's Storybook stories (new; their win32 captures at the catch-up).

**First steps (2026-09-27), before any code,** each put to the operator with its options, costs and a recommendation, and answered the same day as recommended:

1. **The whole status.** A Prompter XL state makes Setup / Support's summary no worse than attention; the header's `Prompter` lamp itself is red for `NOT CONNECTED`, `DUPLICATED` and `NOT SHOWING` (D20). Refined the same day (Push and review, below): only a state the shell has reported counts, and `NOT UPDATED` lights the lamp only. Not taken: the whole status as red as the lamp (the sound and the light are fine); the lamp alone, the summary ignoring the prompter.
2. **Before the shell reports** (after each start, and in every build until Slice 5b): `NOT CONNECTED`. Nothing is known to be drawn on the glass, so `PLAY` stays locked; a test or a lane that plays reports a state first. It lights the lamp and no longer moves the whole status (below). Not taken: a sixth word (`NOT REPORTED`, attention), which the proposal lacks and the operator would rarely see.
3. **What `checks.prompter` carries:** one check with the worse of the screen's state and `NOT UPDATED`, and the screen's name, size and refresh rate, so the header's lamp reads it as the other lamps read theirs and the front end works nothing out. Not taken: the screen alone, with the front end combining it with the prompter's `notUpdated`.

Settled from board 1 and the proposal's §7–8 without a question: `PLAY` is refused for `NOT CONNECTED`, `DUPLICATED` and `NOT SHOWING`, and plays at `LOW RESOLUTION`; a report that takes the screen away while the text scrolls pauses it at its place, and plugging back in leaves it paused; jumps, speed, size, the look and edits keep working.

**Boundary impact** (AGENTS.md): the screen's state is a fact only the shell has, so the shell reports it; the hardware link owns what follows from it — the check, `PLAY`'s lock, the pause — and the front end reads the check and draws. No device I/O moves. The contract gains one request, sent by the shell, and one section of the health snapshot.

**Built (2026-09-27, in the cloud).** In the hardware link:

- `prompter/screen.rs`: the report, the state, its words and sentences, `PLAY`'s refusal and `checks.prompter`.
- `prompter/runtime.rs`: the screen kept in memory, `NOT CONNECTED` at every start.
- `prompter/commands.rs`: `prompter.screen.report`; `PLAY`'s order of refusals; the check compared before and after every request but a read (after the review, only around the eight requests that can change it, below).
- `prompter/snapshot.rs`: `screen` in `prompter.snapshot`.
- `health.rs`: `checks.prompter` and the whole status's cap.
- `app.rs`: `app.changed { reason: "health" }` after a request that changed the check.
- `action_log.rs`: the report is not an action.
- The contract, `v1.md` (a section, the Prompter XL, under Teleprompter; `checks.prompter` under the health snapshot) and `CHANGELOG.md`.

The fixture double, by a second agent (`frontend/packages/engine-client/src/transports/fixture/`): `prompterScreen.ts` (a port of `screen.rs`), the report, `PLAY`'s order, the pause, `checks.prompter` and the status's cap in `state.ts`, and a scenario's optional `prompterScreen`.

The glass, in `frontend/app/src/app/teleprompter/glass/`:

- `glassText.ts`: a paragraph cut into lines, words and spaces by the hardware link's rules for words and cues;
- `glassLayout.ts`: the look in the glass's pixels, the layout from what was measured, and `positionOf` and `lineAt` as `clock.rs` has them;
- `glassMotion.ts`: where the text stands at a moment, and the band over the text already read;
- `glassFont.ts`: Inter's italic for the glass alone;
- `PrompterGlass.tsx` and its stylesheet, over the new `--prompter-glass-*` tokens;
- seven Storybook stories (`Teleprompter/Glass`).

Settled while building, each within the design:

1. The shell reports facts — found, duplicated, the size, the refresh rate, a window that did not open — and the hardware link works out the state; `LOW RESOLUTION` below 1920×1080 is its rule, not the shell's.
2. Before any report the word is `NOT CONNECTED` (first step 2), with a sentence that says what happened: "Windows has not reported the Prompter XL since Studio Control started."
3. `PLAY` is refused in this order: nothing on the prompter, nothing drawn on the glass, at the end, not laid out.
4. The pause when the glass goes is `PAUSE`'s own, its ease included, and the place is saved where the ease stops.
5. The health snapshot's `details` stays as it was: the recovery surface lists it, and a new row there would move a board. Its `summary` gains `Prompter: <sentence>` only when a reported Prompter XL state counts toward the whole status (below).
6. The fixture double starts every scenario with the Prompter XL connected (the shell has reported; the Prompter XL stays plugged in, D20), so no board gains a red lamp in Slice 6; a scenario may start it otherwise with `prompterScreen` (done in 6a: `teleprompter-not-connected`).
7. The glass draws the line whose top is at the anchor's position centred on the reading line, as board 1 draws a paused script, and dims the text above the top of the line at the reading line. An anchor for the glass's own layout is drawn from its pixels, exact in the gaps between paragraphs too; one for another layout is drawn from its words through the glass's layout, and a jump's move only when the key matches.
8. The text never runs under the arrow or the paragraph numbers: when the margin leaves no room (0 % margins, or numbers on at a narrow margin), the column starts further in. At the standard 12 % the arrow stands 64 px clear of the text, as on board 1, and 24 px clear of a number. The line across sits under the baseline, where the proposal says it can meet the descenders.
9. The glass's italics are a family of its own, `Prompter Glass Italic` (Inter's italic files, Latin and Latin Extended): loading Inter's italic for the whole app would redraw the one italic line the app has, in Lighting's DMX strip, and this slice moves no board.
10. For Slice 6: the UI contract's census must skip `[data-picture]` (the glass's type is the presenter's, the proposal §6.1); the page wires `onLayout` to `prompter.layout.report` (both done in 6a).

Gates before the change: `prompter.play` played whatever the Prompter XL did (nothing knew it); `health.snapshot` had no `checks.prompter`; the contract had no `prompter.screen.report`; the glass did not exist.

- the fixture double: `prompterScreen.test.ts` (19, and more after CI and the review, below, among them a guard that reads `screen.rs` up to its tests and holds the double's sentences to it word for word);
- the glass: `glassText.test.ts` (7), `glassLayout.test.ts` (6), `glassMotion.test.ts` (6), `PrompterGlass.test.tsx` (5), and eleven more after the review (below).

**Review (2026-09-27).** Right after the push, three reviewers on what CI does not cover:

- the hardware link and the double's parity (six findings: two medium, one medium latent until Slice 5b, three low);
