A skeptic then checked the nine medium findings against `9ae1322`. Every one is real or partly real, and none stays medium. The worst, a failed read of the glass's script making `PAUSE` fail while the text scrolls, needs a corrupt page or an I/O error on that script's own row: in WAL mode, a full disk or a held write lock does not fail a read.

The operator's answer, the same day, with options and a recommendation: **`NOT UPDATED` lights the lamp only**. An edit before Update is normal work, not a fault, so it never raises the whole status or adds to the summary. That amends the answer after CI above, where `NOT UPDATED` always counted. Not taken: `NOT UPDATED` counting, which would have shown Setup / Support's warning after every edit before Update.

Fixed in `Pages S5a (review): …`:

- **The comparison can no longer hold up a take.** `checks.prompter` is compared only around the eight requests that can change it: `prompter.screen.report`, `putOn`, `update`, `clear`, `script.edit`, `script.rename`, `script.import` and `script.version.bringBack`. The script on the glass cannot be removed, so `remove` and `delete` cannot change the check. A read of the check that fails is a `WARN` line, and the request goes on and raises `app.changed { reason: "health" }` anyway. A dial turn costs no read of the script.
- **`health.snapshot` survives a prompter it cannot read.** `checks.prompter` is then `null`, with a `WARN` line, and the whole status comes from the rest; before, the app's start failed on it.
- **The report's numbers.** A size or refresh rate may be a fraction, rounded to whole units (59.94 Hz is 60), and `refreshHz` may be left out: the sentence then says `1920×1080`. 0 is refused, so Slice 5b leaves the rate out when Windows gives none. A window error is not kept while `DUPLICATED`, where the state ignores it.
- **Only the shell sends the report.** The shell refuses `prompter.screen.report` from the page (`page_may_send` in `main.rs`, with a test); Slice 5b sends it on a path of its own. Type-checked and linted for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`; whether the guard holds at run time on Windows goes on the catch-up list.
- **The double can start as every start does:** a scenario's `prompterScreen: "unreported"`.
- **The records:** the Common rule split by where the work happens (Part C); Slices 7, 9 and 12 built in the cloud; the Baseline refresh procedure's cloud exception; D21 and D23 marked amended; first steps 1 and 2 and settled item 5 amended; `v1.md` (the method count, `app.changed`'s causes, the whole status's causes, the snapshot's `screen`, the report's validation, the events on any change to the report); `CHANGELOG.md`; the stale "Slice 5" references; the S5a drift guard; the S7+S12 pull request's name; the catch-up's doc counts; `AGENTS.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` and `docs/HANDOFF.md` on the cloud's captures; and the next-session doc (what is in review and next, the contract of Slice 5a, the Playwright browser, Windows-only code, the double's start).

Not taken: keeping the report outside the prompter, so that a report still counts when the prompter cannot load. Slice 5b's shell reports on every poll, so a report lost that way comes again (Slice 5b, below).

Tests added:

- Rust: `prompter::tests_screen::a_pause_acts_even_when_the_scripts_cannot_be_read` (it moves `prompter_scripts` away mid-take; it fails on `9ae1322`), `prompter::screen::tests::a_refresh_rate_may_be_a_fraction_or_missing`;
- the shell: `shell_only_method_tests::the_page_cannot_report_the_prompter_xl`;
- the double: a fraction or missing refresh rate, a scenario that starts unreported, and `NOT UPDATED` lighting the lamp only.

Tests changed (old → new → reason):

1. `prompter::screen::tests::a_report_that_is_not_one_is_refused`: `refreshHz` 60.5 and a missing `refreshHz` refused → both accepted, and new cases refused instead (`refreshHz: 0`; a found screen with no height). Fractions and a missing rate are now valid.
2. `prompter::screen::tests::only_a_reported_state_counts_toward_the_whole_status`: an edited script on a connected Prompter XL counts as attention → counts for nothing (`Ok`); a Prompter XL at low resolution with an edited script counts as attention with the screen's sentence. `NOT UPDATED` lights the lamp only.
3. The double's refusal messages: "a whole number above 0" → "a number above 0"; its "not a report" case `width: 1920.5` → `width: 0.4` (a fraction is valid; below 0.5 rounds to 0). The rule changed.
4. The double's "makes the check the worse of the screen and NOT UPDATED": `wholeStatus(screen, edited)` → `wholeStatus(screen)`, and an edited script's whole status "attention" → "ok". `NOT UPDATED` lights the lamp only.
5. The double's guard on the shell's report: a `refreshHz` of 59.94 refused → accepted as 60.

**The glass's review.** The glass reviewer laid the stories out in Chromium with other scripts and looks through Storybook's args, and found two high findings, four medium and five low. A skeptic checked the six against `9ae1322` and upheld them all: the empty paragraph as high; the report once per key as medium but latent, since nothing mounts the glass yet and the page's copy is mounted again by the recovery screen after a restart, so it is Slice 5b's window and a lost or refused report that it strands; the others as low to medium. It added one: a restore of the saved data rewinds the revisions, so a key can come back with other text.

- **An empty paragraph could make the hardware link refuse the layout (high).** The global `box-sizing: border-box` put the paragraph's gap inside its minimum height, so an empty paragraph was one line with no gap, and a whole-pixel `offsetTop` could put the next paragraph up to 0.8 px above the line's end, past `Layout::new`'s 0.5 px. The reviewer saw 80 of 290 looks refused for a script with scattered empty paragraphs, which the editor keeps. Fixed: an empty paragraph is a line and its gap.
- **The glass reported once per key and never again (high).** After the hardware link restarts, the key is the same and the layout is gone, so `PLAY` stayed locked until the look or the text changed; a lost report or a measure while hidden did the same. Fixed: the glass reports again, at most once a second, when an anchor for its key has no position; it sends only a layout the hardware link would take (`layoutProblem`, `Layout::new`'s rules in `glassLayout.ts`), so a glass measured while not drawn sends nothing and is measured again once it is drawn (a `ResizeObserver`). A key that comes back with other text is reported again, since the glass compares the text as well as the key.
- **A word that wraps stood on the middle of its pieces (medium).** A long word that opens a paragraph put the paragraph's first line a line or more too low, so `TOP` and the jumps landed late. Fixed: a word is measured from its first box on the screen.
- **A cue line was taller than a line (medium).** The smaller face on the paragraph's strut made its line box about a tenth of the size taller, and the tops drifted by up to half a line. Fixed: a cue line is set at the line's top; the line boxes are now within 2.1 px of the layout in every look swept.
- **A new look reset the anchor's moment (medium).** Toggling the dimming mid-take drew the text back to the anchor (245 px after 4 s at 140 words a minute). Fixed: only a new anchor takes a new moment; the dimming, the reading line and a still only draw again.
- **The glass measured again for a new object of the same look or text (medium).** With 30,000 words that is a 170–240 ms stall mid-take for a colour or a snapshot fetched again. Fixed: the text is cut again only when it says something else (a comparison, not a measure), and the layout is measured again only when the key, the text or the geometry changes.
- Low, fixed: a cue inside a word (`[smile].`) is drawn in the cue colour where it stands; the paragraph numbers keep room for the script's last number, so ¶ 1000 and on clear the arrow; the glass resets the text properties it could inherit from the view around it, so both views break lines alike.
- Low, not taken:
  - A face that does not load is measured without it rather than never reported: both views load the same faces from the app, so both would lay out alike, and never reporting would lock `PLAY`.
  - The line at the reading line moves on to the next line halfway through a line's words, while the place and `BACK` still count the line being read: that is settled item 7 (the line at the place centred on the reading line when paused, as board 1 draws it), and it is put to the operator with Slice 6's first steps, where the page's copy shows it (moved to Slice 6b's first steps at 6a's review: 6a's four were put before the page could show it).
  - `PLAY` or a speed change within 0.2 s of a jump ends the jump's move early (the hardware link's new anchor carries no move): a snap at the end of a short move, left as is.
  - A size step or an Update of a 30,000-word script lays the text out again in 300–460 ms: the browser's own layout, for Slice 6 to know (6a's copy draws one text in one key, settled 3; the editor's quarter-size copy is 6b's).

Validation of the glass's fixes: every layout in a Chromium sweep of six scripts (scattered empty paragraphs, alternating cue lines, long words that wrap, cues inside words, an empty script) at seven sizes, six line spacings, three margins, with and without paragraph numbers — 1,512 in all — passes `Layout::new`'s rules, with every paragraph's first line where its first word is; the seven stories rendered again at 2560×1440 and compared with the renders before.

Tests added: `glassLayout.test.ts` (3: room for the last paragraph number; what the hardware link takes; what it refuses, the empty paragraph's case and a glass not drawn among them), `glassText.test.ts` (1: a cue inside a word), `PrompterGlass.test.tsx` (7: no layout the hardware link would refuse; reporting again when the anchor asks, at most once a second; no measure for a new object of the same look; a key that comes back with other text reported again; an anchor's moment kept when the dimming changes; a wrapped word on its first piece's line; a cue drawn inside a word). The seven component tests fail on the component at `9ae1322`.

Tests changed (old → new → reason):

1. `glassText.test.ts` "counts words as runs of characters that are not white space, across emphasis": a piece is `{ text, bold, italic, underline }` → it also carries `cue: false`. A piece now says whether it is inside a cue.
2. `PrompterGlass.test.tsx` "reports its layout once for each key, every paragraph with a line": rendered in jsdom as it is → with `layOut`, a stand-in for the browser's layout. The glass no longer reports a layout the hardware link would refuse, and jsdom lays nothing out (every top 0).

Validation after the review (2026-09-27, in the cloud session): `npm run dev:check`, all ten steps (engine 554 tests, shell 23, contract 7, end to end 4; the frontend's tests with their coverage floors, the engine client 228); the shell type-checked and linted for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`; `node scripts/check-operator-copy.mjs`: 0; the frontend's tests with coverage again after the last fix; Playwright's `default` project on Linux, 368 of 371 passed. The three that failed (a Lighting case, a no-shortcuts case and a Storybook story's paint check) touch nothing of this slice and passed on their own; the Storybook spec's check that a story's root has a height reads the box right after the root attaches, and in this container it failed for one or two of the 47 stories on each of five runs, a different story each time, with the glass at `9ae1322` as with the fixed one, so CI's `frontend-e2e` is the authority.

### Slice 5b — Teleprompter: the Prompter XL's window

Status: planned — on the workstation, after the cloud's slices and the catch-up.

**Rescope:** (2026-09-27, Part C's re-cut) the glass, its stories, `checks.prompter`, the screen's report in the hardware link and `PLAY`'s lock went to Slice 5a, in the cloud. This slice keeps the Prompter XL's window, Windows' display configuration and the shell's side of the report, which only the workstation can run.

The shell's second window shows the glass (Slice 5a) only on the screen Windows names `Prompter XL`: fullscreen, borderless, no taskbar entry, never taking the keyboard, the pointer hidden; it closes when that screen goes and never moves to another; Studio Control's own window is held on display 3 when screens come and go. The shell reports the screen's state to the hardware link (Slice 5a's request) at the start, after the hardware link restarts and on every poll of Windows' display configuration (the hardware link ignores a report the same as the last), so a report lost to a moment's failure is repaired by the next (review of Slice 5a).

**Gate from Slice 6b's review (before the second window is built):** the Prompter XL's window is built through `shell_windows.rs`'s `webview_window_from_config`, which gives it no clipboard permission of its own; and because WebView2 saves the main window's clipboard answer in the profile every window shares (wry's handler sets no `SetSavesInProfile(false)`), the glass's window is also kept out of that answer, one of two ways, put to the operator at this slice's first steps: a profile of its own (`incognito`, or a data directory of its own — config a test can hold on Linux; whether an InPrivate profile inherits a saved answer is to be tried), or the shell's own `PermissionRequested` handler for the main window, allowing the read with `SetSavesInProfile(false)` (a second COM call behind `#[allow(unsafe_code)]`, which widens the one exception decision 12 made and `scripts/check-no-shortcuts.test.mjs` holds), with any answer already saved cleared. Either is tried on the workstation (`GetNonDefaultPermissionSettings` shows what the profile holds).

The second window needs a capability of its own, which lets it listen to the hardware link's events and nothing else (`capabilities/default.json` covers `main` only); an entry point of its own, with a read-only client that never starts, stops or restarts the hardware link (the app's one entry starts it and runs its restart policy); and the app ending when the main window closes (Tauri exits when the last window closes, and `shell_confirm_close` closes `main` only). "The live app" of D15 rule 4 is defined in code — a release build started with no `SSE_*` variable and without the test bridge — and anything else draws the glass into an ordinary window, never onto a real screen, which a test holds, so `tauri:dev` on the workstation never draws on the glass that stays plugged in (D20).

Finding the screen: Tauri gives each monitor only its device path (`\\.\DISPLAYn`), size, position and scale — not the name Windows shows, not the refresh rate, and nothing for a duplicated screen, which is not a monitor of its own. The shell reads Windows' display configuration (`QueryDisplayConfig`, `DisplayConfigGetDeviceInfo`): the target's name gives `Prompter XL`, the source's name maps it to Tauri's monitor, the path gives the refresh rate, and two targets on one source are `DUPLICATED`; it polls for changes, since Tauri has no display-change event. Those calls are `unsafe`, so the shell's one allowance (`set_browser_accelerator_keys_off`, pinned by `scripts/check-no-shortcuts.test.mjs`) becomes a named list, each entry with its reason, on the `windows` crate's display features. `native/tauri-shell/src/main.rs` is split first, keeping `set_browser_accelerator_keys_off` and the `.setup` block where that guard reads them. The first step confirms the route on the workstation, drawing nothing. The drift guard: a test over plain monitor snapshots, as `fullscreen_display`'s tests are, proves the window opens only on a screen named `Prompter XL`, so CI runs it.

### Slice 6a — Teleprompter: the page

Status: landed (operator verification pending — the workstation catch-up: every capture and six new ones, the twelve new boards re-seeded with contrast). Landed by #227, squash `d2cb91f` at 13:49 UTC on 2026-09-27, on the operator's go-ahead ("Merge when green, then continue"); its four commits are to be kept under the tag `archive/new-pages-s6a-2026-09` on `b033064`, whose tree `git diff` finds identical to `d2cb91f` (the catch-up pushes it from `refs/pull/227/head`: GitHub deleted the branch at the merge). The runs: the pull request's first, 36319996712 on `28a11d1`, all ten green; its last, 36323145098 on `b033064`, all ten green, and its CodeQL (36323145669) with no new alert (the check read neutral until its Rust analysis finished); `main`'s run 36323747597 on `d2cb91f`, all ten green in 8.5 min, and `main`'s CodeQL 36323747448 passed. Built in the cloud (D23), on branch `claude/gallant-goldberg-o8vsj2`, cut again from `main` at `5261565` on 2026-09-27 once #226 had merged.

**Rescope:** (2026-09-27, Slice 6's first steps, the operator's answer "Split: S6a, S6b".) Slice 6 lands as two pull requests. **Slice 6a** is the page without the editor: the Teleprompter tab, the `Prompter` lamp, the deck lamp's new word, the playing latch, the cluster, the bay with the live copy, the script bar, the cue keys and Go to paragraph, the plate (the selected script with Put on the prompter, Replace and Update, its earlier versions, the scripts, Removed, the look and the Prompter XL), Open file… and Clear the prompter, Setup / Support's row, the fixture double's scenarios, the UI contract's fixtures and `no-shortcuts.spec.ts`. **Slice 6b** is Edit script (the editor in the bay, with the quarter-size copy of the glass), Rename (settled item 7, while building), New script, Paste as a new script and the shell's clipboard permission. Until 6b, the page draws neither Edit script, Rename nor those two standing actions. Not taken: one slice, a review about twice the size of Slice 5a's with the editor, the riskiest part, inside it.

**First steps (2026-09-27), before any code,** each put to the operator with its options, costs and a recommendation, and answered the same day as recommended:

1. **One slice or two:** two, 6a and 6b (the **Rescope:** above).
2. **The editor's keys under D6** (built in 6b): the keys of any text field — typing, Enter, the arrows, selecting, copy, cut and paste, and the browser's own undo and redo, which act on the editor's history as its Undo and Redo keys do. The browser's formatting keys (Ctrl+B, Ctrl+I, Ctrl+U) are cancelled through `beforeinput`, so formatting comes only from the editor's bar. No key listener is added, and nothing on screen names a key. Not taken: the undo and redo keys cancelled too; the formatting keys kept, as in Word.
3. **The deck lamp's word:** `Surface`, as D19 and the boards have it (Lighting · Audio · Cameras · Prompter · Surface); Setup's publish step keeps its own `Deck` label. Every board moves in 6a anyway. Not taken: `Deck` kept, amending D19.
4. **How the editor is built** (6b): the app's own rich-text field over the script's model (paragraphs of runs with bold, italic and underline) on the browser's editable text, with its own undo history; no new dependency. Not taken: an editor library (ProseMirror or Lexical) with its keymaps left out, a new dependency and a guard against its keys; a plain text field, which drops the formatting bar and the cues drawn as they will look.

The Teleprompter tab (D4: after Audio; Slice 9 puts Cameras before it), the `Prompter` lamp in the header (before Surface, D19) and, while it scrolls, the green `Prompter playing · 3:12 left` latch on every page, and the page built from board 1, `A-teleprompter-1.html`, with the decisions of 2026-09-26 (D19, D20). The cluster: the state display with its armed row, `PLAY` · `BACK` · `TOP`, the speed in words a minute, the line, paragraph and cue steps, the paragraph list, and Open file… · Paste as a new script · New script (by the routes Slice 4 settled) · Clear the prompter · press twice. The bay: the live copy of the glass with the place and the time left over it, the whole-script bar, the cue keys and Go to paragraph; Edit script turns the bay into the editor and keeps a quarter-size copy of the glass in view. The plate: the selected script with Put on the prompter (Replace and Update · press twice), its earlier versions, the scripts sorted by name, Removed, the look, and the Prompter XL as Windows reports it. The live copy is the glass component scaled (the same lines, never a second layout), dimmed and marked "Not on the glass" whenever the Prompter XL shows nothing (D12 as amended); the mouse wheel never moves it. At a script's end `PLAY` stays locked until a jump moves the place back. Setup / Support › Workstation gains `Prompter XL · connected · 1920×1080 · 60 Hz`.

Boards: every board (the header gains a tab and a lamp, and the deck lamp reads `Surface`) and the Teleprompter's new ones; built in the cloud, its renders reviewed at 2560×1440 in the three themes, the win32 captures at the catch-up (D23).

**Built (2026-09-27, in the cloud).** The store and the fixture double (`frontend/packages/engine-client`, `frontend/packages/test-fixtures`):

- two snapshots in the store: `prompterSnapshot` (`prompter.snapshot`, read after every `prompter.changed` and every prompter request) and `prompterGlassSnapshot` (`prompter.glass.snapshot`, the text on the glass, read only when the first's layout key has moved, `prompterGlassIsStale`, so a play, a pause or a speed step never reads 30,000 words); their shape guards; the Teleprompter's requests on the store; `refreshPrompterSnapshot`; the page id `teleprompter`;
- the double's scenarios seed the Teleprompter (`prompter`: the scripts and the removed ones by name, the one on the glass, its place, `NOT UPDATED`, the look and the size), through the double's own requests (`prompterSeed.ts`, by a second agent); the scripts are board 1's, in `test-fixtures/src/prompterScripts.ts`, where `INTERVIEW_INTRO` moved (now board 1's own text: 18 paragraphs, 581 read words, three cues on lines of their own);
- four fixtures: `teleprompter-ready` (six scripts, two removed, `02 Interview intro` paused at ¶ 8, "with", as board 1), `teleprompter-empty`, `teleprompter-not-connected` and `teleprompter-not-updated`.

The shell and the page (`frontend/app/src/app`):

- the Teleprompter tab after Audio, its own chunk, the shell's plate region filled by a page for the first time;
- the header: the `Prompter` lamp from `checks.prompter` before the deck's, which reads `Surface`; a green latch, `Prompter playing · 2:28 left`, on every page while the text scrolls, which opens the page (`LampChip` gains the green latch; `prompterTime.ts` counts the time left down between reports);
- the page (`teleprompter/`): `teleprompterModel.ts` (what the page shows, from the hardware link's figures), the cluster (the state display and its armed row, `PLAY` · `BACK` · `TOP`, the speed, the line, paragraph and cue steps, the paragraph list, Open file… and Clear), the bay (on the glass, the place and the time left over the copy; the copy, 1,688 × 950; the whole script; the cue keys; Go to paragraph), the plate (the selected script with Put on, Replace and Update, its earlier versions and Remove; the scripts; Removed with Restore and Delete for good…; the look; the Prompter XL) and the footer;
- Setup / Support › Workstation: the Prompter XL as Windows reports it (`connected · 1920×1080 · 60 Hz`);
- the UI contract's census skips what is inside a picture (`[data-picture]`), and `ui-census.mjs` seeds a board off Windows (no screenshot, no contrast) instead of failing on the missing picture.

Settled while building, each within the design:

1. The state display reads the hardware link: the Prompter XL's words and sentences and `NOT UPDATED`'s are its own, and `ON SCREEN` and `READY` are the proposal's sentences (§8) filled with its figures.
2. The copy spans the bay at 1,688 × 950, as the proposal's §6.1 has it, without board 1's 64 px gutter of paragraph marks, which would have to follow the scroll: the place, the paragraph list and the script bar say where the reading line is.
3. The copy draws the text in the key it arrived with, the geometry that key names coming with it, and the colours, the dimming and the reading line from the current look, so it never lays one text out in another text's key.
4. While the text scrolls, the page reads the prompter's state once a second: the hardware link moves the place then without an event. The header's latch counts down on its own between reports.
5. Replace, Update and Clear are armed (D11), with the state display's armed row (`Replace with 04 Outro · press again`); Put on a blank prompter, Remove (locked while the script is on the glass) and Restore are one press; Delete for good… asks in a dialog (§10).
6. No list scrolls: the paragraph list shows 18 rows, then a window around the place; the scripts page by eight (five while the earlier versions are open above them), and the earlier versions by four, one line each (the review).
7. Rename is Slice 6b's, with the editor: it is text entry, like New script and Paste.
8. The Prompter XL section shows what Windows reports: the link, the resolution, the refresh rate, extended or a copy, a window that did not open. Board 1's brightness (not reported) and its windows' rows (Slice 5b's) are not drawn.
9. The `Prompter` lamp's word is `ready` when all is well, else the hardware link's word in the header's lower case, `pending` before the check; like every lamp it opens Setup / Support, and the latch opens the page.
10. Text printed on a well uses the display's inks (`--display-text`), so the page reads in Bone as in Studio.
11. Go to paragraph jumps only from its Go key, which is locked, with its reason, until the field holds a paragraph of the script: the field is not a form, so Enter in it does nothing (D6; the review).
12. A step the hardware link would refuse from the place is locked while paused, with the hardware link's own sentence as its reason (`Paragraph ▸` from the last paragraph, `◂ Cue` and `Cue ▸` with no cue that way), and so are the speed and the size keys at their ends; while the text scrolls the page's place is up to a second behind, so they stay open and the hardware link answers (the review).

Gates before the change: the page did not exist and `teleprompter` read as Setup; the store dropped `prompter.changed` (Slice 4's "no page reads it until Slice 6"); the header had no `Prompter` lamp and read `Deck`; the census measured the inside of pictures; the double's scenarios all started with no scripts.

The first renders found PLAY drawn as a primary key while paused, the time left without "if played from here", the look pushed to the plate's foot, the armed keys' words cut off, and every well unreadable in Bone (the chassis's dark ink on the black well); all fixed before the push (settled 10).

Review (2026-09-27, at the push of `28a11d1`): three reviewers on what CI does not cover — the records and the rules; the store and the fixture double; the page, run in a browser against the double with board 1's text, a 1,150-paragraph script of 28,750 words, 32 scripts and long names — found 3 high, 17 medium and 22 low findings. A skeptic, judging the code at `28a11d1` in the browser, confirmed eight of the mediums and highs it was given in full and four in part, and refuted none; the speed finding was checked against `commands.rs` (`step × SPEED_STEP_WPM`), and the records findings against the text they cite. Fixed in `Pages S6a (review): …`:

- **The speed and size keys moved five steps a press (high).** `step` counts the hardware link's steps, and the keys sent ±5 and ±4: 25 words a minute and 16 px a press, on the double and on the hardware link alike. They send ±1; `types.ts` and `v1.md` say what a step is, and a Playwright case reads both readouts after a press.
- **Clear moved the selection to the first script (high).** The selected script fell back to the one on the prompter, then the first, so Clear then Put on put another script on, and Clear then Remove (whose lock says "Clear the prompter first") removed another. The script last on the prompter stays selected.
- **The script bar spilled past about 165 paragraphs (high).** Each segment's padding, floor and gap outgrew the bar, while the ticks, the place and a press used the shares, so a press on the drawn ¶ 51 went to ¶ 354. The segments are now placed by the same shares (`scriptBar`, once a text); past 120 paragraphs the bar is drawn as the part read and the part to read. Its inks are board 1's, the display's own, so the part read shows in Bone too (a low).
- **A failed prompter read failed the whole start (medium).** The hardware link keeps a fault of the prompter's alone from stopping the start (`health.rs`), but the store read the prompter with everything else. The two prompter reads now go beside the rest; their failure is recorded, and the page reads again once a second until an answer comes ("Reading the prompter's state…"). A test starts with both refused.
- **A failed read of the glass's text was never tried again (medium).** The copy kept the old text in the old key, never reported the new one, and `PLAY` stayed locked. The page reads the prompter's state once a second while the text is not laid out, and the store reads the text whenever its key has moved; the start reads the text again when its two reads disagree (a low).
- **The copy was cut by 16 px (medium):** the page's bay padded inside the shell's; the padding is gone.
- **`BACK`'s hint named the wrong paragraph at a paragraph's start (medium).** It reads where `BACK` goes as the hardware link works it out, from the lines the copy reported (`backParagraph`).
- **Hidden cue keys could take the focus (medium).** Only the keys that fit three rows are drawn, and a long cue is cut with an ellipsis.
- **The armed rows ran past the state display (medium).** The way out's own key says it is armed, so the armed row is not drawn for it; a name in the armed row is cut at 20 characters, so `· press again` stays in view.
- **A failure could show the hardware link's plumbing (medium).** A rejection that is not a sentence (the shell's own timeout names `engine` and the method) is shown as the page's sentence; a file over 20 MB is refused with the hardware link's own sentence before it is read, and a file that cannot be read says so.
- **The versions list stopped at 5 of 20, and opening it hid the scripts' pager (medium).** Settled 6 above.
- **Keys at their limits stayed open (medium, partly held):** settled 12. **A long window error squeezed the Prompter XL's readouts (medium, partly held; only Slice 5b's shell sends one):** it wraps under them. **The page walked the whole script on every report (medium, partly held):** the read words are counted once a text, the bar is worked out from the text alone, and the paragraph rows are kept between reports.
- The records (6 medium): Rename joins 6b's scope wherever 6b is named; the reading-line question moved to 6b's first steps (Slice 5a's glass review); the `Status:` base; the test changes above (7, 8, and the Tests added); the board and fixture counts in `AGENTS.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`, `docs/HANDOFF.md` and the system's §10; `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`'s front-end map and fixtures, and the next-session page.
- Low, fixed: the header's playing latch shows only while the hardware link answers (it counted on after a crash); the guards check the objects the page reads (`look`, `screen`, `glass` and its cues, the text's look); Enter in Go to paragraph (settled 11); the place never reads 100 % short of the end; `1 paragraph`, `1 cue`; the current paragraph row is `aria-current`; choosing another script drops an armed Replace; the catch-up's capture list names the shell's nine Storybook captures and the visual README's counts; `workspaceChunks.ts`'s comment; `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` §2c on seeding off Windows.
- Low, not taken: each read of the prompter's state makes the hardware link compare the script with the glass's text for `NOT UPDATED` (the page reads it once a second only while playing or waiting; for Slices 5b and 7 to know); the glass's text keeps the name it went on with after a rename (Rename is 6b's, and the page reads the name from the state); for one reply after Replace the new script's figures are drawn over the old text (a blank list would flash instead); `PLAY`'s hint while locked (its reason is the key's description, and its material says it); the plate's length from the words beside the strip's from the layout (both the hardware link's); the shell's and the page's one-second timers (each small); the records commit coming first (harmless once squashed).

### Slice 6b — Teleprompter: the editor, Rename, New script and Paste

Status: landed (operator verification pending — the workstation catch-up: two new captures, the six new boards re-seeded with contrast, and on Windows the main window as the shell builds it, the clipboard read with no prompt and the editor's keys in WebView2). Then continue according to plan."); its thirteen commits are to be kept under the tag `archive/new-pages-s6b-2026-09` on `878e447`, whose tree `git diff` finds identical to `d86de87` (the catch-up pushes it from `refs/pull/228/head`: GitHub deleted the branch at the merge). The runs: the pull request's first, on `1173c81`, all ten green; its last, 36333051288 on `878e447`, all ten green, and its CodeQL (36333052188) with no new alert; `main`'s run 36333620990 on `d86de87`, all ten green in 8.4 min, and `main`'s CodeQL 36333620258 passed. Built and pushed on 2026-09-27, in the cloud (D23), on branch `claude/gallant-goldberg-o8vsj2`, cut again from `main` at `d2cb91f` on 2026-09-27 once #227 had merged.

**Rescope:** split from Slice 6 at its first steps (Slice 6a's **Rescope:**): Edit script (the editor in the bay, with the quarter-size copy of the glass), Rename (Slice 6a's settled item 7: text entry, like New script and Paste), New script, Paste as a new script and the shell's clipboard permission.

**First steps (2026-09-27), before any code,** each put to the operator with its options, costs and a recommendation, and answered the same day as recommended (the editor's keys and the app's own editor were answered at Slice 6's first steps, above):

1. **The line at the reading line** (Slice 5a's glass review, settled item 7): while the text scrolls it moves on to the next line halfway through a line's words, while the place and `BACK` still count the earlier line; paused, the line sits centred, as board 1 draws it. Kept as it is. Not taken: the counted line drawn with its top on the reading line (paused text half a line lower than board 1); the hardware link counting the place from a line's centre (a change to the clock's contract, the double and their tests).
2. **Rename:** board 1's Rename key in the plate's head opens a small dialog with the name field (the one Lighting uses for scenes), Save and Cancel, whether or not the editor is open. Not taken: the name edited in the editor's bar, which needs the editor open.
3. **The editor's own Paste** (the key, and a paste into the editor): what was copied keeps its bold, italic, underline, paragraphs and headings as cues, read by the hardware link's own reader through one new request, `prompter.paste.convert { html?, text? }`, the same rules as Paste as a new script; it creates no script. Not taken: plain text only at the cursor (no contract change; the formatting added again with the editor's bar).

Also in 6b, from the proposal and not a new decision: opening a file whose name was opened before offers a dialog, update that script from the file (its old text kept as a version) or add it as a new script (§3.3; `prompter.script.import`'s `updateScriptId`, Slice 4). Slice 6a's Open file… always added a new script.

The editor binds no key and takes no editor library's keymap (D6): the browser's own formatting keys in a rich-text field (Ctrl+B, Ctrl+I, Ctrl+U) are cancelled through `beforeinput`, and its undo and redo act on the editor's history like the on-screen keys (Slice 6's first step 2, answered on 2026-09-27). `no-shortcuts.spec.ts` gains the focused editor (the page joins it in 6a), and the editor's states join the UI contract as fixtures. The shell grants WebView2's clipboard permission to the main window only (`WebviewWindowBuilder::enable_clipboard_access`, the window built in code from its `tauri.conf.json` entry), and a test holds that no other window gets it; it is checked for Windows in the cloud and tried on Windows at the catch-up (Part C's **Rescope:**). The editor is the app's own (first step 4). Boards: the four of 6a (the bay's head gains Live copy · Edit script, the standing row Paste as a new script and New script, the plate's head Rename) and the Teleprompter's new ones (the editor open, a new script); built in the cloud, its renders reviewed at 2560×1440 in the three themes, the win32 captures at the catch-up (D23).

**Built (2026-09-27, in the cloud).** The hardware link, the contract and the fixture double (by a second agent):

- `prompter.paste.convert { html?, text? }` answers `{ paragraphs, sentence }`: what a paste into the editor inserts, read by the paste's own reader (`read_paste`, now shared with `prompter.script.paste`), in the shape `prompter.script.edit` takes. It makes no script, keeps no version, raises no event and is not a Recent action; its refusals are the paste's. `v1.md`, the contract and the generated bindings follow; the double answers the same request, and its HTML reader's emphasis rules were brought back in line with the hardware link's, from which they had drifted (`<cite>` italic, `<ins>` not underlined, a style's `!important` ignored, font weights and styles as the Rust reader reads them). Its reader is still simpler than the hardware link's in ways the review measured (30 inputs through both, 7 different): tracked changes (`<del>`) kept and not named, text inside `svg` and `object` kept, Windows' clipboard header read as a paragraph, Word's comment and footnote marks kept and not counted, `&mdash;` and `&hellip;` not decoded, and `<pre>` line breaks joined. None is in what a browser puts on the clipboard in the tests, and none was introduced here.

The shell (by a third agent, checked for Windows in the session: `cargo check` and `cargo clippy -D warnings` for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`):

- the main window is `"create": false` in `tauri.conf.json` and is built first in `.setup`, from its own entry, by `shell_windows.rs`: Tauri's `WebviewWindowBuilder::from_config`, with `enable_clipboard_access()` for the main window only (`may_read_clipboard`), so WebView2 grants the page's clipboard reads there; the Prompter XL's window (Slice 5b) is to be built through the same function and gets no permission of its own. The review found the grant is not the main window's alone at run time: wry's handler allows the read without `SetSavesInProfile(false)`, WebView2 saves the answer in the profile for the origin, across sessions, and every window of the app shares one profile (Tauri's one user data folder) and one origin; with one window there is nothing to reach today, and Slice 5b's gate keeps the glass's window out. Everything after the window exists runs as before, in the same order. `main.rs` 1,828 → 1,834 lines.

The page (`frontend/app/src/app/teleprompter/`):

- **Edit script**, the bay's second view beside Live copy (board 1): the selected script's editor (`editor/ScriptEditor.tsx`), and beside it what the glass shows now, a quarter of its size (the same glass component at 480 px), the place, what was edited since it went on, whether the paragraph at the reading line was, and what the edits change on the glass (`TeleprompterEditView.tsx`);
- the editor: the bar (Bold · Italic · Underline · Add cue · Undo · Redo · Paste), the text on a black well with paragraph numbers in a gutter, the paragraph at the reading line and every paragraph the glass does not show as it is marked there (the script on the prompter), cues in the cue colour, and under it the words, paragraphs, cues, the length at the script's speed and `Saved 14:02`; saved as the operator types (§3.3), a moment after the last change;
- the cluster's standing actions: Open file… · Paste as a new script · New script, and Clear the prompter under them (board 1); New script opens the new script in the editor, Paste as a new script selects the pasted one;
- **Rename** in the plate's head: a dialog with the name (Lighting's, moved to `app/shared/RenameDialog.tsx`);
- a file opened again (§3.3): a dialog — update that script from the file (its text kept as a version), add it as a new script, or cancel;
- the store: `createPrompterScript`, `renamePrompterScript`, `editPrompterScript`, `pastePrompterScript` and `convertPrompterPaste` (which refreshes nothing); the page reads the clipboard itself (`clipboard.ts`);
- two fixtures, `teleprompter-editing` (the NOT UPDATED scenario, the editor open) and `teleprompter-new-script` (the empty one, New script pressed), and the UI contract's way to a board a page reaches by a press (`BOARD_STEPS` in `tests/helpers/ui-contract/boards.mjs`, used by the census, the contract and `visual-review.spec.ts`).

Settled while building, each within the design:

1. **The browser makes the edits, and the editor reads the script back from the page after each one**, so the browser's own undo and redo (Ctrl+Z, Ctrl+Y) and the bar's Undo and Redo are one history, as first step 2 has it. The first build took every change in `beforeinput` and drew the text again from its own model; tried in Chromium before the push, the browser then sent no undo at all — it sends `historyUndo` only when its own undo stack holds a step, and nothing the editor cancels puts one there — so Ctrl+Z did nothing. A key listener, the other way to undo, is what D6 forbids. The editor now keeps the browser's own markup (`<p>`, `<b>`, `<i>`, `<u>`, `<br>`) and reads it, styles that turn a format off included (`editorDom.ts`). Slice 6's first step 4 said the editor keeps "its own undo history": that history is now the field's own, in the browser, which the bar's Undo and Redo drive, and the script's model is read back from the page after each edit — a history kept apart from the browser's left its undo keys doing nothing, against first step 2. The choice step 4 answered, the app's own field on the browser's editable text with no library and no new dependency, is unchanged (found at the review: a correction of that answer's wording, not of the choice).
2. What the browser may not do is still cancelled in `beforeinput`: its formatting keys (Ctrl+B, Ctrl+I, Ctrl+U and the others), lists, links and drops. The bar formats through the browser's own commands, which `beforeinput` does not see, so they go into the same undo.
3. A paste (Ctrl+V, or the bar's Paste) is cancelled and read by the hardware link (`prompter.paste.convert`), then inserted where the caret was as one step of undo. The reader's sentence is shown only when it left something out (a picture, a comment).
4. The cues are coloured with the browser's highlights (the CSS Custom Highlight API), never in the text's markup, so the browser's undo never meets markup the editor wrote.
5. Before any request the page saves what was typed (the editor's `flush`), so Update, Put on, Replace, Bring back and Remove act on the text the operator sees; the editor reads the script again when it changed elsewhere (a version brought back, the file opened again, a backup restored) and nothing typed is waiting.
6. The editor's field scrolls, as board 1 draws it ("opens scrolled to the reading line"; its status line ends "· the text scrolls"): the one thing on the page that does; the page never scrolls. A script runs to 30,000 words (the proposal §3.2), which no fixed field holds, and paging an editable text would split its selection and undo; the lists stay paged (Slice 6a's settled item 6). It opens at the reading line for the script on the prompter.
7. "Edited" marks each paragraph whose text and formatting no paragraph of the glass's text has; the paragraph at the reading line is found in the edited text by its words, nearest where it was. Board 1's "What Update changes on the glass" panel, each changed paragraph before and after, is not drawn: the list and the gutter's marks say which paragraphs, and the glass beside the editor shows what the presenter reads now.
8. The quarter-size copy reports its layout like the live copy (both lay out at 1,920 px), so a size step made while editing is laid out, and `PLAY` does not wait for the live copy.
9. The editor's field has no spell check: a script is proofread where it is written, and the red lines would sit under names and cues.
10. The bar's keys keep the focus in the text, so a selection they act on stays selected.
11. A file is "opened before" when a kept script came from a file of that name; a removed one does not count (it can be restored).

Gates before the change: the page drew no Edit script, New script, Paste as a new script or Rename, and Open file… always added a script; the store could not create, rename, edit or paste a script; the hardware link had no way to read a paste without making a script; the shell's main window was made by Tauri with no clipboard access; the UI contract measured a fixture's first render only.

The shell's change was also checked for Windows: `cargo check` and `cargo clippy -D warnings` for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`;

Review (2026-09-27, at the push of `1173c81`): three reviewers on what CI does not cover — the records and the rules; the hardware link, the contract, the fixture double, the store and the shell (against the Tauri and wry sources); the page, run in Chromium against the double — and a skeptic for each medium or high finding. They found 1 high, 9 medium and about 20 low; the skeptics upheld the high and four of the mediums (Rename's in part), and found the other five real but low: the records' four, and the clipboard's, which has nothing to reach with one window.

- **A text the hardware link would not save was dropped when the page moved on (high).** The editor's save caught its refusal, so the page went on: choosing another script or Live copy closed the editor with no word, and New script, Put on, Update and the rest went ahead, Put on and Update with the last saved text. Now the editor's `flush` answers whether the text was saved; `perform` does not go ahead from text that was not (the editor has said why), choosing another script and Live copy wait for the save too, a refusal is said even as the editor closes, and text that could not be saved is kept until it is saved (`unsavedTexts`), so a change of workspace, which cannot wait, opens on it again. The take's own controls (play and pause, the jumps, the speed, the look, Clear) neither wait for the save nor stop for it (`TAKE`, `perform.ts`).
- **Rename read the script again and threw away the editor's undo (medium; only Rename, of all that changes a script's time of change without its text).** The editor now reads the script when it changed elsewhere and keeps itself as it is when the text is the same (`sameText`); it also leaves the text alone when the operator typed while the read was on its way.
- **The readouts missed removed and reordered paragraphs (medium).** A deleted paragraph read as "Edited since it went on: nothing", and a deleted paragraph at the reading line as "unchanged", its mark moved to another. `compareWithGlass` (`editorModel.ts`) matches each paragraph of the glass once: the text's unmatched ones are edited, the paragraphs fewer are "removed", the order is said when only it changed, and the reading line's paragraph is "unchanged", "edited" or "removed" (then no paragraph is marked for it).
- **The cues' highlight cost cues × paragraphs a keystroke (medium).** Each cue's place rebuilt the list of paragraphs; with 1,000 paragraphs and a cue in each, each keystroke made a frame of 424–480 ms. Each cue is now placed within its own paragraph's element, from the editor's own reading of it: no frame over 50 ms on the same script.
- **Repeated pastes into the editor passed the 2 MB cap on a script's text (medium).** `prompter.script.edit` is capped as an import is, by its text as well as its words, and its word refusal counts as the import's does ("30,001 … 30,000"); the double and `v1.md` follow.
- **WebView2 keeps the main window's clipboard answer for every window (medium, low today).** Recorded above (the build) and in Slice 4's first-steps line; Slice 5b gains the gate. The review's commit `17363ed` first replaced wry's handler with the shell's own, which never saves the answer; CI's `scripts:test` failed on it ("the shell names unsafe_code once"), since it is a second COM call behind `#[allow(unsafe_code)]`, and widening decision 12's one exception is the operator's call, so the shell went back to its reviewed form and the choice is put to the operator at Slice 5b's first steps.
- The records (four medium, found low by the skeptic): settled items 1 and 6 cite what they rest on (first step 2's promise, and board 1's scrolling field); the Boards line and the renders name 6a's four boards, which this slice moves (rendered in the three themes at the review); the catch-up's doc counts gain `docs/HANDOFF.md`'s two capture counts, and its Playwright count is 423 now.
- Low, fixed: the bar's Paste and Add cue say "Click in the text where it goes first." when the caret was never there (they inserted at the top, out of view); Add cue across two paragraphs is refused with a sentence (it made a broken cue and an empty paragraph); `flush` waits for every save on its way, not just one; Rename's field takes the hardware link's 80 characters and the dialog is "Rename the script"; the double's HTML reader is recorded as simpler than the hardware link's where the review measured it; the slice's title names Rename; the CHANGELOG's promise of what Update changes; the front-end map's list of what `beforeinput` cancels; the Windows catch-up items for Ctrl+V and the cancelled formatting keys; `system-a` §10's date; Slice 6b's Status.
- Low, not taken: `prompter.paste.convert` runs under the prompter's lock and settles its clock (a small cost, and one path for every prompter request); each save reads the prompter again, and the plate reads the script again when its time of change moves (a cost while typing, not a fault); a failed read after a saved edit reads as a failed save (the store's `performRequest`, as in Slice 6a); the shell's tests hold its rule and config, not what WebView2 keeps (Slice 5b's gate); the status line counts the words read, and the limit every word, cues too (as an import's limit does).
- CI: `rust-coverage` failed at `17363ed` on Slice 4's `a_paragraph_of_many_cue_lines_counts_in_one_pass` (5.2 s against its 5 s in the instrumented build; it had failed twice under `dev:check`'s parallel run in Slice 6a). Its lines are now 60 characters, not 600: each quadratic pass it guards against is still tens of billions of steps, and it takes 0.31 s in a debug build.

Tests added by the review, each failing without its fix (the four Playwright cases against a build of the page without the review's changes; the Rust and double cases on the old `edit`): `teleprompter.spec.ts` "a text that cannot be saved keeps the editor open on it, and the page does not move on", "Rename keeps the editor's text and its undo", "a paragraph removed from the script on the prompter is counted, the reading line's too" and "the bar's Paste and Add cue ask for the caret in the text first"; `editorModel.test.ts` "counts the glass's paragraphs the text no longer holds, a second copy as added, and a new order" and "knows the same text"; `an_edit_is_capped_as_an_import_is_by_its_text_as_well_as_its_words` (`tests_scripts.rs`); and the double's edit case gains the text cap (`prompterScripts.test.ts`, failing on the old reader of an edit).

Tests changed by the review (old → new → reason): `editorModel.test.ts`'s two comparison cases, `editedParagraphs` and `readingLineIn` → `compareWithGlass`, which replaces both (the reading line's edited paragraph is now found between its neighbours' matches, and a removed one is said); `prompterScripts.test.ts`'s edit refusal, "30001 … 30000" → "30,001 … 30,000", counted as the import's; `model.rs`'s one-pass test, 600 → 60 characters a line, above.

### Slice 7 — The PROMPTER deck page

Status: planned.

**Rescope:** (2026-09-27, Part C's re-cut) built with Slice 12 as one slice, in the cloud, after Slice 9: the two deck pages share the `exports.rs` split, the page chain (D5) and Setup's deck steps, which then change once. It lands as one pull request, `Pages S7+S12: …`, and both slices' Status lines record it.

D14 as D20 settled it: the dials are speed (5 words a minute a detent; a push plays or pauses), position (a line a detent), text size (4 px a detent; a push returns to the standard) and paragraph; the keys are `PLAY` (lit green while scrolling), `BACK`, `TOP`, `◂ CUE`, `CUE ▸` and the page key in the chain (D5); the touch strip shows the speed, the place, the time left and the script's name. `PLAY` and the speed dial's push go the deck's locked grey while nothing is drawn on the glass (`checks.prompter.screen.draws` false: `NOT CONNECTED`, `DUPLICATED`, `NOT SHOWING`; Slice 5a), and at a script's end until a jump moves the place back (D19), and every prompter control does while nothing is on the prompter; putting a script on, replacing, updating and clearing it stay on the screen. The page's word on the deck is the one the hardware link accepts, `teleprompter`. The bridge's actions and feedback, and the Companion export (`exports.rs`, split first); the profile is exported and imported at the close-out (D5). Boards: Setup's deck steps, which draw every page the deck has (the new page's keys and dials); built with the other deck page on the workstation, its win32 captures and contrast made in-slice (Part C's second **Rescope:**; it was to be built in the cloud, its captures at the catch-up).

The merge's eleven commits are kept under the tag `archive/new-pages-s8-2026-09` on `ad5b613`, whose tree is `4c2d4bb`'s, pushed from the workstation after the merge (GitHub deleted the branch at the merge). Built and pushed on 2026-09-27, in the cloud (D23), on branch `claude/gallant-goldberg-o8vsj2`, cut again from `main` at `d86de87` on 2026-09-27 once #228 had merged.

**First steps (2026-09-27), before any code,** each put to the operator with its options, costs and a recommendation, and answered the same day as recommended:

1. **One slice or two:** one pull request, as Slice 4 was — the model, the three simulated cameras, the `cameras.*` methods and event, `checks.cameras`, Recent actions, schema 10, the backup's format 7, the drift guard and the fixture double. A camera exists only once Setup holds it, so the saved data does not come apart from the model. Not taken: 8a (the model with its settings in memory) then 8b (schema 10, format 7, Setup's methods, the guard), whose 8a settings would be a stopgap.
2. **Which vMix input carries which camera:** set in Setup's camera step, per camera, and kept with its address (schema 10 and the backup), `1`, `2` and `3` until the operator changes them, so a vMix preset whose inputs move costs one number rather than a picture under another camera's name. Setup's step is drawn in Slice 9. Not taken: fixed, input N is CAM N, as the boards assume.
3. **How far a camera raises the whole status:** attention at most, as the Prompter XL's state does (Slice 5a): a camera set up in Setup and held that does not answer (`UNREACHABLE`). A camera not set up yet, or released to the iPad or LUMIX Tether, lights the Cameras lamp only. Not taken: the lamp only; any camera fault taking the whole status to degraded (a camera switched off between takes would read as the app degraded).
4. **What the simulated cameras report** until Slices 11 and 13 confirm the real links (the question named Slices 12 and 13; the Pocket's link is Slice 11's, the BGH1s' Slice 13's — corrected at the review, the answer unchanged): board 2's assumptions — CAM 1 (the Pocket 6K Pro) every value D10 lists (a focus position, tint, dynamic range, the display LUT, recording and timecode) but its card time, "not reported", as Slice 11 (D18) says the Bluetooth protocol does not carry it (noted at the review); CAM 2 and CAM 3 (the BGH1s) no tint, focus position, ND, dynamic range or LUT, and no recording. Each camera's model says what it reports, so Slices 11 and 13 narrow or widen it without the page changing. Not taken: every D10 value on every camera.

The three cameras in the hardware link: CAM 1, the Pocket 6K Pro, over Bluetooth, and CAM 2 and CAM 3, the BGH1s, at the addresses entered in Setup (schema 10: the addresses and the Pocket's pairing; new saved data holds none, D15 rule 1). Which vMix input carries which camera is decided here — fixed (input N is CAM N, as the boards assume) or set in Setup's camera step and kept with the addresses. For each camera: who holds it (held or released, D13), every value D10 lists as the camera reports it, and "not reported" where it cannot; one selected camera, shared by the page's pictures, the plate and the deck's dials (D19); what is one press and what is armed (D11); `REC` on CAM 1 only (D14). `cameras` joins the pages the hardware link accepts, as Slice 4 added `teleprompter`.

The simulated cameras that every test, lane and scratch run uses (D15 rules 1–2): `laneProcessEnv` forces them on every lane, as it forces the simulated console (S2b). The `cameras.*` methods and a `cameras.changed` event; a `checks.cameras` section of the health snapshot for the header's lamp, the summary and the fixture double, a camera that does not answer reading `UNREACHABLE` (D19), and whether that changes the whole status put to the operator at the slice's start (proposed: no worse than attention); Recent actions for record start and stop, the armed changes, Release and Connect, with Screen or Stream Deck. The backup archive goes to format 7 with the camera addresses (it is a fixed list, so Setup's addresses would not be backed up otherwise). Starting, opening the page, reconnecting, restarting and restoring a backup send nothing to a camera, and the camera wins a disagreement (D12). The drift guard: a test build refuses a camera address that is not on this PC. Boards: none.

**Built (2026-09-27, in the cloud).** The contract first (`v1.md`'s "Cameras" section: fourteen `cameras.*` methods, the `cameras.changed` event, `checks.cameras` and twelve refusal codes, with the snapshot shapes exported to TypeScript), then the hardware link and the fixture double in parallel against it and one shared list of every value and sentence (not committed: `model.rs` and `camerasWords.ts` hold it, and `camerasWords.test.ts` keeps the two together).

The hardware link (`native/rust-engine/src/cameras/`):

- the three fixed cameras (`model.rs`): CAM 1, the Pocket 6K Pro, over Bluetooth; CAM 2 and CAM 3, the BGH1s, over the network; each camera's model says what it reports and the words for what it does not (first step 4: board 2's assumptions);
- the simulated cameras (`simulated.rs`), inside the hardware link, touching no network and no radio: they count what they are sent, and the tests can change a value on "the body", start or stop a take, stop them answering and set their timecode clock; `SSE_CAMERAS_SIMULATED=1` selects them (read at the start, `bootstrap.rs`); without it a set-up camera reads `UNREACHABLE`, "Studio Control has no link to CAM n yet", and pairing CAM 1 is `CAMERA_NO_LINK` (`real_link.rs`, with the drift guard: a test build refuses every camera address but `127.0.0.1`, `::1` and `localhost`);
- holding (`runtime.rs`): every set-up camera is held at the start, CAM 1 selected; before each `cameras.*` request the held cameras are read again, and a value changed on the body, a camera that stops answering and one that answers again are said then (`reported`, `unreachable`, `reachable`); the real links add a watch between requests (Slices 11 and 13);
- the fourteen methods (`commands.rs`), their refusals in one order (`v1.md`), the snapshot and `checks.cameras` (`report.rs`), Setup's part of the saved data (`store.rs`, schema 10: `camera_setup`, one row a camera) and of the backup (`archive.rs`, format 7: the addresses and vMix inputs; CAM 1's pairing stays with the PC);
- the hooks: fourteen literal arms and `dispatch_cameras` in `app.rs`, the event (`engine_events.rs`), `checks.cameras` and the whole status in `health.rs` (attention at most, only for a set-up camera that is held and does not answer; the summary ends ` Cameras: …` after ` Prompter: …`), Recent actions (`action_log.rs`: the record's start and stop, the format, the look, Release, and Connect when the camera is held again), `cameras` among the pages the hardware link accepts (`shell_settings.rs`);
- the lanes (`scripts/native-runtime-harness.mjs`): every lane sets `SSE_CAMERAS_SIMULATED=1`, the live-console lane included, and an environment without it is refused; `native-parity-acceptance.mjs` expects format 7; `tests/end_to_end.rs` sets it.

The fixture double (`frontend/packages/engine-client/src/transports/fixture/cameras*.ts`): the same cameras, requests, refusals, events, check, whole status, Recent actions rows and format-7 backup part, built from the hardware link's files; a scenario seeds the cameras (`FixtureCamerasSeed`: which are set up, released or unreachable, CAM 1 recording, the selection, `simulated: false` for the "no link yet" state), checked by `test-fixtures/src/check-fixtures.mjs`; with no seed every camera is NOT SET UP (D15 rule 1). Its tests read the hardware link's sentences, codes, option lists, starting values and action names out of the Rust files, so a sentence, code or value changed on one side only fails (the guard as the review rebuilt it, below). The store maps `cameras.changed` to no domain (the health refresh follows its `app.changed`); its cameras reads come with the page (Slice 9).

Settled while building, each within the design; 1–7 and 10 recorded in `v1.md`, 8 and 9 in the code (`simulated.rs`, `real_link.rs`):

1. A body change, a camera that stops answering and one that answers again are said at the next `cameras.*` request, when every held camera is read again; a watch between requests comes with the real links (Slices 11 and 13), which alone can change without a request.
2. A change that moves nothing (the same value, a step at an end) is still sent to the camera and still raises its event: the camera answers it. Selecting the camera already selected sends nothing, as no selection reaches a camera (D12), and still raises `cameras.changed { reason: "select" }`.
3. Every applied archive restore raises `cameras.changed { reason: "restore" }`, as it raises the prompter's, a format 6 or older included (which leaves the cameras' setup as it was).
4. `support.backup.verify` names the part: "…, with N scripts and the cameras' setup."
5. A Recent actions row keeps the sentence's full stop, as the operator read it; Connect leaves a row only when the camera is held again.
6. The answers: set, step and auto `{ camera, setting, value }` (the camera's new report); format and look `{ camera, sentence }`; the record `{ camera: 1, recording, sentence }`; Release and Connect `{ camera, state, sentence }`; Setup's `{ camera, setup }`.
7. Setup: a blank address, an address for CAM 1 and `vmixInput: null` are `INVALID_PARAMS`; an address is kept in its plain form (`010.0.0.1` is `10.0.0.1`); a step is a whole number from −1000 to 1000, not 0.
8. The simulated one-shot autos settle at focus 0.5, 5,600 K and f/4.0; the timecode runs from this PC's clock (UTC); `startedAt` is `null` for a take that was running when the camera was first read after a start, a Connect or an unreachable spell.
9. In a test build, a camera the drift guard refuses reads the guard's own sentence as its `UNREACHABLE` sentence.
10. The health event follows any change in the check — a camera's line, CAM 1's recording — not only a change of the worst state.
11. Release and the selection are kept in memory: a start holds every set-up camera again, with CAM 1 selected (D13: Studio Control holds the cameras whenever it runs); the vMix input is 1–1000; `cameras.setup.forget` keeps the vMix input. Once the real links exist, a start takes a released camera back from the iPad or LUMIX Tether without a press — the other side of D18's open check — so Slices 11 and 13 put it to the operator with that check (found at the review).

Gates before the change: the hardware link had no cameras, refused the page word `cameras`, had schema 9 and the backup's format 6; the lanes set no simulated cameras; the double had no cameras.
