### Slice 9 — Cameras: the page, against the simulated cameras

Status: planned — its first steps answered by the operator on 2026-09-28; it starts once the workstation catch-up has landed.

The Cameras tab (D4: between Audio and Teleprompter); the header's five lamps in the tab order with the deck last (Lighting · Audio · Cameras · Prompter · Surface) and the `REC` chip while CAM 1 records; the page built from board 2, `A-cameras-2.html`, with D19's decisions: the selected camera big (the whole frame at 1680 × 945, or 1:1) and the other two small; one selection (board 2's look-only state goes); recording drawn as a red lamp and the word `REC`, like 48 V, never a red fill (system-a §4's red list gains it); the aids' switches (framing guides, zebras and focus peaking, and the 2:1 / 4:1 loupe beside the small pictures; no false colour, no waveform); Release · press twice; the plate's values, one press each, and the formats armed. The pictures are drawn into a canvas, which any route Slice 10 takes can feed; here they are still test pictures (D15 rule 3; the captures mask them). Setup's camera step: the BGH1s' addresses, the Pocket's pairing (`CAMERA_NO_LINK` on the live app until Slice 11) and each camera's vMix input (Slice 8's first step 2), drawn against the simulated cameras; the store's `cameras.snapshot` read and a mapping for `cameras.changed` (Slice 8 maps it to nothing), and `cameras` seeds in `fixtures.json` (none exist; an unseeded camera reads NOT SET UP). A first step from Slice 8's review, to put to the operator before this slice's code: until Slice 13, a network camera whose address is entered has no link and reads `UNREACHABLE`, which raises the whole status to attention (Slice 8's first step 3), so a build without Slice 13 would sit at attention once an address is entered — (a) as built; (b) "no link yet" lights the lamp only; (c, proposed) Setup refuses an address with `CAMERA_NO_LINK` while the build has no network link, as pairing CAM 1 already does, so the state cannot arise. **Answered by the operator on 2026-09-27, during Slice 8's review: (c)** ("Go with (c) for Slice 9"): Slice 9 makes `cameras.setup.update` refuse an address with `CAMERA_NO_LINK` while the build has no network link (the simulated cameras take one), in the hardware link and the double, and Setup's step says why. Also from Slice 8's review, for this slice to settle as it draws them: the `REC` chip while CAM 1 is released (the hardware link does not read it then, so `checks.cameras.recording` is `false` while the take may go on); Connect on a camera shown `UNREACHABLE` that answers again by the press, which is `CAMERA_ALREADY_HELD` (the read before the request held it again, and `reachable` said so); and a hook on the double for Playwright to make a camera change a value, stop answering or answer again while the page is open (Slice 8 exports none). `no-shortcuts.spec.ts` gains the page. Boards: every board (the header) and the Cameras' and Setup's new ones, finished on the workstation, its win32 captures and contrast made in-slice (the **Rescope:** below; it was to be built in the cloud, its captures at the catch-up).

**Rescope:** (2026-09-27, evening, Part C's second **Rescope:**; the scope from its first steps, 2026-09-28) built on the studio workstation, not in the cloud: its boards' win32 captures and Windows' contrast in-slice, by the Baseline refresh procedure. It is no longer the front end only (Program shape): the (c) refusal of an address, answered during Slice 8's review, and the camera-only read of Recent actions (first step 3) are hardware-link, contract and fixture-double work. One slice, one pull request, `Pages S9: …` (first step 1).

**First steps (2026-09-28),** checked before they were put against board 2, D10–D19 and `v1.md`'s Cameras section (three readers, a synthesis and a skeptic, read only), each put with its options, costs and a recommendation, and answered by the operator: "Go with your recommendations on all of it".

1. **One slice**, reviewed by area as Slice 3's was, not the cloud's draft of two (9a: the page, the header, the store, the seeds and the hook; 9b: Setup's part and the (c) refusal). With the captures made in-slice again, a split would refresh Setup's captures twice and add a CI run and a review round; Slice 6 was split to keep its riskiest part, the editor, apart, and the draft's 9b was the smallest part. Its size should come near Slices 6a and 6b together (about 9,000 added lines), well under Slice 4's. Not taken: the draft's split; a first half that moves no board (the store and the hook with no page to exercise them).
2. **The `REC` chip while CAM 1 is released:** an amber chip, `REC` with "not read while released", whenever CAM 1 is released, read from CAM 1's state in `checks.cameras` — the front end only, no contract change. It is D10's rule read literally (a value a camera cannot report says so), and the only answer that also covers a take started from the iPad after the Release. Not taken: (a) the chip goes out, as built and as board 2 draws its `released-main` state — the header would then say nothing of a take that may go on; (c) a "last known" chip only when CAM 1 was recording at the Release — the hardware link would keep a mark across the Release, against `v1.md`'s rule that a released camera shows nothing, and a take started from the iPad would still go unseen.
3. **Recent actions on the page:** a camera-only read of the action log (a small addition to the hardware link, `v1.md` and the double), since `support.snapshot` carries the newest fifty rows of every domain and a busy Console could push the cameras' rows out. Not taken: the shared fifty.

Settled within the design, named with the first steps and taken with them:

- "Try CAM n again", the plate's "Try again" and "Read all cameras again" are one fresh `cameras.snapshot`, which reads all three cameras again; Connect stays for a released camera. The read raises no event of its own and leaves no row, so the page says it tried when nothing changes. A `CAMERA_ALREADY_HELD` from Connect on a camera the page still shows released is worded as already held, and the page reads again; it is not a fault. The page and its tests read again only through `cameras.*`: the hardware link's `health.snapshot` reads the cameras again, the double's does not (a drift, recorded here).
- The Recent list shows the log's camera rows only — the take's start and stop, the format, the look, Release, and Connect when the camera is held again (Appendix B item 10) — each with the log's sentence, and Screen only until S7+S12. Board 2's one-press, "On the camera", "found at Connect" and "At start" rows go: nothing records them. The recorded `cameras.*` requests refresh the list.
- D19: board 2's false colour and waveform go, the loupe stands alone in the picture check, and the board's "Proposal" marks go.
- The deck's bank section waits for S7+S12, which gains the page's "what the dials set" section and a home for the bank that the page and the deck both read (Slice 12); the hardware link has no bank yet.
- The pictures stay test pictures in a canvas (`data-picture`: skipped by the census, masked in the captures) until S10 (D15 rule 3); NO PICTURES and PICTURE MISSING come with S10's route. The aids' switches are the page's own, off at every start (the contract holds none); the guides, the 1:1 view and the loupe are geometry and crops of the test picture, and zebras and peaking are worked out from it.
- NOT SET UP, which board 2 does not draw, is drawn with the model's sentence and a way to Setup's camera part, and `REC` is locked while CAM 1 is not paired. The other pages' fixtures stay unseeded, so their headers show the Cameras lamp amber, "not set up", as a fresh workstation does — and as the live build will until Slices 11 and 13 bring the links (Slice 8's first step 3).
- Setup's camera part is a section of Setup / Support, as S6a's Prompter XL row is, not a sixth runner step: the runner's steps are fixed in the contract (`app_state.rs`), and with (c) the live build could never finish a camera step.
- The (c) refusal refuses a non-empty address only; taking an address away, the vMix input and Forget stay allowed. An archive restore in a build without a network link skips the network cameras' addresses and says so, as `cameras/archive.rs` promises never to write what Setup would refuse; a whole-database restore brings back what it holds.
- The page reads the cameras again once a second while it is open (no watch between requests until Slices 11 and 13); CAM 1's timecode is shown as last read, never advanced on the page's own clock.
- `REC` is a red lamp and the word, never a red fill (D19; system-a §4's red list gains recording), on the page and in the header's chip; an UNREACHABLE CAM 1 whose last report was recording reads amber, "last known", as board 2 draws it; the chip opens the Cameras page. The header's worst case — Scene drift, Solo, Prompter playing and `REC` together — is measured at 2560.
- No Release on an unreachable camera, as board 2 draws it, though the hardware link accepts one; Slice 11 may bring it back (the Bluetooth link shared with the iPad).
- Words the contract cannot back go: "started before 09:05" (the start's time is not known) and "released at 09:06" (no release time is kept).
- The Playwright hook is the fixture double's alone, a window hook like the crash-disarm hook; the double's hooks read the cameras again at once, a watch the hardware link lacks until Slices 11 and 13.

### Slice 10 — Cameras: the pictures

Status: planned.

D17: the three camera inputs vMix publishes over NDI on this PC reach the page, which shows them (the vMix input Setup holds for each camera, `setup.vmixInput`, Slice 8); without vMix, or with its NDI option off, the page says so and the controls still work. The aids are computed on this PC from the picture and drawn on this screen only, never sent to a camera or to vMix. Setup / Support carries NDI's attribution (a link to ndi.video and the trademark line). Tests get a still test picture (D15 rule 3). Walk items 4 and 5 check it, the second while vMix records.

First step, before any code, recorded under this heading with its boundary impact (AGENTS.md): the route the frames take to the page at 25 a second without the page or vMix dropping one. The hardware link's only way to the page is its line-by-line pipe, and the page may connect only to the shell (`tauri.conf.json`'s policy: `connect-src ipc:`; images also `blob:` and `data:`). The routes: (a) the hardware link serves JPEG frames on 127.0.0.1 behind the bridge's token, with the policy widened for that address; (b) the hardware link hands the frames to the shell over a second channel, and the shell passes them to the page as raw data, which a canvas and `blob:` already allow; (c) the shell receives NDI itself, which puts device I/O outside the hardware link and needs an architecture decision. The same step decides where the SDK's calls live (Common), proves that finding the three sources stays on this PC — NDI finds sources by mDNS on every network adapter unless told otherwise (D12) — and measures the processor's load of three pictures decoded while vMix records. The NDI runtime ships in the app's folder: packaging copies two executables today (`scripts/native-package.mjs`, read, never run from a session), so shipping it is packaging, installer, SBOM and checksum work, done on the workstation with the operator's go-ahead under the keep-and-restore rule. Boards: Setup / Support (the attribution); finished on the workstation.

### Slice 11 — Cameras: the Pocket 6K Pro over Bluetooth

Status: planned.

D18: Blackmagic's published Bluetooth protocol (service `291D567A-6D75-11E6-8B77-86F30CA893D3`) behind the hardware link for CAM 1 — the values of D10 it carries (focus and one-shot autofocus, iris, shutter, ISO, ND, white balance and tint, dynamic range, the display LUT, video mode, codec, record), with battery and card time "not reported". Pairing happens once, in Setup, with the operator present (the camera shows the PIN; btleplug does not pair on Windows by itself, so the slice uses Windows' own pairing, on the one `windows` crate). Release hands CAM 1 to the iPad and Connect takes it back (D13). The first step confirms what C0 left open: whether the service carries timecode and reports focus as a lens position, and whether the display LUT reaches the HDMI output vMix gets. The drift guards: no Bluetooth call is reachable from a test, and nothing ever writes the camera's status characteristic (a write there can switch the camera off). Tests, lanes and scratch runs use the simulated camera (D15 rule 2). Boards: none expected. From Slice 8: the real `cameras.setup.pair`, the end of the "no link yet" state for CAM 1, and the watch between requests (Slice 8's settled item 1; Appendix B item 7, "within about a second"), which reads the camera off the request loop so an unanswering camera never holds a request; and whether taking CAM 1 back at a start disturbs the iPad (Slice 8's settled item 11) goes to the operator with the first step. Gates from Slice 8's review: the Bluetooth guard mirrors Slice 8's address guard — the one function that opens the adapter calls it first, and with CAM 1 paired and no simulated cameras a test build reads the guard's sentence as CAM 1's `UNREACHABLE` sentence —; a step from a value the camera reports off its model's list moves from the nearest listed option in the step's direction and is refused when the value cannot be read, never from the list's first option as Slice 8's code does (its simulated cameras hold only listed values), with a Rust test through `body_sets`; and the `paired` a `.sqlite3` database backup brings back is held to Windows' own pairing.

### Slice 12 — The CAMERAS deck page

Status: planned.

**Rescope:** (2026-09-27, Part C's re-cut) built with Slice 7 as one slice, in the cloud, after Slice 9 (Slice 7's **Rescope:**).

D14: `CAM 1`–`CAM 3` choose the camera the dials drive (amber = selected, the same selection as the page's); `BANK` switches the dials between exposure (ISO · shutter · iris · ND), colour (white balance · tint) and focus (focus; a push is one-shot autofocus where the lens allows); `REC` always acts on CAM 1 — one press starts, a second within 3 s while the key reads `STOP?` stops — and while CAM 1 records it shows `REC` with a red lamp on a dark key, never a red fill, as the screen draws it (D19); the touch strip shows the selected camera's values; resolution, frame rate, profile and LUT stay on the screen. The page's word on the deck is the one the hardware link accepts, `cameras`. From Slice 9's first steps: the Cameras page's "what the dials set" section (board 2's bank keys, the dials' hints and the footer's dials) and a home for the bank that the page and the deck both read — Slice 9 leaves them out, as the hardware link has no bank yet; and the camera rows of Recent actions from the deck, with Stream Deck as their source (Appendix B item 10). The bridge's actions and feedback, and the Companion export; the profile at the close-out (D5). Moved ahead of the BGH1s' link at the review of 2026-09-26, so D18's checks hold up nothing else. Boards: Setup's deck steps, which draw every page the deck has (the new page's keys and dials); built with the other deck page on the workstation, its win32 captures and contrast made in-slice (Part C's second **Rescope:**; it was to be built in the cloud, its captures at the catch-up).

### Slice 13 — Cameras: the BGH1s over the network

Status: planned — gated: it starts once the operator has read the LUMIX SDK's licence and checked on one BGH1 whether it goes back to LUMIX Tether without a settings reset (D18, rule 5), both recorded here.

D18: Panasonic's LUMIX SDK (a C++ library for 64-bit Windows, in beta) behind the hardware link for CAM 2 and CAM 3, over Ethernet, at the addresses Setup holds and no other (D12). The first step, before any code, settles what the SDK does on the wire: whether it connects to a given address without searching the network (D12 forbids scanning) and to 127.0.0.1 at a given port, which a fake BGH1 needs (D15 rule 1). If it can only find cameras by searching, D18 goes back to the operator. If it cannot reach a local port, the tests stop at a camera interface backed by Slice 8's simulated camera, the refusal of addresses off this PC sits in front of the SDK's calls, and rule 1's wording goes back to the operator. The same step confirms what the SDK reports back rather than only accepts — the exposure and white-balance modes, the resolutions and frame rates it lists, a focus position — and the page shows only those (D10). What Release can promise follows the check (D13). If the licence rules the SDK out, D18 goes back to the operator before any code (rule 6 is the fallback). Boards: none expected. From Slice 8: the end of the "no link yet" state for CAM 2 and CAM 3, and the watch between requests (Slice 8's settled item 1; Appendix B item 7), off the request loop; the network link calls Slice 8's drift guard before every connection; and whether taking a BGH1 back at a start disturbs LUMIX Tether (Slice 8's settled item 11) goes to the operator with D18's check. A gate from Slice 8's review: a step from a value the camera reports off its model's list (a faster shutter, an extended ISO) moves from the nearest listed option in the step's direction and is refused when the value cannot be read, never from the list's first option as Slice 8's code does, with a Rust test through `body_sets`.

## Found, fixed

**2026-09-25 — the flash test waits for its selection** (branch `fix/lighting-flash-test-waits-for-selection`, cut from `main` at `e8d43c5`; a task the operator started from the chip raised while S2 was checked). `frontend/app/tests/lighting-rig-actions.spec.ts` "The page shows each flash start and end by itself: Identify, then Find" failed now and then on the workstation, before this program too (3 of 5 runs at `359ee4a`, 4 of 5 at `e8d43c5`, 2 of 20 and 4 of 30 in repeated runs), while CI passed it on every run. The failing line was 149 — the check after **Find**, which has the same words as the one after Identify. Five failing traces showed the same thing at the Find click: Key at 100 percent and Back off, so Find had run over the selection as it was before Shift+Enter added Back. The cause is the test: its `selectFixture` pressed Enter or Shift+Enter and returned at once, and the Find click followed about 2 ms later; the selection reaches the screen a render after the key (the store asks the fixture double, the reply comes back, the page draws it), and on a fast, idle page the browser handles the click first. Anything that slows Playwright or the machine hides it — a trace, one more round trip, load, even the page's CPU slowed sixfold (the click then waits behind the pending render) — which is why CI and loaded runs passed. The product is right: a person cannot click within a render of the key.

Fix: `selectFixture` waits for the marker to say it is selected (`aria-pressed` `true`); every one of its eleven calls selects a light. Under the same conditions the case with the old helper failed 4 of 30 runs and with the new one passed 30 of 30; with the page's CPU slowed sixfold (CDP `Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate` 6) 10 of 10; the whole file three times, 30 of 30. Landed by #212, squash `1ed8d86`, on the operator's go-ahead of 2026-09-25 ("Both, flash fix first"); its runs, push 36138804879 and the pull request's own 36145074794, were all ten jobs green on the first attempt.

## Baseline refresh procedure

Since Slice SW (D22, 2026-09-26) the captures are the win32 ones at 2560×1440 and nothing else. They are refreshed on the workstation with `--update-snapshots=changed`, every changed PNG inspected before `git add`, and checked only there: CI's Linux runner skips every screenshot expectation and the contrast sampling, so the local Playwright lane runs before every workstation push. Cloud slices (D23) push without it: their captures and contrast are refreshed at the workstation catch-up (Part C), and main's win32 captures stay stale until then. A board that goes loses its capture. Build first: `npm run build --workspace frontend/app && npm run frontend:storybook:build`. Never Playwright and a qualification lane at the same time. (Before Slice SW, under the operator's rules of 2026-09-22 and 2026-09-23, a change that moved a board also refreshed its linux capture from the branch push run's `playwright-test-results` artifact, and the captures at other sizes and on macOS kept their baselines.)
